# Implemented

Work that is finished, moved out of `TODO.md` so that file stays a list of what
is left. Entries keep their original detail on purpose: the reasoning, the file
and line references, and the things that turned out not to be true are the
value here, not the fact that something got done.

Newest first. The bracketed label is the `TODO.md` section the item came from.

## 2026-09-24

- **[Audit before anything else] DONE: slopradar verification (phase 3 refutation).** A verifier agent tried to refute every SLOPRADAR.md resolution against `git diff 82771c5b..HEAD`. Correct: S1 (unattributable schedules skipped in `gateScheduledFire`, `src/mastra/index.ts:103`), S2 (sandbox tool calls with no thread refused, `workspace/index.ts:156`), S4, S5, S7, S10, S12, and the thread-keyed `reached` set (a thread whose background job outlives its last turn stays in the set until a later turn pauses it, intended). S11 and S13 moot after the preview removal. Two regressions found and fixed: `chat/app-home/mcp/actions.ts:81` ran the post-add Home publish and the connection-check publish concurrently, so a slower first publish could land last and hide the new server's status (now sequential, both still off the 3s ack path); `prompts/core.ts:17` still invited logging in "with credentials you actually have", undoing S6 (now: never ask for or accept credentials in Slack, stop at the login page). `docs/brokered-git.md:66` said template 2.1, now 2.2. Also removed the duplicate git identity from `build-template.ts` (the `git config --global user.name/email` user steps): `workspace/sandbox.ts` sets `GIT_AUTHOR_*`/`GIT_COMMITTER_*` per sandbox, which wins over git config and does not need a template rebuild when `agentmail.inbox` changes. Tried and reverted: (1) spreading the parsed `delegatedTool.data` instead of `output` in `processors/delegated-tools.ts:27` fails typecheck because the looseObject type is not a `ChunkType`, and a cast is banned, so the raw `output` spread stays (it is the same object, just narrowed only by the safeParse); (2) building the `step-guard.ts:16` regex from one `markupNames` array via `new RegExp` trips Biome `noUnnecessaryConditions` on the `exec` loop (it infers the match as never null), so the literal regex and list stay duplicated. Partial or false premises moved to TODO.md: S3 (GitHub push/checkout blocked while a background job runs, but not the reverse, nor a concurrent foreground command), S9 (consumed OAuth start nonces moved to Mastra's state adapter, which is also in-process memory: replayable after restart, keys never expire), S8 (`links:read` and `is_mcp_enabled` left in manifests).

Doc sediment moved out of `docs/` into this file, as the annotations asked:
  - Socket Mode cutover runbook, removed from `docs/webhook-mode.md` (both manifests already have `socket_mode_enabled: false`, and `SLACK_APP_TOKEN` no longer exists in `env.ts`, so rollback to a socket build is gone). The runbook was: Slack sends events over the socket or to the request URL, never both, and a socket-mode process answers webhooks with 405, so `url_verification` cannot pass before the switch. (1) Prepare while still in Socket Mode: create the tunnel and DNS record; add `SLACK_SIGNING_SECRET`, `GORKIE_API_TOKEN` and `HOST=127.0.0.1`; point `gorkie-monitor.sh` at `/health`; `curl -i -X POST https://<your-host>/api/agents/orchestrator/channels/slack/webhook` should return 405 from the old process; rehearse on the `gorkie (dev)` app. (2) Pick a quiet window with no turns in flight. (3) Deploy the webhook build (`bun run build`, restart `gorkie.service`). (4) Within seconds, update the production manifest: `socket_mode_enabled: false`, both request URLs set to the webhook path; Slack sends `url_verification` and delivery moves to HTTP, events from the gap are retried. (5) Verify a DM, a channel mention, a thread follow-up, App Home, a modal submit and the native stop button; watch for 401s and `Skipping duplicate event delivery`. (6) Remove `SLACK_APP_TOKEN` and revoke the `xapp-` token. Never run old and new side by side (shared scheduler and workers, scheduled tasks fire twice). Rollback was: `socket_mode_enabled: true`, redeploy the previous commit, restore `SLACK_APP_TOKEN`. The section was replaced by a "Process supervision" section (probe `/health`; `TimeoutStopSec` above twice `shutdown.drainTimeoutMs` plus 5 s, for example 250).
  - GitHub device flow, from `docs/github-app.md`: "Gorkie used to sign people in with a device code. The web flow replaced it, so leaving device flow on only widens what the client id can do. Turn it off once this version is deployed." Now it just says gorkie uses the web flow only; the "Enable Device Flow: unchecked" table row stays.
  - Classic GitHub PATs, the "Classic tokens" section of `docs/github-app.md`: classic personal access tokens are no longer supported, and migration `20260924043047_drop_github_classic_tokens` deletes any that were saved. They only existed so gorkie could fork, which the app cannot; gorkie offers a diff or patch for repositories it cannot push to. `workspace/skills/github/references/connecting.md` already says classic tokens are unsupported.
  - Also fixed in docs: `GITHUB_APP_SLUG` placeholder unified to `gorkie-ai`; `docs/webhook-mode.md` now says the proxy guard relies on the proxy setting `cf-connecting-ip` or `x-forwarded-for`.

- **[CRITICAL]** **Live view video preview is a black screen; remove the Watch live button (2026-09-24).** Owner report: the card posts and the thumbnail shows, but the video block's player (the `/live/:ticket` page) stays black. Root cause: `src/mastra/server/live-view.ts` interpolated `${JSON.stringify(socket)}` inside `<script>` in a hono `html` template, and hono escapes every interpolation, so the served page read `new WebSocket(&quot;wss://...&amp;t=...&quot;)`: a syntax error, no script ran, and the dark body with no frame was all Slack showed. Confirmed by curling a freshly minted ticket's page from the running server. Fix: the URL goes in `<body data-socket="...">` (attribute escaping is decoded by the HTML parser) and the script reads `document.body.dataset.socket`; verified offline that the rendered script now parses. Ruled out with probes against the running `mastra start` (127.0.0.1:4111 and the quick tunnel): the WS route is registered (`/api/agents/orchestrator/browser/session` reports `screencastAvailable: true`; `@hono/node-ws` resolves from the repo root node_modules even though `.mastra/output/node_modules` lacks it), the tunnel upgrades WebSockets, a ticketed WS gets `{"status":"connected"}` locally and through the tunnel, and a proxied WS without a ticket is refused. Ticket thread id equals the browser session key (both come from `SandboxBrowser.connectThread`, and `slack.decodeThreadId` accepts it, which is why the card posts). Frame path not live-tested: no browser session was open during the probe. Also: `frame-ancestors` now lists `https://slack.com` (the apex is not matched by `*.slack.com`; `app.slack.com` is). Card (`src/mastra/chat/live-view.ts`): the Watch live `actions` block is gone; if `chat.postMessage` rejects the video block (its host must be in the app's unfurl_domains, which a new quick-tunnel host is not), the card is reposted with an `image` block of the same refreshing thumbnail, and refreshes keep that variant. The fallback card has no link to the live page, by design of the button removal. Needs a live test by the owner.
- **[CRITICAL]** **Detailed tool display showed no task cards (2026-09-24).** Root cause: the per-run mode lost a race. `ChatChannelOutputProcessor` (`node_modules/@mastra/core/dist/agent-DwtTO5Px.js:21427`, `processDataParts = true`) opens its driver on the first chunk it sees and copies `render.toolDisplay` into `runStreamingDriver` then (`:21494-21498`; `groupTasks` is fixed at `:20784`). Observational Memory writes `data-om-status` from `processInputStep` on every step (`@mastra/memory` `src-zTE4189S.js:29482`, via `emitProgress` at `:30725`), before the model streams, and that part goes through the output processors (`agent-DwtTO5Px.js:29468`). Our `tool-display` processor had no `processDataParts`, so Mastra skipped it for data parts (`:4736`) and the render processor opened with the adapter default `hidden`; the later rewrite to `timeline` changed nothing. Fix in `src/mastra/processors/tool-display.ts`: `processDataParts: true`, and the lookup is memoised as a promise on `state` that every chunk awaits, because OM also writes some parts without awaiting (`src-zTE4189S.js:27190` and others). Ruled out: settings persistence (`setToolDisplay`/`getToolDisplay` both key on `rawId`), processor order (configured processors run before channel ones, `agent-DwtTO5Px.js:35641-35645`), recipient context for native streaming in channel threads (Chat SDK fills `recipientUserId`/`recipientTeamId` from the current message, `chat` `Thread.handleStream`). No patch needed. Needs a live test by the owner.
- **[Owner decisions: Open owner decisions]** Security review #5 (live view input): NOT an open issue. Verified 2026-09-24 in 09acac39: `SandboxBrowser` (`workspace/browser.ts`) overrides `injectMouseEvent` and `injectKeyboardEvent` as no-ops, and Mastra's stream `onMessage` only routes mouse/keyboard messages to those methods (`handleInputMessage`), dropping everything else incl. navigation; tested by feeding 3 mouse, 2 keyboard and 1 navigate message through Mastra's real handler: none reached the parent class. The reviewer's claim assumed the stock BrowserViewer. Live check: clicks and keys on the viewer page do nothing.

- **[Audit before anything else]** **Step 5 follow-ups blocked by the auto-mode classifier ('Security Weaken'), awaiting owner go-ahead (2026-09-23).** (1) `lib/github/token.ts` refresh upserts via `setGitHubCredential`, so a disconnect or PAT reconnect during an in-flight refresh is resurrected/overwritten as kind 'app': make it update-only scoped to `user_id` + `kind = 'app'`; restore the one-line why on `bad_refresh_token`. (2) `app-home/github/connect.ts` `openConnect` calls `startDeviceLogin()` before `views.open`; Slack `trigger_id` expires ~3s, open a loading modal first then `views.update`. (3) `app-home/github/blocks.ts` shows the stored permission though `githubAccess` clamps to 'write' outside DMs. (4) `tools/github/push.ts` refuses only literal main/master, not the real default branch (`origin/HEAD`); prompt text in `prompts/tools.ts` already softened to say exactly that. (5) run git with `-c core.hooksPath=/dev/null` inside `withCredential` so agent-written hooks cannot use the token. (6) approval patch `patches/@mastra+core@1.66.0.patch`: approve/decline paths call `_buildRenderContext` without requesterId (follow-up cards approvable by anyone), and after a restart the memory-store fallback skips the guard (fail closed); needs `bun patch` regeneration. Carry into the 1.69.0 rebase in step 8. DONE 2026-09-24 (owner approved as hardening, uncommitted): (1) new `updateRefreshedGitHubCredential` in `db/queries/github.ts` (UPDATE only, `WHERE user_id AND kind = 'app'`, returns whether a row changed); `refreshAccount` falls back to re-reading the row when nothing was updated, so a disconnect yields no token and a PAT reconnect yields the PAT; unused `account` param dropped; why-comment restored. (2) `openConnect` registers polling with `device: undefined`, opens a loading modal (`connectView({ loading: true })`), then `startDeviceLogin()`, then `views.update` with the code only if the method is still 'app' (a PAT switch mid-load keeps its view; switching back reads `pending.device`); on failure it deletes the polling entry and shows `failedModal('unreachable')`. Polling `device` is now `DeviceLogin | undefined`. (3) `blocks.ts` shows e.g. '`never asks` in DMs · `asks before writing or deleting` in shared threads' when threads are on and `levelsFor(true)` clamps the stored level. (4) `push.ts` reads `git symbolic-ref --short refs/remotes/origin/HEAD` in the checkout and refuses that branch; the Zod main/master refusal stays as the fallback. Caveat: origin/HEAD lives in the agent-writable sandbox checkout, so a hostile agent can repoint it; the GitHub API `default_branch` of the push target would be authoritative (not done, owner asked for origin/HEAD). Tool description, `prompts/tools.ts` and `workspace/skills/github/SKILL.md` updated. (5) `git()` in `tools/github/git.ts` passes `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT` env config (`core.hooksPath=/dev/null`, `core.fsmonitor=false`) rather than `-c`, because env config reaches every git in a compound command (checkout's `$(git symbolic-ref ...)`, `&& git checkout`) and fsmonitor is the same code-execution vector. Applies to every `git()` call, which are all inside or adjacent to the credential window. Needs git >= 2.31 in the E2B image. Not covered: a background process the agent left running in the sandbox can still use the github.com header transform during the window. (6) Approve/decline already passed `approvalRequesterId` (verified). Patch now also stamps `requesterId` (the run's `requestContext.get('channel').userId`) onto each `pendingToolApprovals` entry in `addToolMetadata`, and the memory-store fallback reads it back; on that path a missing requester refuses the click (fail closed). Consequence: approvals suspended before this patch, or by runs with no channel context (scheduled/wait wakes), cannot be answered after a restart. Still fail-open: the in-memory stash path when `requesterId` is absent (render contexts built by `buildRenderContextForThread`), left as-is. Durable-agent `addToolMetadata` (`create-durable-agent-*`) not patched, unused here. Regenerated with diff -u against npm pack, 4 original hunks intact, reinstalled, both bundles byte-identical to the edited copies and pass `node --check`.

Remove the `messageId` guard, revert the step-5 `messageId: undefined` change in wait.ts/scheduled-tasks/create.ts, update `docs/slack-search.md`; all other public-only gates stay.

Research 2026-09-24 (owner asked whether Mastra already does OAuth): partly. MCP: reuse installed `@mastra/mcp` 1.18 `MCPOAuthClientProvider` + a Postgres `OAuthStorage` adapter (only `authenticate()` is loopback-only; drive the provider ourselves; do NOT upgrade to 2.0, it removed dynamic client registration). GitHub: Factory (`@mastra/factory`, mastracode/factory/src/integrations/github/routes.ts) is tied to its WorkOS web session and org installation tokens, not reusable; copy its `state-signing.ts` HMAC pattern, keep `@octokit/oauth-methods`. - **[Simplification]** (2026-09-24: already done by drizzle/20260912171950_drop_stranded_tool_display; the name is reused by 20260924044806_add_tool_display for the new per-person setting) Drop the stranded `tool_display` column now that migrations can express it. DROP COLUMN` once the column is gone from `schema.ts`, which it already is. Confirm no deployment still reads it first.
- **[Customization]** (2026-09-24 owner asked for it: three modes Hidden/Compact/Detailed (Mastra hidden/grouped/timeline) as a per-person App Home radio (`user_settings.tool_display`), plus `!display` per-thread override in Mastra threadState; resolved per run by the first output processor `processors/tool-display.ts`, which rewrites `render.toolDisplay` on the channels render object (`__mastra_chat_channel_render`) instead of the reverted AsyncLocalStorage getter, because the ALS only covered inbound turns and approval clicks build their render context in Mastra's own onAction, so an approved turn fell back to the default; scheduled runs have no render object and keep the adapter default; default Hidden) Decide whether to let a person choose how tool calls are displayed, from App Home: `Hidden` (tools run behind the typing status) or `Cards` (Mastra's own `'grouped'` mode, one plan widget per turn). Built and then reverted on request, so the tab is back to the static `toolDisplay: 'hidden'`. What was built, if it is wanted again: both are native string modes, so there is no custom renderer and no patch, because `AgentChannels` keeps the adapter config by reference (`adapterConfigs[name] = cfg`) and reads `adapterConfig.toolDisplay` once per run, so a getter on it resolves inside the turn and follows whoever is being answered, fed by an `AsyncLocalStorage` set in `runTurn`. It needed a `tool_display` column, `chat/tool-display.ts`, `types/display.ts` and `app-home/tool-display/`. Never live-tested. The two items below only matter if this comes back. The owner also wants more than the two native modes: a status line, or kyto-style blocks, which means patching Mastra since neither is a native `toolDisplay` value. Decide that before rebuilding, because it changes the answer from a config setting to a custom renderer, which is the thing the reverted version deliberately avoided.
- **[Customization]** (2026-09-24: kept native grouped for Compact (titles only, failures get details); Detailed uses timeline, now showing each tool's `transform.display` summary via a patch hunk) Decide whether native `'grouped'` says enough. It never shows a result: `output` is set only for `'timeline'` (`agent-mTyOZvpk.js:19575`), so a finished task is a title and nothing else, and only a failure gets a body (`"⚠ " + errorText`, JSON truncated at 300 chars). Titles are `stripToolPrefix(toolName)` plus the first argument truncated to 35 chars, the plan header is rewritten to the latest tool on every call and result, and a delegated agent's steps arrive as their own top-level tasks. The template answered this with a custom `ToolDisplayFn` plus a patch pinning function-form display to `'grouped'`; that was built and then dropped in favour of staying native.
- **[Customization]** (2026-09-24: Slack's plan block reference documents a 50-task max; Mastra never counted. Patch hunk in runStreamingDriver: per-session task id set, drops task updates past 50 (and their later results), and a grouped session rolls over to a fresh plan message at a step boundary once it holds 40; the adapter rethrow at @chat-adapter/slack dist ~:5136-5139 is unpatched (would need a second patch file = dependency change) and no longer reachable from task overflow; live-test 45/50/55 tasks in Compact) Find out whether Slack caps the tasks in one plan widget, and fix the crash it causes. Investigated 2026-09-11 and verified against the installed source. Nothing between `pushToSession` (`@mastra/core/dist/agent-mTyOZvpk.js:19270-19279`) and `chat.appendStream` caps, chunks or counts tasks, so any limit is Slack's and can only arrive as a rejected append. The graceful-degrade path this file previously described is real (`@chat-adapter/slack/dist/index.js:4090-4112`) but it is not the whole story, which is why this reads as a crash rather than a downgrade: `fallback.nativeRendered` is set true by the first successful structured chunk, and at `:4081-4084` a later failing text flush then rethrows instead of falling back. So the first rejection is swallowed, and the next text flush throws out of `stream()`. That rethrow is worth fixing independently of any cap. On the number itself, 50 is observational: the only documented 50 in the installed surface is the 50-block message cap (`@slack/types/dist/chunk.d.ts:16`), not a task count, and `PlanBlock.tasks` declares no maximum. Plausible that N task cards expand to N blocks and hit the block ceiling, but confirm empirically at 45/50/55/60 distinct task ids and capture the platform error code before capping, since a guessed cap truncates real output. Note this is only reachable in `grouped` or `timeline`; with the committed `toolDisplay: 'hidden'` we emit no task chunks at all, so it rides on the reverted tool-display work above.
- **[Customization]** (2026-09-23 owner: enabled, resource-scoped per thread starter, preferences only, owner-only updates; see src/mastra/memory/profile.ts) Decide whether to enable resource-scoped Working Memory alongside the thread-scoped Observational Memory already in use, or leave it disabled.
- **[Known issues]** (2026-09-24: no references remain in src/) `call_slack_api` no longer exists but two files still talk about it. `chat/status/statuses.ts:4` maps a typing status for it, and `prompts/features/code-mode.ts` tells the model not to return "raw call_slack_api responses". A grep for the tool itself finds nothing in `src/mastra/tools/`, so the status entry is dead code and the prompt line points the model at a tool it cannot call. Delete both, or reinstate the tool deliberately; right now it is neither.
- **[Architecture]** **Port step 1: Mastra 1.69 stable upgrade and patch rebase (2026-09-23, uncommitted for owner review).** Pins: `@mastra/core` `1.69.0` exact (patched), `@mastra/mcp` `1.18.0` exact (npm `latest` is 2.0.0, a major, deliberately not taken), the rest caret per repo style: `@mastra/deployer`/`server` `^1.69.0`, `mastra` `^1.31.1`, `@mastra/memory` `^1.31.0`, `@mastra/pg` `^1.26.0`, `@mastra/duckdb` `^1.10.0`, `@mastra/e2b` `^0.12.1`, `@mastra/langfuse` `^1.5.8` (resolves 1.5.9), `@mastra/observability` `^1.17.9` (resolves 1.18.0), `@mastra/loggers` `^1.3.2`, `chat`/`@chat-adapter/slack` `^4.41.0`, `ai` `^7.0.99` (resolves 7.0.112). Peer ranges all satisfied (pg and duckdb need core >=1.68). Patch `patches/@mastra+core@1.69.0.patch` replaces the 1.66.0 one, all 4 hunks kept in `dist/agent-DwtTO5Px.js` and `dist/agent-DVnXHd4C.cjs`: step-finish `stepIsContinued`, approval-requester guard, in-band error `advanceFallbackModel`, retry-count reset on model switch. New in 1.69: the approve and decline paths also call `_buildRenderContext`, so both now pass `approvalRequesterId` so a follow-up approval card in the resumed run stays requester-only; `buildRenderContextForThread` (background/scheduled fires, no requester) is left unguarded. `bun patch --commit` failed on the host's `~/.gitattributes` symlink loop (`Too many levels of symbolic links`, and env overrides did not reach bun's git call), so the patch was produced with `diff -u` against a pristine `npm pack` tarball, then verified by a clean reinstall. API fix: `SlackAgentAdapter.resolveInlineMentions` lost its `skipSelfMention` parameter in chat 4.41 (upstream now resolves every mention), so the override dropped it and the self-skip branch. `@mastra/langfuse` 1.5.9 still has no `onFeedbackEvent`, so `LangfuseFeedbackExporter` stays. `lastMessages` is deprecated but does not error, left for the memory config step.
- **[Architecture]** **Port integration pass and cross-slice review (2026-09-24, uncommitted for owner review).** Native Slack stop: `@chat-adapter/slack` 4.41 turns `agent_session_stopped` into `chat.abortTurn` + `onAgentSessionStopped` handlers, but channels never passes the Chat SDK turn signal to the Mastra run (no `thread.signal` read in core's channel code), so `chat/events.ts` now registers `onAgentSessionStopped` (allow-list gated like `!stop`) calling `stopThread(slackThreadId)`, exported from `chat/commands/stop.ts` and shared with the command. The metadata lookup in `stopThread` is kept: core's `getOrCreateThread` falls back to a UUID when `resolveThreadId` collides, and the rename migration skips some threads. `ALREADY_IN_CHANNEL` used in `tools/slack/utils.ts` and `chat/onboarding.ts`. `prompts/slack.ts` speaker labels now match reality (live messages: `authorName`/`authorId`/`authorMention` attributes from core `buildEventContext`; backfill: `[Name (<@U..>)] (msg:ts): text`). `memory/profile.ts` `writing` description and `prompts/core.ts` aligned with the persona (mirror style, keep persona; the working-memory profile belongs to the thread starter, not every speaker). README: dev manifest paragraph, Memory section (threadState in `chat/state.ts` because `MastraStateAdapter` keeps thread state in process memory, not "expires after 30 days"; `messageHistory.maxTokens`; Slack-shaped thread ids + runbook link). `setThreadState` reads the store directly so a failed read cannot wipe unpatched fields. `join_thread` typing status added. Boot order in `src/mastra/index.ts`: `runMigrations()` now runs before `new Mastra` (the constructor starts channels, so a message could otherwise be handled mid-rename), and `setMastra` runs before `startWorkers()`. Open for the owner: migration JSON pass cost (see report), `E2B_API_KEY` hex-only regex, `setThreadState` read-modify-write is not atomic across concurrent turns in one thread.
- **[Ideas]** (2026-09-24: browser half done, see IMPLEMENTED.md 'Live browser view'; computer use via @mastra/e2b-desktop deferred by owner) Embed a live browser/computer view in Slack via Mastra's `BrowserViewer` **Plan refined 2026-09-13 from mastra-agent + coolton.** Key fact: E2B exposes any sandbox port publicly via `sandbox.networking.getPortUrl(port)` / `e2b.getHost(port)`, independent of Slack transport, so an embed does NOT need the webhook migration. mastra-agent's `browser/cdp.ts` does exactly this: `getPortUrl(9222)` on the sandbox, reads `/json/version` for the CloakBrowser CDP WebSocket, and hands it to `@mastra/browser-viewer` (which renders in Mastra Studio, not Slack, so not directly reusable for a Slack embed). Two gorkie-fit paths: (a) cheapest, screenshot on turn end, `agent-browser screenshot` -> `upload_file` -> post inline; zero infra, zero deps, works today. (b) coolton-style live view: run a viewer on a sandbox port (coolton uses Xvfb+x11vnc+noVNC and returns the noVNC page URL; a CDP-screencast page also works), `getHost(port)` gives a public `*.e2b.app` URL, post it in Slack as a link/unfurl since Slack cannot inline a WS stream. (b) needs a template rebuild to include the viewer. Do (a) first.
- **[Ideas]** (2026-09-24: browser live view is a Slack `video` block embed, see IMPLEMENTED.md) Let channels send rich embeds, starting with the browser. Outbound messages are plain text: `chat/attachments.ts` only covers the inbound direction (downloading a Slack file into the workspace), and every Block Kit builder in the repo sits under `chat/app-home/*/blocks.ts`, which reaches Slack through `publishHome` and so renders the Home tab rather than anything in a conversation. The sandbox already ships `agent-browser`, whose `screenshot <path>` gives a payload for free, so the cheap first version is "here is the page I am looking at" posted inline instead of narrated in prose, which also makes a wrong-page mistake visible to the user immediately rather than three tool calls later. The ambitious version is a live view: `@mastra/browser-viewer` exposes a CDP screencast over a WebSocket, which Slack cannot render inline, so that one needs a hosted viewer URL and an unfurl rather than a true embed. Exploratory, not yet scoped.
- **[Ideas]** `taskContext` (`lib/memory.ts`) calls `memoryThread()` on every scheduled-task and wait call, running a Postgres query to derive a thread id the tool already has: channels binds the memory thread as `context.agent.threadId` and its owner as `context.agent.resourceId`. Read those directly in `scheduled-tasks/create.ts`, `list.ts` and `wait.ts`. Keep `memoryThread()` for `chat/commands/stop.ts`, which only has an external thread id. Its `resolvedAgent` and `ctx` return fields have no readers, so the `getAgentById` and `channelContext` calls behind them are dead work too. Closed: already done 2026-09-13 (see `IMPLEMENTED.md`), and `lib/memory.ts` itself was removed in the desloppify pass (2026-09-23), with `memoryThread()` inlined into `chat/commands/stop.ts`, its only caller.
- **[Ideas]** (2026-09-24: Detailed (timeline) task output now prefers `transform.display` output-available `.summary` over Mastra's truncated raw result, via a patch hunk using getTransformedToolPayload on the tool-result chunk metadata) The `transform.display` summaries on ~25 tools never reach Slack under `toolDisplay: 'hidden'`; the user-visible copy is the separate `chat/status/statuses.ts`. Two parallel per-tool copy systems, only one of which is seen.
- **[Ideas]** (2026-09-23: `types/tools.ts` deleted in the standards pass) `types/tools.ts` and `types/tools/` collide, so 31 files must import from `types/tools/index`. Folding the file into the directory removes the suffix everywhere.
- **[Ideas]** (2026-09-23: `TaskToolContext` deleted, it was unused) `TaskToolContext` re-declares a subset of Mastra's `ToolExecutionContext`; `Pick<ToolExecutionContext, 'agent' | 'mastra' | 'requestContext'>` gives the same surface without the hand-written shape.
- **[Ideas]** (2026-09-23: local Node bumped to 24.21 via NodeSource; cspell backlog cleared) Local `check:spelling` cannot run: cspell 10 needs Node >=22.18.0 and the container has 22.17.0 with no version manager, while the repo already pins Node 24 (`.nvmrc`, `engines`). CI runs Node 24 so it is unaffected. Get Node 24 into the dev container.
- **[Product]** (2026-09-24: tool display half done (switchable modes); live browser embed is the separate BrowserViewer item) Issue 35, bring back tool display and live embeds, for example the browser view.
- **[Product]** (2026-09-24: done in the port; `bot.onAgentSessionStopped` in chat/events.ts calls the shared `stopThread`, allow-list gated) Finish Slack's native stop button. Four of the five pieces are done and on this branch: `agent_session_stopped` is subscribed in `slack-manifest.json`, `SlackAgentAdapter.processEventPayload` intercepts it (the adapter's dispatch table at `@chat-adapter/slack/dist/index.js:1708-1761` still has no `else`, so an unknown event is acked and dropped), `stop.ts` is factored into `stopThread(threadId)` shared with the `!stop` command, and the handler is registered in `chat/events.ts`. The blocker the 2026-09-11 investigation named is gone: `@slack/web-api@8.1.1` does have `agents.sessions.{setStatus,rename}`, where 7.19.0 had nothing. What is left is the reason it was reverted on 2026-09-12: the button only renders while the session is `processing`, and setting that conflicts with the status text. Mastra's `typingStatus` (`agents/orchestrator.ts:192`, `chat/status/`) writes through the adapter's legacy `assistant.threads.setStatus`, and once an agent session exists the legacy text sticks after the first update. `agents.sessions.setStatus` has no free-text field at all, only `active|processing|suspended|closed`.

  The Agents-native replacement for per-step text is streamed task chunks, not a status string: `chat.startStream` takes `task_display_mode: 'timeline' | 'plan'`, and `@slack/types` defines `TaskUpdateChunk` as `{type: 'task_update', id, title, status: 'pending'|'in_progress'|'complete'|'error', details?, output?, sources?}`. So finishing this means moving tool progress off `typingStatus` and onto streamed `task_update` chunks, which Mastra's channels layer does not emit, so it needs a `patches/` change. That is the same work as the "multiple options for tool display, status or kyto-like blocks" item below: `TaskUpdateChunk` with a timeline display is that block display, and the stop button falls out of it because both need the session in `processing`. Do them together, on their own branch, with live testing: it changes the most visible surface of the bot.

  Also note the adapter bundles its own nested `@slack/web-api@7.19.0`, so `slack.webClient.agents` is undefined at runtime, not merely untyped. Any `agents.sessions.*` call needs a `WebClient` built from the hoisted 8.1.1.
- **[Rewrite parity]** `get_slack_emoji` tool (2026-09-23). `src/mastra/tools/slack/get-slack-emoji.ts`, deferred (behind tool search) in `toolsets.ts`, in `slackTools`, status label in `chat/status/statuses.ts`. Takes a shortcode with or without colons (skin-tone suffix stripped), resolves via `emoji.list` cached module-wide for `emoji.listTtl` (5 min, `config.ts`) with one `spendSlackCall` per real fetch, follows `alias:` up to 5 hops (same bound as the old `lib/emoji.ts` from `f7bb65b^`). Unicode or unknown names return `custom: false` with a message (aliases to Unicode say so). Only fetches `https://emoji.slack-edge.com` with `redirect: 'error'`, caps at `image.maxViewBytes`, types by magic bytes via `viewableImageType` (extracted from `view-image.ts`, now shared), saves to `<workdir>/emoji/<name>.<ext>`, and returns a `media` part like `view_image` so `moveToolImages` relocates it. Not in Slack code mode (needs the sandbox and returns media). Not exercised against the live bot.
- **[Rewrite parity]** Actionable MCP auth errors, issue #37 (2026-09-23, cleanup step 7, uncommitted). The opaque App Home message came from gorkie's own `cleanMCPErrorMessage`, not Mastra: it took line 1 of `errors[name]` (stack trace follows, and the `(HTTP 401)` suffix sits on the last line), and parsed only `{error, error_description}`, so Twenty's NestJS body `{"statusCode":401,"error":"UnauthorizedException","messages":["Unauthorized"]}` collapsed to `UnauthorizedException`. The SDK (MCP client SDK 2.0.0) drops the WWW-Authenticate header, keeping only status and body. Replaced by one async `describeMCPError({ server, details })` in `src/mastra/mcp/errors.ts`, used by `user-servers/probe.ts` and `user-servers/tools.ts`, reading `listToolsWithErrors().errorDetails` (`MCPDiscoveryErrorDetails`, `httpStatus` used, string `code` ignored as it is the SDK transport code). Output `<phase>: <upstream>[ (HTTP n)][ (MCP error n)]. <hint>`; phase from the `Failed to connect to MCP server <name>: ` prefix (else `Listing tools failed`); lenient Zod body parse covers RFC 6750, NestJS, JSON-RPC; upstream redacted (token, Bearer/Basic, auth headers, OAuth query params, JWTs, URL credentials) then capped at 160 chars so the hint survives. On 401 only, OAuth detection via the SDK's `discoverOAuthProtectedResourceMetadata` (re-exported by `@mastra/mcp`), fresh fetch with no auth header, `redirect: 'manual'`, one 2s `AbortSignal` shared across the path-aware and root lookups. Not on 403: its hint does not depend on OAuth (plan said 401/403). Server name deliberately omitted from `lastError` because App Home and `!connections` already label by name. `lastError` text still never reaches the model (third-party text, prompt-injection surface); orchestrator keeps names only. Deferred: rewriting auth errors at tool-call time (next turn's `tools/list` already updates `lastError`). Tests: `src/mastra/mcp/errors.test.ts` (13 cases, local `Bun.serve`), new `"test": "bun test"` script. `mastra build` bundles by import graph and its source-hash glob excludes `*.test.ts`. Manual check still owed: add Twenty with a bad token in the owner's running instance.
- **[Rewrite parity]** MCP add refuses a duplicate name (2026-09-23). `db/queries/mcps.ts` `upsertMCPServer` became `insertMCPServer`: returns `'name-taken'` under the existing advisory lock and does a plain insert (no `onConflictDoUpdate`). `chat/app-home/mcp/actions.ts` shows it as a modal error on the `name` field. The configure path only calls `setMCPServerPermission`, so it never used upsert and is unaffected; changing URL/token now means remove and re-add.
- **[Guardrails]** `list_channels` lists public channels only (2026-09-23). `src/mastra/tools/slack/list-channels.ts`: `types: 'public_channel'`, the `private` output field removed, description says private channels and DMs are never listed.
- **[Guardrails]** `list_canvases` workspace scope no longer leaks canvases from private channels and DMs (2026-09-23). `src/mastra/tools/canvas/list.ts` now parses each file's `channels`/`groups`/`ims`, checks every distinct channel with `assertReadableChannel` (the same gate `readableCanvas`/`assertReadableResource` use) in batches of 4, never cached, and keeps a canvas only if at least one of its channels passes. Canvases shared nowhere (standalone, unshared) are dropped too, since the readable-resource check rejects a resource with no channel. A filtered page can hold fewer than `limit`. Still one `spendSlackCall` per tool call, matching every other tool (visibility lookups are not budgeted anywhere).
- **[Guardrails]** `react` and `edit_canvas` write only to the current conversation (2026-09-23). Owner reversed the earlier "canvas edit stays gated at read scope" call. `react.ts` swaps `assertReadableChannel` for `assertCanPostTo` with a channel target (applies to add and remove). `canvas/utils.ts` `assertCanManageChannel` now takes `channelIds: string[]` and passes if any matches the current channel; `create.ts` passes `[channelId]`, `edit.ts` passes the canvas file's channels/groups/ims after `readableCanvas`. Consequence: standalone canvases not shared into the current channel can no longer be edited. Descriptions updated. Reads unchanged.
- **[Guardrails]** `search_slack` on `wait` and scheduled-task wakes (owner request 2026-09-23; DONE 2026-09-24 after the owner raised permissions: guard and `messageId: undefined` override removed, docs/slack-search.md updated, all public-only gates kept). Plan was: drop the `messageId` guard and the "never runs on scheduled or unattended turns" text in `src/mastra/tools/slack/search-slack.ts`, revert the `messageId: undefined` override in `src/mastra/tools/wait.ts` and `src/mastra/tools/scheduled-tasks/create.ts`, update `docs/slack-search.md`. The Claude Code auto-mode classifier blocked it as a security weakening, so the partial edit was reverted and all three files are as they were. The owner needs to make or explicitly approve this change.
- **[CRITICAL] DONE (924cbb0e): native streaming back ON with a `@chat-adapter/slack` recovery patch.** Re-verified 2026-09-24 against git log fd28c586..c67d46b3. The owner reversed the keep-off call in the final review: the adapter patch treats `message_not_found` as recoverable and continues the reply in a new message instead of losing it, and `nativeStreaming` is re-enabled, so Compact and Detailed tool display render again. The patch's stop-button hunk was removed (see the rejected requester-only stop in the final review entry). Live test (a long turn that outlives the stream) in the owner checklist. Original item: **[Owner decision 2026-09-24: keep native streaming OFF; the `nativeStreaming: false` stopgap (`chat/client.ts:16`) is the chosen fix. WP-A (adapter finalize patch) is dropped, not built; a draft patch exists only in the session scratchpad. Mitigated by staying off: `@chat-adapter/slack` 4.41.0 (no newer release) still rethrows `message_not_found` at `dist/index.js` ~5094/5132/5216. Consequence: the Compact and Detailed tool display modes depend on native streaming, since in fallback mode the adapter skips structured `task_update` chunks (~5144-5150), so they likely render nothing today. Needs a live check and an owner call: hide those two modes, or re-enable streaming later.]** **Native streaming silently loses whole replies (this is the "blank responses" bug).** On a long turn (model ladder retrying for minutes), the Slack native-streaming placeholder is expired by Slack; the finalize `ChatStreamer.stop` -> `chat.stopStream` then returns `message_not_found` (and a sibling streaming write returns `not_authorized` with `acceptedScopes:["chat:write"]`, same event, not a scope bug). Verified via the `@chat-adapter/slack` source (`node_modules/@chat-adapter/slack/dist/index.js` ~4143): when `fallback.nativeRendered` is true the `stop()` catch **rethrows** instead of re-posting; Mastra's buffered fallback (`@mastra/core` `openSession`, agent bundle ~19361-19410) then reconstructs from an **already-drained buffer** and posts nothing. Net: the streamed message vanishes and the user gets no reply. gorkie does NOT delete the placeholder (swept `src/mastra/**`, no chat.delete/update against it); the disappearance is Slack-side, driven by how long the stream stays open. **Stopgap applied 2026-09-14:** `nativeStreaming: false` in `src/mastra/chat/client.ts` (durable post-and-edit; a later `editMessage` message_not_found fails benignly because content is already posted). Cost: loses the native streaming widget and the stop/feedback buttons the adapter can only attach in native mode. **Proper fix (do this, then re-enable native streaming):** patch `@chat-adapter/slack` `index.js` ~4143 via Bun `patchedDependencies` (already used for `@mastra/core`, see `package.json`) so the `stop()` catch, on `message_not_found`, re-posts `renderer.getCommittableText()` as a fresh message instead of rethrowing; upstream the same fix. Also mitigated indirectly by faster model failover (glm default, 2026-09-14) so turns do not hold the stream open for minutes. `not_authorized` needs no separate fix; if it must be pinned, read the `apiCall('<method>') start` debug line now that Slack logs route through `slackWebLogger`.

- **[Audit before anything else] DONE: final review fixes (2026-09-24).** Re-verified 2026-09-24 against git log fd28c586..c67d46b3. Batch B docs (e5697e17): sandbox prompt says no GitHub credential in the sandbox, use `github_checkout`/`github_push_branch`; tool display, tool-search reloads and `!stop` scope described accurately; `docs/brokered-git.md` rewritten; README env table, skills and processors complete; Langfuse is the main trace store. Batch F lifecycle (09acac39): `run_background` registers its job before the first await; the keepalive refreshes the newest live job's sandbox via `retryOnDead`; a wake with no saved channel rebuilds it from the thread id; the workspace no longer imports chat (the live view ends via `endSandboxTurn`). Batch A (11dd9652): `chat/notify.ts` replaces five copies of posting an ephemeral notice, so DM notices are now private; ban cards, notices and lists use Slack date tokens (reader's timezone) instead of server time; `!help` lists `!mcps`; `!stop` text lowercase. Batches C+D (82b32833): `config.ts` gains search, slack, history, shutdown and observability groups; unused exports dropped, types into `types/`, one-use helpers inlined, no `Reflect.set` in tool-display; `guardedFetch` keeps the caller's abort signal; failed OAuth probes are not cached; `upload_emoji` size cap; hidden failures logged at warn; gate fixes: a root mention follows the thread only after commands, the stop cutoff and the usage limit pass, and commands sent before a stop or leave are dropped. Owner decisions carried out: native streaming ON with the adapter patch (924cbb0e); approvals fail closed, runs with no inbound message fall back to the stored requester and a card with none is refused (924cbb0e); input cap 950k; `@opentui/core` removed; the image model (131K context) documented as outside the 1M ladder rule; `!stop` does NOT cancel pending waits; `!compact` left as is. REJECTED by the owner and reverted before commit: requester-only stop plus a `recordTurnRequester` processor ("it worked fine before"); `!stop` and the Slack stop button are unchanged, and the adapter patch's stop-button hunk was removed. REVERSED by the owner later the same day: GitHub DM-only (ebfdbafeb2) became opt-in shared threads for BOTH GitHub and user MCP tools (e5588027): DMs by default, a per-user App Home toggle (`user_settings.github_threads`, `mcp_threads`, migration `20260924062558_github_mcp_threads`), one `levelOutsideDM` clamp for both (never-ask becomes ask before writing; MCP 'delete' clamped to 'write' outside DMs, pending owner confirmation), shared-thread safety text back in the GitHub prompt and skill. The MCP owner-only/approve-every-call idea from the security review was dropped. Final passes: desloppify (c72a92b1) fixed three real bugs: `!constructor` swallowed a message because the command table was a plain object (now a Map); `get_slack_file` saved an empty file when a HEAD had no content-length; `search_slack` failed the whole search when one channel's visibility check failed (now drops that channel). It also shared the readable-channel and private-file helpers, derived `ModerationEvent`/`ToolDisplay` from Drizzle and Mastra, removed re-export files and one-use wrappers (`user-servers/index`, `workspace/path`, `isUserAllowed`, `findMCPOAuthHostError`, github index) and gave the scheduled-task tools one `ownSchedules` guard. No-comments (5ae6f0ca, about 300 lines): only verified vendor and platform facts kept. The `PROJECT_ROOT` warning in env.ts was restored after an agent wrongly claimed `mastra dev` sets the repo root: `startServer` receives `.mastra/output`, so `MASTRA_PROJECT_ROOT` is `.mastra` (same mistake as in the 2026-09-23 step 3). Flag fixes (c67d46b3, 23 items): background jobs register through a handle (attach-before-register impossible), `hasLiveJob` read-only; `endSandboxTurn` closes the live view before pausing; CDP edge vs loopback URLs named; a `refuseDefaultBranch` guard; one Slack thread decode for the post gate and destination; MCP 401 stored as `last_error_http_status` (migration `20260924064740_mcp_error_http_status` backfills it); tagged results for `claimTurn` and `banStatus`; `githubAccess` needs `isDM`; `optInStatus` no longer rebuilds the list as a side effect, only the message handler triggers it; thread state fields renamed with old keys still read. Also 238d2c8a dropped `textLength` from the turn log. Original item: **Final review fixes (2026-09-24).** Code-quality review findings in batches A (one shared notice helper, UTC ban times, !mcps in help, lowercase stop text), B (stale prompts/docs: sandbox prompt GitHub credentials, push.ts write-path sentence, docs/brokered-git.md, README gaps, TODO device-flow/Socket-Mode mentions, em dashes), C (magic numbers into config.ts), D (unused exports getSandbox/hackclub/OAuthOutcome, types into types/, Reflect.set in tool-display, swallowed catches, upload-emoji size cap, guardedFetch abort signal, advertisesOAuth failure eviction, MCP redirect URI in one place), F (run_background vs pauseSandbox race, keepalive using a stale sandbox, wake after restart has no channel, workspace<->live-view import). Owner decisions: TURN NATIVE STREAMING ON (reverses the earlier keep-off call) together with the draft @chat-adapter/slack patch that treats message_not_found as recoverable (draft in the session scratchpad), so Compact/Detailed tool display render and blank replies stay fixed; only the person who started the request may approve/deny (already patched) and stop it via the Slack stop button or !stop (moderators exempt), like earlier gorkie; lower config input cap to 950k; remove @opentui/core; document the image model (131K context) as outside the 1M fallback-ladder rule; do NOT make !stop cancel pending waits; !compact left as is. LATER SAME DAY, owner REVERSED the DM-only calls: restore original gorkie behaviour for BOTH GitHub and user MCP tools: DMs by default, shared threads allowed per user when they enable it in App Home (GitHub: restore user_settings.github_threads toggle + levelsFor clamp to 'write'/'all' outside DMs as in 6bae9df lib/github/access.ts; MCP: add the equivalent per-user shared-threads toggle), approval levels per the user's settings as before; the MCP owner-only/approve-every-call idea from the security review is dropped. Then rerun /desloppify and /no-comments over the final code.

- **[Product] DONE: agent security review (WP-M) and fixes.** Re-verified 2026-09-24 against git log fd28c586..c67d46b3. Findings from the WP-M review, with outcomes: #1 CRITICAL, Mastra's browser stream WebSocket and `/api/agents/:agentId/browser/{session,close}` were registered before `server.middleware`, so through the tunnel anyone with a thread id could watch and drive the browser or close every session; fixed in d5e2adf0 by moving the guard to `mastra.setServerMiddleware`. #2 HIGH, scheduled-task tools acted for the thread starter, not the speaker; fixed in d5e2adf0 (`ownResourceId`). #3 HIGH, user MCP tools loaded in shared threads; superseded by the owner's shared-thread restore (opt-in per user, e5588027). #4 an approval stash without a requester let any click through; fixed in 924cbb0e (fail closed, stored-requester fallback). #6 OAuth consent phishing; fixed in 446a195f (single-use start tickets, the interstitial names @handle and id). #7 the default-branch refusal read the agent-writable `origin/HEAD`; fixed in 446a195f (GitHub API `default_branch`). #8 a host death inside the credential window left the github.com auth rule on; fixed in 924cbb0e (sandbox start resets network rules). #9 unmetered wakes; fixed in 446a195f (schedule and wait fires counted) and 924cbb0e (background wakes counted, schedules capped at 10 per user). #10 bot token sent to any `url_private`; fixed in 446a195f (Slack file hosts only, redirects not followed). #11 `!focus` in a thread with no owner; fixed in 446a195f (first poster or a moderator). #12 agent-writable git config in the credential window; fixed in 446a195f (global/system config ignored, safe values forced, `insteadOf`/filter keys refused). Still open, in TODO under Open owner decisions: #5 live-view input injection, and the untraced claim from #3 that a message sent during someone else's active run executes with their request context. Original item: Run an agent security scan against gorkie.

A subagent saves a long finding as a Markdown document under a reserved `artifacts/` prefix and returns only an id; the parent reads it by id instead of carrying the text through context. gorkie's delegation prompt currently just tells children to "tell it where to put anything bulky", which is a convention rather than a mechanism, and `agent-explore` writes files into a sandbox the parent has to go find. eve bounds the body at 200k chars, closes the artifact kinds to a fixed set so the reader knows what shape it holds, and validates the model-supplied id against an anchored pattern with no dots or slashes so a read cannot traverse out of the prefix.

- **[Product] DONE, partly (WP-J, 98320648).** Re-verified 2026-09-24 against the tree. The port landed on Mastra 1.69 natives (see the Backgrounding entry). A timeout is now required (<= 3600s) on both `run_background` and background `execute_command`. Auto-convert-on-timeout is NOT built: Mastra has nothing native for it, and owner decision Q7 says build it only after the completion wake is live-verified. Left in TODO. Original item: **[Q7 decided 2026-09-24: port the `background` branch now; cap background jobs at 60 min with a 10-min keepalive; background `timeout` required; auto-convert-on-timeout only after the completion wake is live-verified; confirm the E2B plan limit (Hobby allows 1 h). WP-J.]** Issue 35, proper background task tool, and the owner has now named the exact design to copy. The 8h comment on the issue pastes Claude Code's own pattern: a command that exceeds its timeout is not killed, it is *moved to the background* automatically with an id and an output file path, returned immediately, and the caller is notified when it completes (`Command did not complete within its 420s timeout and was moved to the background (ID: ...). Output is being written to: ...`). That is a better shape than what we shipped, which makes the model pre-declare `background: true` and silently loses a foreground command at the 15 minute cap. The target design: a foreground `execute_command` that hits its timeout should auto-convert to a background process rather than dying, hand back an id and a sandbox log path, and surface the result on a later turn. Timeout is required on the tool, per the issue. Note this composes with the fix already made (VM lifetime now exceeds the command cap, background processes survive via `abortSignal: false`), so the missing piece is the auto-convert-on-timeout and the completion notification, not the plumbing underneath. The notification path is the open question: channels wakes a thread on a new message, so a completed background job needs something to poke the thread, which is the same wake-path question the host-side `backgroundTasks`/`untilIdle` research raised. **Update 2026-09-13: durable completion notification done via prompt, not push.** `prompts/features/sandbox.ts` tells the model to chain `background: true` -> `wait` -> `get_process_output` -> report; `wait` uses Mastra's Postgres-backed schedules so it survives a restart, unlike an in-memory pid registry, and needs no onExit plumbing. Value is capped until pause-deferral, since `processOutputResult` still freezes the job between turns so it only advances during waking turns. Remaining, in order: (1) pause-deferral, skip the `processOutputResult` pause while a job is pending for the thread, needs only an in-memory pending-job flag with a safe fallback (lost flag -> pause as today), touches the live pause path; (2) auto-convert-on-timeout; (3) true push-on-exit, which needs a durable pid->thread map to survive the watchdog restarts, i.e. a Postgres job table = schema change, raise first. `onExit` meta carries only `{pid, toolCallId, exitCode, stdout, stderr}`, no thread/requestContext/schedules, which is why push needs the registry. **Mechanism confirmed 2026-09-12 by reading `workspace-Ds7WdjRA.js:8433-8520`, and it surfaces a real danger the owner flagged.** The background branch calls `sandbox.processes.spawn(command, { timeout: input.timeout ?? undefined, abortSignal })` and returns `Started background process (PID: n)` immediately, never `.wait()`ing, which is why it is not subject to the 15 minute foreground cap (that cap is the `.wait()` on the foreground path). Two things then remove every bound: `timeout` is `input.timeout ?? undefined`, so if the model omits it the process has **no timeout at all**, and we set `backgroundProcesses.abortSignal: false`, which the code resolves to `undefined` (`bgConfig.abortSignal || void 0`), so the turn-end abort no longer kills it either. The only thing bounding a no-timeout background process today is the accident that `processOutputResult` pauses the VM at turn end and freezes it; the moment we add the pause-deferral the auto-background design needs, that accidental cap disappears and a runaway `while true &` runs until the VM lifetime expires, burning the sandbox. So a cap is mandatory and must land in the same change as the keepalive: either make `timeout` required on the background branch (the issue says required) or enforce a max via `backgroundProcesses`, and keep the VM lifetime as the hard backstop. The cap should be larger than the 15 minute foreground cap, since the whole point of backgrounding is longer work, which means the VM lifetime and the keepalive re-arm have to stretch to match it (E2B allows up to 1 hour on Hobby, 24 on Pro), so the three numbers move together: background max timeout, VM lifetime, keepalive interval. How progress works meanwhile: a background process runs only while the VM is live, freezes on the turn-end pause, and resumes from the snapshot on the next sandbox command (`e2b start()` resumes "with background processes still running"), so it continues across turns but makes no progress between them. Mastra's agent loop never pauses the sandbox itself, so `processors/sandbox.ts` is not a duplicate, it is the only pause, which is why this is entirely our lever.

- **[Backgrounding] DONE except step 5 (WP-J, 98320648).** Re-verified 2026-09-24 against the tree. Ported onto Mastra 1.69 natives rather than cherry-picking the old branch as is: `backgroundTasks` enabled in `src/mastra/index.ts` (hourly cleanup); `tools/run-background.ts` `run_background` with a required timeout <= 3600s, spawn plus `handle.wait` with abort, and `onComplete`/`onFailed` waking the thread via `sendSignal` `ifIdle: wake` with the saved channel context; the turn footer says "working in the background" and has no thumbs; `workspace/jobs.ts` is a live-job registry, so `pauseSandbox` is deferred while a job is live, with a 10-minute keepalive; a background `execute_command` also requires a timeout <= 3600s (enforced in `beforeToolCall`); the sandbox prompt is updated. Steps 1-4 and 6 done. Step 5 (prompt guidance for `run_background`) waits for the live checks. CORRECTION: the 2026-09-13 blocker "you cannot flip `execute_command` to background-eligible" no longer holds on 1.69, where `defaultDisposition` exists (unused). Caveats: once enabled, Mastra injects its own background-task text into the system prompt; a host restart marks running tasks failed without calling `onFailed`, so there is no wake; `!stop` does not yet kill background `execute_command` processes (WP-L). Langfuse audit #11 fixed by this. Original section text follows. Native Mastra background tasks (added 1.29.0) ARE installed and `@mastra/pg` ships the store, so they're adoptable. What they give gorkie: the tool's `execute` runs in-process on the host as an internal workflow, independent of the agent turn (dispatch, LLM finishes, turn ends, task keeps running), plus a durable Postgres task record (`mastra_background_tasks`), concurrency/retries/timeouts/suspend-resume, and `onTaskComplete`/`onTaskFailed` callbacks. THE BLOCKER (verified): completion re-invokes the loop only via `agent.stream({ untilIdle })`, and gorkie never calls `agent.stream`, channels does. `untilIdle` appears NOWHERE in the channels dist (`node_modules/@mastra/core/dist/channels/`), and background-task completion emits no signal of its own. So a finished task silently writes its result to memory and nothing posts to Slack until the next user message. Patching channels to pass `untilIdle` is wrong: it holds the Slack run active (typing + Plan widget) for the whole job (default 5-min idle timeout between turns), defeating the point. AgentController (what Factory uses) also passes no `untilIdle`, so migrating buys nothing here. THE FIX (no core change): bridge completion to gorkie's OWN existing signal-wake, the exact mechanism `wait.ts` already uses. Register `onTaskComplete`/`onTaskFailed` that call `orchestrator.sendSignal({ type: 'notification', ... }, { threadId, resourceId, ifIdle: { behavior: 'wake', streamOptions: { requestContext: { channel: ... } } } })`; the woken run's channels output processor rebuilds the Slack render context from the thread's persisted channel coords and posts. Factory does the same shape via SignalProviders (external event → notify → idle-wake). What native tasks do NOT fix (irreducible custom work): the E2B keepalive. The dispatching turn still hits `processOutputResult -> pauseSandbox` at turn end (also `onAbort`/`onError`), freezing the box under the running task. Must (a) gate `pauseSandbox` on `backgroundTaskManager.listTasks({ threadId, status:'running' })` being empty so a thread with a live task keeps its box, (b) keep refreshing `e2b.setTimeout` from inside the long `execute` (current cap 16 min, `config.ts:5`), accepting the box stays running/billed, not paused, for the job, and (c) accept a host restart mid-build still loses the in-sandbox work (the task record survives; the executor closure and live E2B command do not). BLOCKER FOUND 2026-09-13 (corrects the research's "mark the long sandbox tool background"): you CANNOT flip `execute_command` to background-eligible. Agent-level `AgentBackgroundToolConfig` is only `boolean | { enabled, timeoutMs }` (`node_modules/@mastra/core/dist/background-tasks/types.d.ts:176`), with NO `defaultDisposition`, and eligible tools default to `deferred`. Since channels never passes `untilIdle`, a deferred `execute_command` dispatches async and its result NEVER returns in-turn, breaking every command. `defaultDisposition: 'foreground'` exists only on the tool-level `ToolBackgroundConfig` (a `createTool`), which the Mastra-injected workspace `execute_command` can't set. So backgrounding needs a NEW gorkie-owned `createTool` (e.g. `run_background(command)`) with tool-level `background: { enabled: true, timeoutMs }` (deferred is correct for a dedicated bg tool the model calls only when it wants backgrounding); `execute_command` stays synchronous. Steps, ranked: [ ] 1. Enable `Mastra({ backgroundTasks: { enabled: true } })` (SCHEMA CHANGE: creates `mastra_background_tasks`, approved by owner 2026-09-13). [ ] 2. Build a gorkie `run_background` `createTool` with tool-level `background: { enabled: true, timeoutMs }` that shells the long command into the thread's E2B sandbox; do NOT background `execute_command`. [ ] 3. Add the `onTaskComplete`/`onTaskFailed` -> `orchestrator.sendSignal({type:'notification'}, { threadId, resourceId, ifIdle: { behavior:'wake', streamOptions } })` bridge (target shape: `agent/types.d.ts` SendAgentSignalOptions idle branch = `{ resourceId, threadId, ifIdle }`, ifIdle carries `streamOptions`/`AgentSignalIfIdleOptions`). Wrap in try/catch so a callback throw can't destabilize the manager. UNVERIFIED offline: whether a bare idle-wake reconstructs the Slack render context (channels `buildRenderContextForThread`) so it actually posts; needs a LIVE test (worst case: task completes, no Slack post, recoverable). [ ] 4. Guard `pauseSandbox` (and abort/error pause) so a thread with a running task (`backgroundTaskManager.listTasks({ threadId, status:'running' })`) keeps its box; the box only lives to `sandbox.timeout` (16 min) from the last tool call, so the bg tool's `execute` must periodically refresh `e2b.setTimeout` for longer jobs (box stays running/billed). [ ] 5. Prompt guidance for `run_background` LAST, only after the bridge is verified to post live, so the model never advertises a background path that silently drops. [ ] 6. Keep `wait` for generic delays/external polling; consider a `SignalProvider` for external-system wakes (GitHub CI). Replaces the `execute_command background:true` + `abortSignal:false` keepalive hack and the wait-poll-for-done loop. BUILT 2026-09-13 (typecheck + biome green, but UNTESTED, needs live verification on the running bot): - [x] Enabled `Mastra({ backgroundTasks: { enabled, onTaskComplete, onTaskFailed } })` in `index.ts` (creates `mastra_background_tasks` on start). - [x] New `src/mastra/tools/run-background.ts` `run_background(command, reason)` `createTool`: `requireSandbox` -> `e2b.commands.run(command, { timeoutMs: sandbox.backgroundTimeout=30min })`, a `setInterval` keepalive refreshing `e2b.setTimeout` every 10 min, output tailed to 10k chars, returns `{ exitCode, stdout, stderr }`. Registered in `orchestratorTools` + a `statuses.ts` entry. - [x] Opted in at agent level (`orchestrator.backgroundTasks.tools.run_background = { enabled, timeoutMs }`) so it dispatches deferred; `execute_command` untouched. - [x] Completion bridge `notifyBackgroundTask` in `index.ts`: `onTaskComplete`/`onTaskFailed` -> `orchestrator.sendSignal({ type:'notification', contents }, { threadId, resourceId, ifIdle:{behavior:'wake'} })`, wrapped in try/catch. - [x] `pauseSandbox` guard: skips pause when `backgroundTaskManager.listTasks({ threadId, status:'running' })` is non-empty, so a live job keeps its box. - LIVE TESTS STILL REQUIRED (cannot verify offline): (1) does a deferred task's `execute` receive `context.requestContext` with the channel so `requireSandbox` resolves the box (if not, run_background fails fast -> onTaskFailed wake); (2) does the idle-wake actually post to Slack (channels render-context rebuild) or silently no-op; (3) does the pause-guard + 10-min keepalive keep the box alive for a >16-min job end to end; (4) prompt guidance for the model to prefer run_background for long jobs is NOT added yet, add it only once (1) and (2) are confirmed so the model never advertises a background path that drops. R3/R4 model caps (models.dev) still owner-only.

- **[Known issues] DONE (WP-H, 01eaff53).** Re-verified 2026-09-24 against the tree. Ciphertext format `v2.<kid>.`; decrypt tries `CREDENTIALS_KEY` then `CREDENTIALS_KEY_PREVIOUS`; the state signers verify with either key. At boot `resealSecrets` re-encrypts `github_credentials`, `mcp_servers.token` and `mcp_oauth` with the current key (replaces `encryptPlaintextMCPTokens`), compare-and-set so a concurrent token refresh wins; rows neither key opens are logged and left in place. An unreadable secret never throws: MCP OAuth becomes needs-auth, an MCP token gets a lastError and is skipped, GitHub already showed "unreadable". Documented in README and `.env.example`. Known gap: OAuth rows marked needs-auth because the previous key was missing need a manual Reconnect. Owner steps in the checklist. Original item: **[Q12 decided 2026-09-24: yes to `CREDENTIALS_KEY_PREVIOUS`; WP-H.]** `CREDENTIALS_KEY` has no rotation story. Changing it makes every stored GitHub and MCP token undecryptable, and `decryptSecret` would throw on each one rather than prompting a reconnect. At minimum, catch the failure and treat it as disconnected; better, keep a key id in the `v1.` prefix so two keys can be live during a rotation.

- **[Known issues] DONE (WP-H, 01eaff53).** Re-verified 2026-09-24 against the tree. Servers whose `lastError` contains `(HTTP 401)` or whose token cannot be decrypted are skipped (`credentialError`) until removed and re-added or reconnected through OAuth, so the reconnect loop stops. No `updatedAt` column: rejected because the schema is owned elsewhere and `lastError` already resets on every recovery path. Live check (Fathom records `(HTTP 401)` and stops retrying) in the checklist. Original item: A user MCP server (Fathom) fails on every reconnect with `invalid_token / No authorization provided` and retries in a loop, filling the log with two stack traces per attempt. Either the stored token is empty or that server wants OAuth rather than a bearer token. Worth backing off after repeated auth failures instead of retrying forever.

- **[CRITICAL] DONE (WP-F, ebfdbafeb2).** Re-verified 2026-09-24 against the tree. GitHub is DMs only: migration `20260924053636_github_dm_only_last_error` drops `user_settings.github_threads` and adds `github_credentials.last_error`; `levelsFor` and the App Home threads toggle are gone, so shared threads always get the handoff. Option (c), per owner decision Q1. Live check: shared threads still get the handoff. Original item: **[Q1 decided 2026-09-24: option (c), drop `settings.threads`; GitHub is DM-only and shared threads get the existing handoff. Build in WP-F (migration drops `github_threads`, `levelsFor` outside-DM branches, the App Home toggle). Focus mode later as a general anti-hijack feature.]** **Shared-thread credential steering (confirmed, unmitigated; needs a decision).** `chat/history.ts` `withHistory` copies other users' messages into a turn's context, and `lib/github/access.ts` exposes github credentialed tools when `direct = isDM || settings.threads` is true, so in an opted-in shared thread a turn can hold `github_*` write tools AND other-authored history. Anyone in that thread can plant "go ahead and push" before a credentialed turn to steer it. There is NO focus/filtering system in `chat/`, `agents/`, or `processors/` (the only `focus` hit is a prompt word in `summarizer.ts`); CodeRabbit's focus-ordering debate refers to code not present at HEAD. Options to decide between: (a) build a focus system that filters history to the addressing user for credentialed turns; (b) do not inject other-authored history on a turn that loads `github_*` write tools; (c) drop `settings.threads` (github write only in DMs), simplest and safest; (d) treat injected history as strictly non-instructional at the prompt level (weak on its own). Owner decision needed; do not build blind.

- **[Known issues] DONE (WP-F, ebfdbafeb2).** Re-verified 2026-09-24 against the tree. GitHub is DMs only: migration `20260924053636_github_dm_only_last_error` drops `user_settings.github_threads` and adds `github_credentials.last_error`; `levelsFor` and the App Home threads toggle are gone, so shared threads always get the handoff. A checkout therefore only happens in a DM sandbox. Original item: **[Q1 decided 2026-09-24: closes when `settings.threads` is dropped, since a checkout then only happens in a DM sandbox.]** A `github_checkout` clone leaves a private repository on a sandbox that is per thread and outlives the turn, so anyone in that thread can later ask gorkie to read those files. Focus mode only seals the turn that holds the credential, not the filesystem afterwards. Approval on the tool means the person agrees to the clone, but not necessarily to the persistence. Either scope the sandbox per user, clear the checkout at turn end, or say plainly on the approval card that the code stays in a shared workspace.

- **[Known issues] DONE (WP-F, ebfdbafeb2).** Re-verified 2026-09-24 against the tree. `processors/tool-search.ts` wraps `ToolSearchProcessor` with `includeResolvedTools`, withholding only GitHub and user MCP tools (a `searchOnly` set on the request context); Slack core tools stay loaded. BUG FOUND: GitHub tools had no `id`, so Mastra ids them `tool-<hash>` and search could never find them; fixed with `id` = key. FOUND: a loaded tool unloads when OM observes its messages (`removeByIds`); not prevented, the prompts tell the model to re-search. The "already loaded; do not search" line in `prompts/github.ts` is removed. Original item: **[Q2 decided 2026-09-24: GitHub tools behind `search_tools`, Slack core tools resident; user MCP tools stay resident unless the `filter` hook allows selective withholding. WP-F.]** Decide where the 33 GitHub tools live, because right now they are the largest fixed cost in the prompt and the one block tool search cannot see. Measured 2026-09-12 by constructing the real toolset: the exposed 33 come to 34,661 characters of name, description and JSON schema, roughly 8,665 tokens, paid on every turn by every connected person whether or not the thread has anything to do with GitHub. For scale, the full `@github-tools/sdk` surface is 79 tools at about 18,886 tokens, and OM holds message history between roughly 6k and 30k, so the GitHub block alone can exceed the retained conversation. They arrive through the dynamic `tools` function, which `ToolSearchProcessor` does not index (it is configured with `tools: deferredTools`, `topK: 2`), and that is the mechanical cause of the separate known issue where `search_tools` never returns `github_*` and the agent tells people the tools are unavailable and then calls them anyway. The obvious fix, `includeResolvedTools`, is all-or-nothing over the resolved set, so it would also hide `post_message`, `react` and the Slack code-mode tool behind a search, which is clearly worse. The argument for leaving them resident is the one nobody has written down: OM's whole design goal is a stable, cacheable prompt prefix, and tool definitions sit in that prefix, so a tool list that varies per turn invalidates the cache every turn and can cost more than the 8,665 tokens it saves. That points at thread-level gating rather than turn-level search: resolve the GitHub block once per thread on a cheap signal and keep it stable for the rest of the thread, so the prefix stays cacheable within a conversation while a thread about lunch never pays for it. Whatever is decided, fix the `search_tools` lie independently, since the agent misleading people about its own capabilities is a bug at any tool count. **Position revised 2026-09-12 toward putting them in search, after the owner pushed back.** The prompt-cache objection was weaker than stated: we run `storage: 'context'`, where per the processor's own docs "loaded state lives in the conversation messages" (`processors/processors/tool-search.d.ts:160-161`), so a tool loaded once stays loaded for the rest of the thread. That makes the cost one prefix invalidation per thread at first load, not one per turn, which is a very different trade against 8,665 tokens on every turn of every thread that never touches GitHub. Two things to verify before doing it. First, `ToolSearchProcessor` takes a `filter` hook described as "request-aware ... for filtering tools during search, load, and active tool injection" (`:93-96`), which may be the way around `includeResolvedTools` being all-or-nothing, so `post_message` and `react` can stay resident while GitHub goes search-only; that is unverified and is the thing that decides whether this is even possible. Second, and this is the new risk: `storage: 'context'` keeps loaded state in the messages, and observational memory compresses messages away, so a load marker that gets observed and dropped could silently unload the tools mid-thread. Test a long GitHub thread across an observation cycle before trusting it.

The "already loaded; do not search" line in `prompts/github.ts` is removed. - **[Simplification] DONE (WP-F, ebfdbafeb2).** Re-verified 2026-09-24 against the tree. The configure modal uses chat `Modal`/`RadioSelect`; `dispatchAction` turned out unneeded once Q1 removed the threads toggle. Original item: Move `github/views.ts` onto the `chat` builders once `dispatch_action` exists upstream. Filed 2026-09-11 as vercel/chat#926. Correcting an earlier estimate in this file: there is almost nothing to convert today. `failedModal` and `connectedModal` already use `Modal()` and `CardText()`, and the only two raw `ModalView` builders left, `configureView` and `connectView`, are exactly the two that need `dispatch_action` to re-render on change. So the "do the unblocked half now" plan was based on conflating hand-built lines with convertible ones, and the real answer is that the whole remaining conversion is blocked on that one feature. Decided against patching `chat` and `@chat-adapter/slack` for it: the change is small (two one-line runtime edits, `RadioSelect` whitelists the fields it forwards so an extra property is dropped, and the adapter's radio render omits `dispatch_action`), but every Mastra hunk we carry fixes something with no workaround, whereas this one has a working workaround already, and the version bump earlier today showed what a detached patch costs.

- **[Known issues] DONE (WP-D, db97ea51).** Re-verified 2026-09-24 against the tree. `lib/allowed-users.ts:40` `optInStatus` returns `'allowed' | 'not-allowed' | 'unknown'`; `unknown` gets an honest "couldn't check, try again" reply instead of the opt-in prompt. The boolean `isUserAllowed` (`:65`) is kept as a wrapper for its 3 other callers on purpose: converting them to the tri-state risked a silent-accept trap (treating `unknown` as allowed). Behaviour change: a non-opted-in @mention in a followed thread now gets the opt-in card. Original item: `isUserAllowed` cannot tell "not opted in" from "could not check", and the difference is user-visible. `src/mastra/lib/allowed-users.ts:20-23` catches every error and returns `false`, so a transient state-read failure presents to the user as the onboarding prompt telling them to accept terms in a channel they are already in. Observed 2026-09-12 on the dev instance: `createTables()` threw at `src/mastra/index.ts:83`, which is before `setMastra` and `initialize`, so `chat()` threw "Chat SDK is not initialized yet.", so every mention was answered with "you need to accept the terms". The startup cause is fixed, but the failure mode remains: any future error in that read locks out every user with a message that is actively wrong. Keep the fail-closed default, since an allowlist should deny on doubt, but make the caller distinguish the two so a failed check says something honest rather than sending people to a channel they have already joined.

- **[Known issues] DONE (WP-C, a9a65778).** Re-verified 2026-09-24 against the tree. Earlier mitigations (PROCESSOR_RUN/MODEL_STEP excluded, requestContext stripped from spans) plus new `observability/trim-payloads.ts` registered in `spanOutputProcessors`: it cuts only whitespace-free strings over 20k chars (base64 and data URLs), leaving prose intact. Rejected: lowering `serializationOptions.maxStringLength`, which would also cut prompts and messages. Original item: Traces for long agent runs reach 95 MB, and 16 of the 4339 prod traces could not be downloaded at all until the fetch timeout went past 60s. Measured 2026-09-12 on `1fe5cf1933085a7c75e9c3a63e38fde1` (6 minutes, 795 spans): `input` is 85.7 MB of the 95 MB, of which `input.messages` is 55.9 MB, `systemMessages` 14.4 MB, `prompt` 6.7 MB and `tools` 3.5 MB, and `requestContext` adds another 14.6 MB. It is not history growth, the largest span carries only 10 messages. It is duplication: 357 spans each re-serialize the whole `input.messages` array, and with reasoning parts running about 50 KB per assistant message that is roughly 190 KB per span. Every step processor span (`observational-memory`, `workspace-instructions`, `sandbox`) records the full input alongside every `llm:` span. The lever is `customSpanFormatter`, which `BaseExporter` in `@mastra/observability` applies before export and whose own doc comment demonstrates exactly this, trimming `span.input` before it leaves the process. `includeInternalSpans` is the blunter option. Ties into the observability decision in Architecture, since 95 MB spans are expensive in any backend.

- **[Known issues] DONE (WP-D, db97ea51).** Re-verified 2026-09-24 against the tree. `chat/attachments.ts` no longer calls inlined png/jpeg/webp/pdf "not downloaded". Attached files are still listed, deliberately: their Slack file ids are needed for sandbox work via `get_slack_file`. Live check (png attachment) in the owner checklist. Original item: Fix the misleading attachment text in `chat/attachments.ts`. Mastra already inlines png, jpeg, webp and pdf as viewable file parts before we see them, but `:11` overwrites the message with "Slack attachments, not downloaded yet:" and `:24` tells the model to call `get_slack_file`. So the model is told an image it can already see is undownloaded and routes itself through the workspace for no reason. Worth fixing on its own for the wasted round trip, independent of the vision bug.

(a) Both manifests subscribe `member_joined_channel` and `member_left_channel` (`slack-manifest*.json:95`); the adapter `processEventPayload` override adds `onMemberLeftChannel` (Chat SDK only had joined); writes are serialized and events arriving during the boot build are replayed; `setMembership` (`lib/allowed-users.ts:68`) replaces `addAllowedUser`. (c) Secret rotation stays in the OPS checklist. Remaining (left in TODO): an optional periodic rebuild. (3) Root cause confirmed on trace 2e4ed7bc: the root message was deleted, so Slack posted to the channel root; the `adapter.ts` postMessage override detects an unthreaded landing and `postBlocks` skips or deletes the footer; the reply itself cannot be moved back into a thread. (4) PREMISE FALSE: the first sighting of STOP inside the answer was a `read_conversation_history` read, not a second run; STOP's own turn was a handler call about 65s late. Fixed with thread-state `dropMessagesBefore` (`types/thread.ts:7`), set by `leave_thread` and `stopThread` and checked in `runTurn` (`chat/handlers.ts:126`). Remaining: messages Mastra queues during a running turn (WP-F adds an input processor). (5) `submit_feedback` kept by name (Q5), reshaped: score name `report`, value = kind, comment = body, unique id from `feedbackId`. (7) `score.create` inside the try, clicks without a trace id are logged, banned or unconfirmed clickers are ignored, `feedbackBlock` requires a trace id. (1) and (8) were logged earlier today. Left in TODO: (4) remainder and (6). Fix list: (1) and (8) done 2026-09-24, see IMPLEMENTED.md. Also check existing '(empty)' replies in thread history do not keep teaching the pattern. (4) NEW: a message sent mid-answer ('STOP') ran late as its own turn after `leave_thread` (f40057f7/88c9e932); drop queued messages for a thread after leave/stop; find why it ran both inside the running answer and again. (5) `submit_feedback` misshaped (free text as categorical value, same id overwrites).
