(6) Prompt: never state counts/rankings without checking tool results (trace 14a73e41 'slop', 12 vs 11 emoji); web builds must deliver inline screenshots, never a sandbox `localhost` link. (7) Silent drop paths: footer without trace id drops the rating unlogged; `score.create` outside the try. Thumbs click not gated by allow-list/bans.

- **[CRITICAL] DONE (WP-B, committed 424cfdf3, 2026-09-24). `processors/tool-media.ts` `relocateToolImages` now counts Slack uploads (inlined `data:` file parts) toward `maxContextImages`/`maxContextBytes` newest-first together with tool images; dropped ones are replaced by a note pointing at `view_image`/`get_slack_file`. Making oversized-image 400s terminal was investigated and REJECTED: `abort()` in `processAPIError` throws a TripWire that ends the run with no Slack reply and no `onError` sandbox pause, and the next rung may accept the payload (rungs differ in image and size limits). Reason recorded at `lib/error-handling.ts:28-33`. Live check still owed (images end to end, in the owner LIVE checklist).** Original item: **Image cap residual (WP-B).** The tool-result image cap is done (see IMPLEMENTED.md 2026-09-24). Still open: (a) extend `relocateToolImages` in `processors/tool-media.ts` so inline Slack-upload image `file` parts count toward `image.maxContextImages`/`maxContextBytes`, dropping the oldest first with a text note; (b) a `too_many_images` or request-too-large 400 gets 0 retries (`lib/error-handling.ts:32`) but still fails over through every ladder model, all of which reject the same payload. First check whether an error processor can stop ladder failover; if not, document why and leave failover alone.

- **[Known issues] DONE (WP-B, committed 424cfdf3, 2026-09-24). `tools/code-mode/slack.ts:62` uses `requireSandbox` (so `ensureRunning()` covers SandboxNotReadyError); code mode now refuses when no thread is bound instead of sharing the `__unscoped__` sandbox; `outcome.success === false` logs `logger.error` and marks the span with `currentSpan.error({ endSpan: false })` (Langfuse audit #12). Still open elsewhere: splitting the SandboxNotReadyError note out of mastra-ai/mastra#22197 upstream.** Original item: Code mode still calls `getSandbox` rather than `requireSandbox` (`tools/code-mode/slack.ts:60`), so it can hit `SandboxNotReadyError` on a sandbox that was never started; `requireSandbox` (`workspace/index.ts:40-52`) already calls `ensureRunning()`. Switch it, and log `outcome.success === false` at `logger.error`. WP-B.

- **[Known issues] DONE (WP-B, committed 424cfdf3, 2026-09-24). `toolCallConcurrency: { limit: 10, strategy: 'called' }` on orchestrator (`:137`), research (`:62`) and explore (`:59`), so a step serializes only when an approval tool is actually called (Mastra 1.69). Consequence: approval-false GitHub tools (e.g. `github_checkout` in a DM at `write`) can now run alongside `execute_command` in one step, which reopens part of the credential-window race; ties to the egress decision (Q3 `denyOut`).** Original item: `github_push_branch` being in the toolset forces sequential tool execution for every step of every turn once GitHub is connected. `effectiveToolSetRequiresSequentialExecution` (`@mastra/core/dist/agent-DSxJoGjY.js:24043`) drops concurrency from the default 10 to 1 when *any* tool in the active set carries `requireApproval`, whether or not it is called. That does close the credential-window race, but it also serialises unrelated sandbox, file, and search calls. Decide whether to accept the cost or gate the tool more narrowly.

- **[Known issues] DONE in the prompt only (WP-B, committed 424cfdf3, 2026-09-24). Premise partly wrong (Langfuse audit #10): `external_read_file` returns display text, there is no `.content` field. `prompts/features/code-mode.ts` now tells the model to parse files with `node:fs/promises` `readFile` inside the program and to view images with `view_image`; the base64 flattening itself is unchanged.** Original item: Code mode `external_read_file` (`tools/code-mode/slack.ts`) sends its return through `JSON.stringify`, so an image read there is flattened to base64 text. Tell the model in `prompts/features/code-mode.ts` to use `view_image` for images, and that `external_read_file` returns the read-tool result object, not raw text. WP-B.

- **[Known issues] DONE (WP-B, committed 424cfdf3, 2026-09-24). `chat/status/statuses.ts:223` matches `external_(\w+)\s*\(` in the `code` arg and lists the distinct function names.** Original item: `slack` (code mode) typing status is a flat "is working in Slack…" regardless of what the generated code actually does. Its input is `{ code: string }` (arbitrary TypeScript calling `external_*` functions), so there's no single field to show like `call_slack_api`'s `method`. Cheap fix: regex the `code` arg for `external_([a-zA-Z0-9_]+)\(` calls and list the distinct function names in the status, same non-invasive pattern already used for `grep`'s `pattern`.

- **[Audit before anything else] Backlog triage and owner decisions (2026-09-24).** Every open TODO item was triaged against the tree (plan in the session scratchpad `backlog-plan.md`) into DONE, STALE, BUILD, DECIDE, LIVE, OPS and UPSTREAM, then re-verified after a04990e. The owner accepted the 12 recommended defaults with revisions: Q5 keeps `submit_feedback` with no ask-before-filing; Q6 also shows remaining turns in App Home; Q9 (models, image generation, BYOK, per-agent split, model router) skipped entirely, those items left open; Q10 topic summaries as a `topic-summaries` runtime skill and no thinking display; Q11 defers (left open) instead of closing, and the Legacy Gorkie auto-add is pending an explanation. The decisions are recorded in TODO.md under "Owner decisions". The entries below are the items this closed.

- **[CRITICAL] DONE, partly.** Re-verified 2026-09-24 against the tree at a04990e. `processors/tool-media.ts:47-62` keeps the newest images within `config.image.maxContextImages` (8) and `maxContextBytes` (60 MiB, `config.ts:37-38`), dropping older ones. A bad-request 400 now gets 0 retries (`lib/error-handling.ts:32`, "A 400 is deterministic, so fall through to the next model rather than retry"). Left in TODO: inline Slack-upload file parts are not counted toward the cap, and a `too_many_images` 400 still fails over through the whole ladder. Original item: **Cap images sent to the model, GLM rejects >8 inline / >64 MiB.** Live failure `trace_id ff8dd4c72b5f6ec373e7ac758021f7ca` / `runId ce485f75-ce39-4dbc-a478-ba733684d03f` on `opencode-go/glm-5.3-flash`: `AI_APICallError` 400 `[too_many_images]` ("at most 8 inline PNG/JPEG/WEBP/GIF ... 16 MiB each ... 64 MiB total"), `isRetryable: false`. Root cause: a thread that views many images (the ~50-frame OCR thread) piles them all into context, and `processors/tool-media.ts` `moveToolImages` relocates EVERY tool-result image into one user message with NO count or byte cap, so it blows past GLM's limit. `view_image` (added 2026-09-13) makes this easier to hit. There is NO Mastra built-in image-limiting processor (`maxImagesPerCall` is model metadata only, never enforced by trimming), so the cap must be ours: keep the most-recent N image parts (<=8) within a byte budget (~64 MiB, ideally per-model from the model's `maxImagesPerCall`), dropping older ones with a text note ("N earlier images omitted"). Enforce client-side: the 400 is non-retryable, so failover can't save it. Secondary: `lib/error-handling.ts` currently retries a bad-request 400 once (`isBadRequestError -> maxRetries: 1`) AND the ladder fails over across models, all of which reject the same oversized payload, ~30s + quota wasted per occurrence; consider treating an oversized-image/oversized-request 400 as terminal (0 retries, no failover) once the cap is in.

- **[Simplification] Decided not to build (2026-09-24).** Owner decision Q2 (2026-09-24): keep the `@github-tools/sdk` tools and the firewall-injected credential. No `gh` CLI and no session-long `GH_TOKEN` in the sandbox, because that widens the credential from one brokered push to the whole session. The tool-prompt cost is addressed by putting GitHub tools behind `search_tools` instead (still open). Original item: Decide whether to drop the enumerated GitHub tool surface for the `gh` CLI, the way Mastra's own Factory does. Read from a sparse clone of `mastra-ai/mastra` on 2026-09-11 and spot-verified: it does not depend on `@github-tools/sdk` at all, has no allowlist, no preset, and no read/write classification of GitHub operations. Its entire GitHub tool registration is four tools in `mastracode/factory/src/integrations/github/session-subscriptions.ts:221-263`, each existing only because the CLI cannot do that job: refresh the credential, write a marker-guarded idempotent comment, subscribe and unsubscribe a PR. Everything else is `gh issue view`, `gh pr diff`, `gh api graphql` written as shell in skill markdown. The authorization boundary is the token's own GitHub-side permissions, not a name list, so adding a capability is zero code and the list cannot drift. Read/write separation is by module (`supervisor/read-tools.ts` has no `requireApproval`, `write-tools.ts` sets it on all seven), so the file boundary is the classification. The cost is the thing to weigh: this only works because a session-long `GH_TOKEN` lives in the sandbox env, which is a real widening of our current position, where a credential reaches the sandbox only for the duration of one brokered push.

- **[Simplification] Decided not to build (2026-09-24).** Owner decision Q2 (2026-09-24): closed. The firewall header injection in `tools/github/git.ts` already keeps the token out of the VM entirely, which is stronger than any of Factory's four lifetimes, and the strict drop (item "Make the credential drop strict") is done. Original item: Steal Factory's four git credential lifetimes rather than its tool design, since these are portable even if the `gh` CLI question above is answered no. One, clone puts the token in the remote URL then resets it, and a leftover from a previous failed scrub is detected on every start with `if (/\/\/[^/]*@/.test(existing))` (`integrations/github/sandbox.ts:292-294`). Two, steady-state git auth is `credential.helper = '!gh auth git-credential'` (`:370`), which stores nothing on disk because git asks `gh`, which answers from the env. Three, `withInstallToken` (`:702`) is our `github_push_branch` broker as a named reusable primitive, scrubbing on both the success and failure paths. Four, the E2B template build passes `$GH_TOKEN` by reference, expanded in the build shell, so the stored layer command holds the variable name and never the value (`workspaces/e2b/src/utils/repo-template.ts:388-396`).

- **[Simplification] DONE.** Re-verified 2026-09-24 against the tree at a04990e. `tools/github/git.ts` retries the credential drop 3 times (`:115`), then kills the sandbox (`:126`) and throws a token-free error, instead of logging and swallowing. Two-path shape as proposed: strict once the credential was installed. Original item: Make the credential drop in `withCredential` (`tools/github/git.ts`, the `finally` in `withCredential`; overlaps the credential-window item below) strict, the way Factory's `scrubRemote` is. Ours logs and swallows: `.catch((error) => logger.error('[github] failed to drop the credential'))`. If `updateNetwork` fails to restore `baseRules()`, the firewall keeps injecting the `Authorization` header for that sandbox, so anything running in it can reach github.com authenticated until the sandbox dies, and nobody is told. Factory's equivalent throws on a failed scrub and appends it to the primary error rather than replacing it (`mastracode/factory/src/integrations/github/sandbox.ts:545-574`, plus `scrubbedFailure` at `:576-601`), so the caller keeps the error it would have had and the leak warning rides along. Worth copying that two-path shape: best-effort when the credential never got installed, strict once it did.

- **[Simplification] STALE.** Re-verified 2026-09-24 against the tree at a04990e. Redone in the 2026-09-23 cleanup steps 3-6. The named symbols (`MAX_MESSAGES`, `MAX_SCANNED`, `MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES`, `lib/version.ts`, `github/actions.ts`) no longer exist; the two safety reshapes (path-traversal refine, credential boundary) were handled by the step 5 git hardening. Original item: Finish the `/no-comments` pass started 2026-09-12. The deletion half landed: 83 comment lines went from `src/` and `scripts/`, taking the tree from 121 comment-bearing lines to 38, and `bun run typecheck` plus `bun run check` are clean. The reshape half did not, because the skill failed its own audit gate at step 2 after two rejected reports, so the named surprises are now undocumented *and* unfixed. That is the worst of both states and is why this is logged rather than left. The seventeen reshapes the deleted comments were standing in for, each one a name that lies about what the code does: `excludeSpanTypes` as a bare literal in `index.ts`; `MAX_MESSAGES` and `MAX_SCANNED` in `chat/history.ts`, where one is a yield cap and the other a walk budget and the names do not say so; the 1M-context invariant in `providers.ts` living as prose rather than a type or an assert; `usedSandbox` in `workspace/index.ts`, a flag that three tools never set and that `processors/sandbox.ts` patches with a hardcoded name list; `getSandbox` returning paused sandboxes, which is the only reason `requireSandbox` exists; `clearSandboxCache` over an unbounded `Map` whose eviction is a caller's duty; the order-dependent `matchers` array in `lib/error-handling.ts` whose ordering is load-bearing and untyped; `settled` in `app-home/view.ts`, which means "never throws, logs and returns undefined"; `viewOf`/`ViewTarget` in `github/views/shared.ts` and `polling[].viewId` in `github/actions.ts`, neither of which says the payload carries Slack's concurrency hash; `removeGitHubCredential` in `settings-actions.ts`, which should clear settings itself rather than being paired by a caller, since reconnecting can be a different account that never agreed to the last one's permissions; `repositorySchema` in `tools/github/git.ts`, where the `.` and `..` refine is path-traversal defense wearing a validation error message; `withCredential` in the same file, whose name says nothing about the CLAUDE.md boundary that the token never enters the VM; `MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES` in `slack/upload-file.ts`, which should be `SLACK_MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES` so the 1GB stops looking arbitrary; the inline `id` template in `observability/langfuse-feedback.ts`, which is an upsert key and should be a named derivation; `observeAttachments: ['image/*']` in `orchestrator.ts`, a token-budget decision as a bare literal; and `git()` in `lib/version.ts`, a helper that swallows every failure into `undefined`. Two of these are safety, not tidiness, and should go first: the path-traversal refine and the credential boundary, both in `tools/github/git.ts`. Decide before starting whether to keep the deletions at all: reverting is one `git checkout` of the 17 source files, and the argument for reverting is that a deleted comment on an unfixed surprise is a net loss.

- **[Simplification] DONE.** Re-verified 2026-09-24 against the tree at a04990e. The lessons are already written in the item itself (Comment Sicko edited app code, invented a comment, ran `git add`, moved open items into IMPLEMENTED.md; guards: working tree is not its scratch space, no `git add`, no IMPLEMENTED.md writes, verify via `git show HEAD:<file>`). Recording it here is the whole job. Original item: Record what the `/no-comments` run cost in process terms, so the next run is cheaper. Comment Sicko was rejected twice on the same pass. It edited application code (`config.ts` `agent.maxTokens.input` from `200_000` to `1_000_000`), which contradicted the context-window decision written in this file on the same day; it reported killing a 15-line comment at `config.ts:9-23` that never existed in HEAD; it ran `git add` across the tree, then read the resulting empty `git diff` and concluded a third party was reverting its work, which produced a false "two things happened under me" section blaming edits on the caller; and it moved nine open `- [ ]` items out of this file into `IMPLEMENTED.md`, recording unfinished work as done. All of it was reverted. The cheap guards for next time: tell it up front that the working tree is not its scratch space, that `git add` is not its to run, and that `IMPLEMENTED.md` is not a place it writes. Verify its claims against `git show HEAD:<file>` rather than its own diff reads, since its diff reads were the thing that failed.

Not a duplicate: it is the same skills with `name` and `disable-model-invocation` stripped from the frontmatter, a variant for agents that do not support those fields. - **[Known issues] DONE (residual goes to the egress decision).** Re-verified 2026-09-24 against the tree at a04990e. Per-sandbox mutex `oneWindowPerSandbox` over a `credentialWindows` Map in `tools/github/git.ts:48-73`, fatal teardown (3 retries, then kill the sandbox and throw) at `:115-138`. Approval tools force sequential steps. What is left, a concurrent unapproved command or a leftover background process using the github.com header transform during the window, belongs to the sandbox egress question (Q3), not to this item. Original item: Scope the GitHub credential window and stop swallowing a failed teardown. Overlaps the existing `withCredential` item under Simplification, but the approval-bypass half is new. `tools/github/git.ts:60-98` injects `Authorization: Basic x-access-token:<token>` at the E2B firewall for every request to github.com from the whole sandbox for the duration of `operation()`. Workspace tools default to `requireApproval: false` and the repo sets no override, and in a DM at the default `write` level `github_checkout` is unapproved too, so a step pairing a long clone with a concurrent `execute_command` gets an authenticated `git push` or `curl` against github.com inside the window, skipping both the approval on `github_push_branch` and its main/master refusal (`push.ts:26-28`). The teardown only logs on failure, and the sandbox is keyed on `threadId` and survives pause/resume, so one failed `updateNetwork` leaves the token injected into other people's turns in a `githubThreads` thread. Wants a per-sandbox mutex around `withCredential`, a refusal to open a window while another call is in flight in that sandbox, and a fatal path on failed reset that kills the sandbox rather than logging.

- **[Known issues] DONE (4fd70ce).** Re-verified 2026-09-24 against the tree at a04990e. Exported `confinePath` in `workspace/filesystem.ts:45`, shared by the workspace file tools and `upload_emoji`, `view_image`, `generate-image/request.ts` (reference read) and `slack/upload-file.ts`. Absolute paths outside `/home/user` are now refused, which matters most for `upload_file` (it was an out-of-sandbox read of any VM file). Known limit, accepted: symlinks inside `/home/user` and `execute_command` can still reach outside, which is fine because everything is inside the sandbox anyway. Original item: Route the remaining direct `sandbox.e2b.files` reads through `E2BFilesystem.resolve()`. `tools/upload-emoji.ts:68-70`, `tools/slack/upload-file.ts:71,103` and `tools/generate-image/edit.ts:16-20` pass a model-supplied path straight to `files.read`/`getInfo`, skipping the only thing that confines paths to `/home/user` (`workspace/filesystem.ts:403-415`, which is itself correct: it normalizes before comparing and requires an exact match or a `basePath + '/'` prefix, so there is no `/home/user-evil` bypass). Contained to the sandbox, which holds no real secrets, so this is defence in depth rather than a break, but `upload_file` turns it into an out-of-sandbox read of any file in the VM.

Confirm in order: a trace arrives at all; `sessionId` groups every turn of a Slack thread into one session, which is the thing that replaced the trace viewer, so paste a thread id like `slack:C0B9M2S2LSU:1789199712.114669` into Langfuse and expect the whole conversation; `userId` and `channelId` filter correctly; `environment` separates `bun dev` traffic from production, since both use the same keys and polluting prod is the easy mistake here.

- **[Known issues] DONE.** Re-verified 2026-09-24 against the tree at a04990e. Feedback persists: Langfuse scores via `LangfuseFeedbackExporter`, confirmed by the 2026-09-24 user feedback audit (TODO item, part 8). The id is `${traceId}:${user}:${feedbackType}` (`observability/langfuse-feedback.ts:31`), so a re-click overwrites rather than duplicates, which answers "has this user already rated this message" without a Postgres table. Original item: Test feedback against Langfuse and confirm it persists. Feedback is currently broken in the way `TODO.md` already records: it never reaches Mastra Platform, is not deduplicated, and `listFeedback` reads local storage that production never writes. Langfuse has a Scores API and `BaseExporter` exposes `addScoreToTrace`, so a thumbs up or down should land as a score on the trace and survive a restart. The specific thing to prove is the one no current store can answer: has this user already rated this message. If scores carry an idempotency key this removes the need for the bespoke Postgres table that was otherwise required.

- **[Known issues] DONE.** Re-verified 2026-09-24 against the tree at a04990e. `processors/step-guard.ts` strips `<think>`/`<annotation>` blocks and leaked tool markup in `processOutputStream` and `processOutputResult`, and is the first output processor on all three agents (`orchestrator.ts:174`, `research.ts:63`, `explore.ts:60`). This is the "strip in an output processor instead" branch; `extractReasoningMiddleware` was not restored because the opencode rung is a router string. Original item: Reopen and fix issue 4: the `<think>` leak guard is a regression, not a closed bug. Verified 2026-09-11. `d979755` ("bork-ify gorkie", 2026-08-16) deleted `import { extractReasoningMiddleware, wrapLanguageModel } from 'ai'` and the wrap around the opencode route when it moved to Mastra's string model router. `grep -rn "extractReasoning\|wrapLanguageModel" src/` returns nothing today. The guard had already been added twice, in `d59e9f1` and again in `7e8d2a4`, so this is its third removal. Nothing upstream covers it: the only `<think>` strip in `@mastra/core@1.66.0` is inside `generateTitleFromUserMessage`, which is thread titles, not the reply stream. The specific model in the original report is no longer configured, but all four opencode models now in the chain are the same class of inline-reasoning model on the same unwrapped transport, so reasoning can leak into Slack replies. The open question that stopped me applying the fix: restoring the wrap needs `model` to be a wrapped `LanguageModel` instance rather than the `opencode-go/<id>` router string, and I could not determine from the source whether `ModelWithRetries.headers` still applies in that case. That matters because the `x-opencode-session` per-thread header just landed in the merge and would be lost if it does not. Settle that first, or strip in an output processor instead.

Two of its three claims are wrong: it validated against `https://opencode.ai/zen/v1/models`, but `opencode-go/<id>` resolves to `https://opencode.ai/zen/go/v1` (`provider-registry.json`), and the two catalogs do not overlap. - **[Known issues] DONE.** Re-verified 2026-09-24 against the tree at a04990e. `prompts/github.ts` is 25 lines with one path (disconnected vs connected). The PAT path was dropped by migration `20260924043047_drop_github_classic_tokens`, so there is no `app`/`pat` branch and no fork tool advice left to describe. Original item: Collapse the GitHub prompt down to one flow. The surface now branches on credential kind (`app` or `pat`), on location (DM, shared thread, shared thread with `github_threads` on), and on approval preset, and the prompt carries a paragraph for most combinations. The combinations are not equivalent either: `github_fork_repository` works with a classic token and is structurally impossible through the App outside the repositories it is installed on, so the model is told about a tool that cannot work for half the people who see it. - **[Known issues] DONE (interim).** Re-verified 2026-09-24 against the tree at a04990e. `prompts/github.ts:24` says "Every github_ tool is already loaded; do not search for one". The structural fix (GitHub behind `search_tools`) stays open in TODO under the tool-surface items (Q2). Original item: `search_tools` never returns `github_*`, so gorkie repeatedly told people the tools were unavailable and then called them successfully in the same thread. The tools come from the dynamic `tools` function while `ToolSearchProcessor` only indexes the static `deferredTools`, so they are always loaded and never findable. Same root cause as the `includeResolvedTools` item under Ideas. Until that is resolved, the prompt should tell the model that `github_*` tools are always present when GitHub is connected and never need searching for.

- **[Known issues] DONE, partly.** Re-verified 2026-09-24 against the tree at a04990e. `requireSandbox` calls `sandbox.ensureRunning()` before use (`workspace/index.ts:40-52`), which covers SandboxNotReadyError for every caller that goes through it. Code mode still calls `getSandbox` (`tools/code-mode/slack.ts:60`), so that residual stays in TODO (WP-B). Original item: `retryOnDead` cannot recover a `SandboxNotReadyError` (noted under Related on mastra-ai/mastra#22197, not yet split out). `get e2b()` (`:730`) throws `SandboxNotReadyError` ("Sandbox is not ready: <id>") whenever `_sandbox` is unset, and that matches no branch of `isSandboxDeadError`, patched or not. So the retry helps a sandbox that died remotely but not one that was never started; only calling `ensureRunning()` before touching `.e2b` covers that, and every caller has to know the distinction.

- **[Known issues] DONE.** Re-verified 2026-09-24 against the tree at a04990e. `agents/orchestrator.ts` sets `onAbort` (`:135`) and `onError` (`:151`) and both call `pauseSandbox` (`workspace/index.ts`), so a thrown or aborted turn no longer leaves the sandbox running to its timeout. Original item: `processOutputResult` does not run on a thrown or aborted turn, so `sandbox.ts`, `clear-status.ts`, and `turn-footer.ts` all leak on those paths: the sandbox is never paused, the Slack status stays stuck, and no footer posts. Corrected 2026-09-12 on two counts before anyone acts on it. There is no `clear-status.ts`: `src/mastra/processors/` holds `delegated-tools.ts`, `sandbox.ts`, `tool-media.ts`, `turn-footer.ts` and `working-model.ts`, and typing status is channels' own `typingStatus` rather than a processor of ours, so whether it clears on a failed turn is channels' behaviour to check, not ours to fix here. And the claimed precedent does not exist either: `onError` and `onAbort` appear nowhere in `src/`, so focus mode is not already doing this. That leaves one processor that genuinely leaks something expensive, `sandbox.ts`, whose `processOutputResult` is the only thing that calls `e2b.pause()` and `clearSandboxCache(threadId)`; skip it and the sandbox runs on until its own 8 minute timeout. `turn-footer.ts` not posting on a failed turn is arguably correct, since the adapter's `formatError` already posts the error. The fix shape is real though. `onError` and `onAbort` are valid execution options (`loop/types.d.ts:151`, surfaced at `agent/agent.types.d.ts:457`), and neither callback receives a request context (`{ error }` and `{ steps, text }` respectively), which would normally make them useless for per-thread cleanup. The way around it is that `defaultOptions` is typed `DynamicArgument<AgentExecutionOptions<TOutput>, TRequestContext>` (`agent/types.d.ts:620`), so it can be a function of `{ requestContext }` and the hooks can close over the right context per run. Two things to settle while implementing: confirm the hooks fire for a turn that throws rather than only one whose stream errors, and make the pause idempotent, since on a healthy turn `processOutputResult` already did it.

Checking the real rows in prod is an owner step. Confirm nothing reads the column outside those helpers, and consider a one-off backfill so no plaintext survives.

- **[Known issues] STALE.** Re-verified 2026-09-24 against the tree at a04990e. The ladder is glm-5.3-flash on two routes plus deepseek-v4-flash-vision-exp (`providers.ts`), all vision-capable; `ox-alpha-free` is gone, and the working-model pin is 5 minutes and never renewed (`lib/working-model.ts:42` `setIfNotExists`, `config.ts:60`). Original item: Gate image turns to vision-capable routes. Still the best explanation for broken vision, but narrower than first written, and one claim in the earlier version was wrong. Corrected 2026-09-11: `muse-spark-1.2-contributor` does have vision, so `explorerModels` is fine and the only route in any list without it is `opencode-go/ox-alpha-free`, which sits at position 3 in orchestrator, scout and explorer and is also listed under `deprecatedModels` in the provider registry. Treat the earlier "neither advertises vision" claim as unsound: it was inferred from model naming, not from capability data, and `provider-registry.json` stores the `opencode-go` models as a bare name list with no capability metadata, so nothing here is statically answerable. The mechanism still holds and still fits direct uploads failing: `preferLastWorking` (`providers.ts:30-47`) hoists whichever model last answered to the front for 30 minutes (`config.ts:18`), so a text-only turn landing on `ox-alpha-free` pins the next half hour of image turns to it, and an inlined file part on a user message needs the same capability a tool-result media part does. Owner's call is to leave `providers.ts` alone, so the fix is capability-aware routing at send time, or dropping `ox-alpha-free`, not reshuffling the lists. Confirm first by correlating a failing image turn against the model that served it: `workingModel` persists it, and `processors/working-model.ts` reads `args.result.steps.at(-1)?.response.modelId`.

- **[Known issues] DONE, partly.** Re-verified 2026-09-24 against the tree at a04990e. `read_file` `mediaTypes` is now `['application/pdf']` only (`workspace/index.ts:142`), images go through `view_image`, which types by magic bytes. The code-mode `external_read_file` flattening residual stays in TODO (WP-B). Original item: Two smaller image traps, both independent of the routing bug above. ~~`workspace/index.ts:80` registers `read_file` with no `mediaTypes` override, so the default png/jpeg/webp/pdf applies and a GIF, SVG or HEIC comes back as metadata-only text.~~ **Half fixed, half wrong, 2026-09-12.** `mediaTypes` is now set explicitly and `image/gif` added, which is the only one of the three that vision providers actually accept. Deliberately not `image/*`: the comment above `DEFAULT_MEDIA_TYPES` (`workspace-Ds7WdjRA.js:9548`) says the default is narrow because the rest are types "that some providers reject", so a glob would hand HEIC, TIFF and BMP to providers that refuse them and turn a metadata-only read into a failed turn. HEIC therefore still returns metadata only, correctly, and wants conversion in the sandbox rather than a config change. The SVG claim was simply wrong: `isTextLikeMimeType` returns true for anything ending `+xml` (`:9578`), and SVG is `image/svg+xml`, so it already falls through to the utf-8 path and the model gets readable source, which is better than an image part would be. Also worth knowing for later: `maxMediaBytes` defaults to 10 MB and a larger image silently degrades to a metadata string. And code mode's `external_read_file` (`tools/code-mode/slack.ts`) sends its return through `JSON.stringify`, so an image read there is flattened to base64 text unconditionally.

The owner's preference is stronger than a refactor: remove `call_slack_api` entirely, add `uploadEmoji`, then reintroduce raw API access only once it is properly secured.

- **[Known issues] STALE.** Re-verified 2026-09-24 against the tree at a04990e. Luna is not in any model ladder, so the concatenated-tool-JSON quirk no longer reaches gorkie. Original item: `openai/gpt-5.6-luna` occasionally streams two complete tool calls' JSON concatenated under one tool-call ID (e.g. an `execute_command` call and a `read_file` call glued together with no separator), which Mastra's `tryRepairJson` (`node_modules/@mastra/core/dist/stream-BZ38co-u.js`/`.cjs`; note the fallback-escalation patch lives in the `agent-*` bundles, not this one) can't recover since it only handles single-object malformations (missing quotes, trailing commas, etc.), not multiple concatenated objects. Args come back `undefined`, the tool call fails validation, and the resulting broken message in history can trigger a `400 Bad Request` from whichever provider gets the next request. Same class of provider quirk as the upstream-documented Kimi/K2 issue (`mastra-ai/mastra#11078`). Fix: extend `tryRepairJson` with one more heuristic that detects a `}{` boundary at brace-depth 0 outside strings and keeps just the first balanced object, recovering one tool call instead of losing both.

- **[Known issues] DONE.** Re-verified 2026-09-24 against the tree at a04990e. `chat/history.ts:26` stops scanning at 30 messages and `:58` keeps 10 lines, with a truncation note pointing at `read_conversation_history`. Both numbers settled as the item proposed (lower the walk budget, keep the prefill). Original item: Decide the thread backfill numbers in `chat/history.ts`. `MAX_MESSAGES = 10` is the real prefill, the count of history lines actually injected. `MAX_SCANNED = 200` is only a walk budget over `thread.messages` and never puts 200 messages in context. The two interact in a way that is easy to misread: the loop increments `scanned` for every message it looks at but only counts a message toward `MAX_MESSAGES` when it is not gorkie's own (`previous.author.isMe`) and not a `##` side comment, so in a busy thread where most messages are the bot's the scan can genuinely run to 200 Slack messages to find 10 quotable ones. That is pagination cost, not context cost. Lowering `MAX_SCANNED` to around 30 bounds the Slack work; raising `MAX_MESSAGES` is the separate lever if the agent actually needs more backfill.

- **[Known issues] Decided not to build (2026-09-24).** Owner decision Q4 (2026-09-24): keep the in-process 5-minute non-renewing working-model pin as is, no persistence. The type cast is already gone (`providers.ts:19`), so nothing else in this item is wanted. Original item: Clean up `providers.ts`, which is now mostly machinery for a cache that does not persist. Two concrete things. First, `model: \`opencode-go/${modelId}\` as const` on line 14 is a type cast used to satisfy TypeScript, which `CODING_STANDARDS.md` forbids outright; find the typed constructor or widen the parameter instead of asserting. Second, `modelSlug` and `preferLastWorking` are 38 lines whose only purpose is reordering a ladder from `recallModel`, and the existing entry on the working-model cache establishes that `chat().getState()` resolves to `MastraStateAdapter`, whose `get`/`set` are an in-process `Map`, so the value never survives a restart and the 30 minute TTL is bounded by process lifetime. If that cache is deleted rather than moved to real storage, `providers.ts` loses both functions, the `recallModel`/`slugOf` import, and drops to roughly 40 lines of ladder definitions. Do not do this one in isolation, it is the same decision as the working-model entry and should be settled once.

- **[Known issues] STALE.** Re-verified 2026-09-24 against the tree at a04990e. `postbuild` now runs `bun install` in `.mastra/output` (`package.json:23`), so the npm lockfile timeout warning is cosmetic. Closed. Original item: The build's "Failed to generate package-lock.json" warning is a false negative and the lockfile it claims to have failed at is sitting right there. Diagnosed 2026-09-12. `@mastra/deployer` runs `execSync("npm install --package-lock-only --force", { cwd: outputDir, stdio: "pipe", timeout: 6e4 })` at `dist/bundler/index.js:261-265` and swallows the result in a bare `catch {}`, so with `stdio: 'pipe'` npm's own output is never shown and every cause prints the same sentence. The build log's elapsed time is 60.06s (`18:00:19.914` to `18:01:19.974`), the 60,000 ms timeout to the millisecond, not a resolution error. `.mastra/output/package-lock.json` is present at 270,566 bytes with `lockfileVersion: 3` and a complete packages map, so npm finished writing it and was then SIGTERMed before exiting, most likely during the audit request that follows every `npm install`. Reproduced the exact command twice against the real output `package.json`: 7.8s in a clean directory and 8.1s with a 619 MB `node_modules.__tmp` alongside it to mimic the move the deployer performs first. So it is not `patchedDependencies` (npm ignores the field), not the copied `patches/`, not the moved `node_modules`, and not a stray `.npmrc`, there is none at the repo root. That leaves host I/O contention at build time, for which the entry below is a concrete candidate. If it recurs the fix is ours: generate the lockfile in `scripts/postbuild.ts` where the timeout is ours to set, or upstream a patch making the 60s configurable and the error visible.

- **[Architecture] STALE.** Re-verified 2026-09-24 against the tree at a04990e. Webhook mode replaced Socket Mode (nothing connects at import) and `chat/instance.ts` is gone. Original item: Two parts of the above deliberately left alone, decide whether they are worth doing. First, `chat/client.ts` still constructs `SlackAgentAdapter` at import. Socket Mode connects on `initialize()` rather than in the constructor, so this costs an env read and no I/O, and making it lazy means rewriting 39 `slack.` call sites on a live bot for little gain. The second part, `chat/instance.ts` throwing rather than holding a test adapter, is moot: the file was removed in the desloppify pass (2026-09-23), since nothing read it. The lazy adapter becomes worth doing the moment a first test exists.

- **[Architecture] DONE.** Re-verified 2026-09-24 against the tree at a04990e. Tracing is Langfuse only (`@mastra/langfuse`, `src/mastra/index.ts:165-167`), plus local DuckDB in dev. Langfuse Cloud was chosen over self-hosting. Original item: Decide whether to move tracing off Mastra Platform. Verified 2026-09-12: `MastraPlatformExporter` is not OpenTelemetry, it speaks Mastra's own wire format, and `@mastra/observability` ships no outbound OTel code at all. The only OTLP in the package is `dist/client/otlp.d.ts`, an inbound decoder for browser-side spans from `@mastra/client-js`. Switching sinks does not mean leaving Mastra: `exporters` in `src/mastra/index.ts:59` is an array, so sinks can run side by side and be compared before one is dropped. Real OTel is `@mastra/otel-exporter` (`OtelExporter`; Dash0, SigNoz, New Relic, Traceloop, Laminar, or a custom endpoint over http/json, http/protobuf, grpc, zipkin). Langfuse is `@mastra/langfuse` (`LangfuseExporter`, self-hostable via `baseUrl`). Neither is installed. Dropping Platform also means relaxing `MASTRA_PLATFORM_ACCESS_TOKEN` and `MASTRA_PROJECT_ID` from `.min(1)` to optional in `src/env.ts:25-26` and rewriting the observability paragraph in `.claude/CLAUDE.md`. Decided 2026-09-12, research only, nothing installed and no dependency added: Langfuse is the direction if we move. Chosen over `@mastra/otel-exporter` because generic OTel backends render an agent run as a span waterfall, which is the right shape for latency work and the wrong shape for reading a Slack thread back as a conversation, and because self-hosting would put traces in a database we can query with SQL instead of pulling them one at a time through `mastra api trace get`. Self-hosted costs us a ClickHouse, a Postgres and an ingestion worker to operate; Langfuse Cloud gives the same UI and SDK export without the ops but without direct SQL. Not yet decided which of the two. Costed 2026-09-12 against real volume: 4434 traces in 31 days, mean 28.4 spans, so about **126k billable events a month**. `processor_run` is **66.6% of the event count**, so excluding those spans takes it to ~42k. Mastra Starter is free to 100k events with **15 day** retention, then $10/100k; Mastra Teams is $250/mo for 1M events and 6 months. Langfuse Hobby is free to 50k units with 30 days; Core is $29/mo for 100k units and **90 days**, then $8/100k; self-hosted is unlimited and free but costs a ClickHouse, a Postgres, a Redis, an S3 bucket and two app containers to run. At today's volume the money is nearly identical ($10 vs ~$31) and **retention is the real difference**: 15 days against 90. Note the downloaded corpus spans 31 days, which is longer than the stated free-tier retention, so the current plan is not confirmed. The other decisive gap is the API: Mastra's trace list filters on `entityId` only and ignores `resourceId`, `spanType`, `status` and `name`, so the trace viewer has to pull the whole index and search locally. Langfuse has server-side full-text search over inputs, outputs and metadata plus structured filters and cursor pagination, which would delete the sync engine entirely.

- **[Architecture] STALE.** Re-verified 2026-09-24 against the tree at a04990e. Superseded by the owner ruling on resource-scoped, preferences-only working memory (`memory/profile.ts`, orchestrator `workingMemory`), 2026-09-23. Original item: Build the per-user profile on `user_settings`, not on Mastra working memory. Schema change, so ask before doing it. The shape that works given the frozen-`resourceId` finding above: a second text column next to `user_settings.instructions`, keyed the same way, read in the orchestrator's `instructions` function through `channelContext(requestContext).userId`, which is the live speaker on every turn rather than the thread owner. Two slots with different owners, both injected: `instructions` stays human-authored and the agent never edits it, the new column is agent-maintained and the human can view, edit or clear it in App Home. Keep it prose, not a Zod schema, precisely because it has to be editable: a template stores a markdown string, so the App Home editor is the existing plain multiline input from `chat/app-home/instructions/`, while a schema stores JSON and would need a field-per-input form or a JSON textarea. The agent's write path should be a tool rather than an OM `Extractor`: an extractor runs over a batch spanning several speakers and has no way to say which one a fact belongs to, whereas a tool call happens inside the current turn where `requestContext` already names the speaker.

- **[Architecture] DONE.** Re-verified 2026-09-24 against the tree at a04990e. The 1M-input rule is in CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md Boundaries; `config.ts:43` `maxTokens: { input: 1_000_000, output: 65_536 }`; `messageHistory: { maxTokens: 200_000 }` (`orchestrator.ts:205`) is the real budget guard; the OM summarizer overrides `maxOutputTokens` (`orchestrator.ts:233,239`) to stay under 65,536. Original item: Settle the context-window policy, which is two different numbers that keep getting conflated. Verified against models.dev on 2026-09-12, every model in `providers.ts` is at or above 1M context: `opencode-go/glm-5.3-flash` 1,000,000/131,072, `opencode-go/deepseek-v4-flash-vision-exp` 1,000,000/384,000, `opencode-go/muse-spark-1.3-contributor` 1,048,576/131,072, `opencode-go/mimo-v2.5` 1,000,000/128,000, `openrouter/z-ai/glm-5.3-flash` 1,310,720/131,072, `openrouter/google/gemini-3.5-flash-lite` 1,048,576/65,536. The floor is exactly 1,000,000 in, 65,536 out. That makes "every model must be a 1M model" a real and currently satisfied rule worth writing into `AGENTS.md`. It does not follow that `agent.maxTokens.input` should become 1M: that value feeds `TokenLimiterProcessor`, which is a cost, latency and resident-memory ceiling rather than a model capability, and Mastra's own guidance is to "add `TokenLimiter` as a final budget guard rather than relying on the model's maximum context window". Raising it to 1M deletes the guard on a bot that was OOM-killed in production on 2026-09-12, and OM already holds steady state near 30k, so the limiter only ever fires on a pathological turn. Note the output floor too: `maxTokens.output` is 32,768 and `gemini-3.5-flash-lite` caps at 65,536, so the OM `modelSettings.maxOutputTokens` override exists to stay under that and must not be raised to Mastra's 100,000 default.

- **[Ideas] Built and reverted (2026-09-24).** Tried and reverted (4fd70ce built it, 6c5a59a reverted it by owner decision). The build reserved `.artifacts/` for `save_artifact` with a guard on general writes. Reverted because nothing in the sandbox needs reserving: skills load on the host (`LocalSkillSource` at `PROJECT_ROOT/workspace/skills`), and GitHub checkouts are meant to be edited by the agent. Do not re-propose without a new sandbox-resident namespace that must be protected. Original item: Steal eve's reserved-namespace registry (`agent/lib/blob.ts`). One module lists every reserved storage prefix with the tools that own it, and a single guard refuses any general-purpose write that lands inside one, quoting the owning tool in the refusal. gorkie has the same shape of problem in the sandbox filesystem: nothing stops a model-supplied path from overwriting a skill, a checkout, or another thread's work. The leading-slash normalisation is the detail worth copying, since `/artifacts/x` and `artifacts/x` are the same object.

- **[Ideas] DONE.** Re-verified 2026-09-24 against the tree at a04990e. Dedupe by upsert id `${traceId}:${user}:${feedbackType}` (`observability/langfuse-feedback.ts:31`); feedback lands as Langfuse scores and survives trace expiry on the Langfuse side. The free-text shape problem is a separate open item (user feedback audit part 5). Original item: Feedback ratings are not deduplicated, so Slack re-dispatching a click records the same thumb twice and skews the aggregates. The observability store cannot answer "has this user already rated this message": `listFeedback` reads local storage, and prod exports Platform-only, so nothing is there to read. Durable dedupe would need our own Postgres table keyed on message and user. Wider than dedupe: the owner reports feedback is completely broken, never reaches Mastra Platform, and is lost once traces expire, so this needs a full revamp rather than a fix. It is coupled to the observability decision in the legacy backlog, since moving to Langfuse or a self-hosted store would change where feedback lands.

- **[Product] Decided not to build (2026-09-24).** Owner decision Q10 (2026-09-24): closed. The two cheap levers are in place: `stepGuard` re-prompts once on a lone marker line or leaked markup, and `reasoningPrompt` sits last in the system messages. Structural marker emission and tripwire regeneration are not wanted. Original item: Issue 35, force the agent to follow the spec on output formatting, arrows and the like. Currently advisory in the prompt and not followed. Scoped 2026-09-12. The rule lives in `prompts/reasoning.ts`: every message before the final answer opens with one marker (`→`, `↺`, `?`, `●`, `◐`, `○`, `⚠`) and is italicised, the final answer takes neither. Nothing checks it. Channels offers no outbound text hook to check it with either: `channels/types.d.ts` has `formatError` and the deprecated `formatToolCall` and nothing that transforms an ordinary outbound message, so any enforcement has to be an agent output processor running ahead of `chat-channel-render`. The thing that makes a deterministic check awkward is that "is this the final answer" is only knowable at the end of the turn, so a `processOutputStream` rewrite cannot tell an intermediate step from the answer without buffering, and buffering kills streaming. That suggests inverting it rather than validating it: the harness already knows a message is intermediate, because another one follows, so emit the marker structurally and leave the model only the wording. The model stops being able to forget the marker because it never writes it. Cheaper things to try first, in order: move `reasoningPrompt` to the end of the system messages, since a formatting rule early in a long prompt decays over a long turn; and add two or three worked examples of a full turn, marked steps then a plain final answer, because models copy formatting from examples far more reliably than from description. Rejected: a guardrail that throws a tripwire and regenerates, which costs a model call per violation and can loop, and structured output, which does not compose with channels streaming.

- **[Product] DONE.** Re-verified 2026-09-24 against the tree at a04990e. `tools/slack/upload-file.ts:47-89` streams from the sandbox to `files.getUploadURLExternal` with `duplex: 'half'`; the cap is `config.ts:22` `upload.maxBytes` 1 GB. Live Slack verification is in the owner LIVE checklist. Original item: Stream file uploads instead of buffering them, so the cap can be raised toward Slack's own 1 GB limit. Today `src/mastra/tools/slack/upload-file.ts` reads the whole file into memory and `MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES` is 100 MB, which is about 7% of the service's entire memory budget in one allocation. That is what OOM-killed the bot at 15:42 on 2026-09-12, with the kernel naming the thread: `HTTP Client invoked oom-killer`. Do not raise the cap before this lands, since every byte is currently resident and a 1 GB cap on a 1550 MB budget guarantees the kill rather than risking it. The route: E2B supports `format: 'stream'` returning a `ReadableStream<Uint8Array>`, and Slack's `files.getUploadURLExternal` plus a plain POST to the returned URL plus `files.completeUploadExternal` streams end to end, with `fetch(url, { body: stream, duplex: 'half' })`. `length` is required upfront and `stat.size` already has it. The blocker is that this has to bypass `destination.post()`: the Chat SDK's `FileUpload.data` is `Buffer | Blob | ArrayBuffer` with no stream variant, so the tool would drive the Slack client directly and resolve the raw channel id and thread ts itself via `src/mastra/lib/ids.ts`.

- **[Same-branch batch] Live browser view in Slack (2026-09-24).** `@mastra/browser-viewer` 0.2.4 (owner asked for BrowserViewer by name) as `SandboxBrowser` (`src/mastra/workspace/browser.ts`) on the workspace (`browser` in `src/mastra/workspace/index.ts`). There is no 'CUA viewer' in Mastra: computer use is `@mastra/e2b-desktop` (noVNC URL), deferred by the owner. Design: CloakBrowser runs INSIDE the thread's sandbox as `cloakserve` on port 9222 (pattern from /workspaces/mastra-agent `browser/cdp.ts`); the host attaches with Playwright over E2B's edge for the screencast only; every `agent-browser` command gets `--cdp ws://127.0.0.1:9222/...` (sandbox loopback) via Mastra's CLI injection (`getCdpUrl` override), so the sandbox never needs a token. Chrome never launches on the host: `launch`/`ensureReady` are overridden to only attach, and `executablePath` points at a nonexistent file so any missed path fails (BrowserViewer's `launchBrowser` calls `chromium.launchServer` on the host, and mastra-agent's `super.launch()` fallback does exactly that; not copied). Security: sandboxes now created with `allowPublicTraffic: false`; E2B header confirmed as `e2b-traffic-access-token` (E2B docs 'Restricting public access'); it is create-only per E2B docs, so existing sandboxes (no `trafficAccessToken`) skip the live view and agent-browser keeps its own browser. The header needed `patches/@mastra+browser-viewer@0.2.4.patch` (options arg on `connectToExternalCdp` -> `connectOverCDP`), generated with diff -u against `npm pack` (bun patch --commit is broken here), checked by `scripts/verify-mastra-patch.ts`. Viewer: `/live/:ticket` (signed with a second Factory `createStateSigner`, key derived with info `gorkie-live-view`, 10 min) serves an `<img>` fed by Mastra's own `/browser/orchestrator/stream` WebSocket (@hono/node-ws, already a deployer dependency, so it works under `mastra start`); CSP `frame-ancestors` Slack only so the video block can embed it; `/live/:ticket/thumb.jpg` is a host-side Playwright screenshot. The WS route has no Mastra auth, so the proxy guard in `src/mastra/index.ts` only lets a proxied request through with a ticket whose threadId matches (bug caught during review: an undefined/undefined comparison would have let an request with no ticket through). Watch-only: `injectMouseEvent`/`injectKeyboardEvent` are no-ops. Slack card (`src/mastra/chat/live-view.ts`): `video` block (owner said yes to the inline embed; needs `links.embed:write` + `unfurl_domains`) + Watch live button + context, refreshed every 15s with a fresh ticket and cache-busted thumbnail (no Slack file uploads), ended in `pauseSandbox` (normal end, abort, error) with the browser session closed. A failed CloakBrowser start or CDP attach logs and falls back to agent-browser's own browser rather than failing the command. Template bumped to `gorkie-workspace:2.1` (pinned `cloakbrowser[serve]==0.5.5`, `cloakserve` from the v0.5.5 tag, `python3 -m cloakbrowser install` as `user`). NOT live-tested (no bot or template build run).

- **[Same-branch batch] Moderator bans (2026-09-24).** Ported the ban half of `techwithanirudh/gork-slack`'s reports feature (`server/slack/features/reports/*`; `gork` itself has no ban code on any branch). Owner cut user reporting mid-build: no `report_user` tool, no LLM validation, no strikes or auto-ban, no report cards. What shipped:
  - Storage: one append-only `moderation_events` table (`action` ban/unban, `user_id`, `actor_id`, `reason`, `expires_at`, `created_at`, index on user+time), one migration `drizzle/<ts>_moderation`. The newest row decides the state; expiry is lazy (no cron). `src/mastra/db/queries/moderation.ts`: `activeBan`, `activeBans` (DISTINCT ON user), `recordDecision`, `getModerationEvent`.
  - Who: `MODERATORS` env list of Slack user ids. Deliberately not "members of gorkie-logs" (invites would grow authority unaudited) and no workspace-admin fallback. Guards: no self-ban, no banning moderators or the bot.
  - `/ban @user [1h|1d|7d|30d|perm] [reason]` and `/unban @user [reason]` (dev manifest: `/dev-ban`, `/dev-unban`) go through the channels `handlers.onSlashCommand` override in `chat/moderation/commands.ts`. That override is required: Mastra's default catch-all would otherwise also run the command text as an agent turn (`@mastra/core` agent dist ~22152, Chat SDK runs catch-alls before specific handlers). Any other slash command is ignored. Replies go through the payload's `response_url` (validated to hooks.slack.com), which works in conversations the bot is not in, unlike `chat.postEphemeral`. No argument lists active bans. Mentions arrive escaped (`should_escape: true`).
  - `LOGS_CHANNEL` (gorkie-logs): every ban/unban posts a card (user, by, until, reason) with Unban and More info buttons. Unban on a card re-checks the moderator list, records an unban only if the person is still banned, and edits the card to "Lifted by". Non-moderator clicks get an ephemeral refusal (gork-slack failed silently).
  - Enforcement (`isBanned`, fails open on a DB error so an outage does not lock everyone out): `onMention` (before the allow-list, ephemeral notice), `onDirectMessage` (notice in the DM), `onSubscribedMessage` (notice only on a mention, silent otherwise), a new channels `onAction` override that blocks banned clickers before Mastra's built-in tool approve/deny, the native stop button, App Home (publishes only the ban notice), and `schedules.prepare` returning `null` (skip, not delete, so an unban resumes their tasks; creator read from the stored wake request context, falling back to the schedule resourceId).
  - Not done: stopping a turn already running when the ban lands; filtering banned authors out of shared-thread history.

- **[Customization] Switchable tool display.** Three modes, stored as gorkie's own words so a Mastra rename cannot strand saved data: Hidden (`hidden`, typing status only, the default), Compact (`grouped`, one plan widget per reply) and Detailed (`timeline`, a card per step). The mapping lives only in `types/display.ts`. Precedence is thread override (`!display hidden|compact|detailed|reset`, kept in Mastra `threadState`), then the answered person's App Home radio (`user_settings.tool_display`, migration `20260924044806_add_tool_display`), then `config.toolDisplay.default`. Resolution happens in `processors/tool-display.ts`, placed first in the orchestrator's `outputProcessors`: on the first chunk it rewrites `toolDisplay` on channels' render object (`__mastra_chat_channel_render`, `agent-DwtTO5Px.js:21317`), which Mastra's own channel processor reads lazily (`:21451`, `:21480`). Configured processors run before channel processors (`listResolvedOutputProcessors`, `:35620`). This replaced the earlier AsyncLocalStorage getter on the adapter config, which was built and reverted: it only covered turns entering through `runTurn`, and approval clicks build their render context inside Mastra's own `onAction` (`:22333`), so an approved turn fell back to the static default mid-turn. Scheduled runs carry no render object and keep the adapter default, which is right for unattended runs. The footer now reads `done in 42s · 7 tools` (skip excluded). Two hunks added to `patches/@mastra+core@1.69.0.patch`: a 50-task cap per plan session (Slack's documented plan-block maximum; an over-cap append silently disabled structured chunks and a later text flush threw again, losing buffered text) that drops extra task updates and rolls a grouped session to a new message at a step boundary once it holds 40; and timeline task output preferring each tool's `transform.display` summary over the truncated raw result. `scripts/verify-mastra-patch.ts` (run by `postbuild`) fails the build if any patch marker or the render key literal is missing. Not done, on purpose: a function-form `toolDisplay` (resolves to `cards` and closes the stream on every step, `:23150-23180`), per-mode `typingStatus` (not part of the render context, so it cannot vary per run; Compact therefore hides the typing status after the first tool), and patching the Slack adapter rethrow (a second patch file is a dependency change). Needs live testing in each mode, including an approval mid-turn in Compact and a 45/50/55-task turn.

## 2026-09-18

- **[Known issues]** Langfuse credentials are now required, fixing a boot crash that made every production deploy impossible. Symptom on the VPS (`gorkie.service`, systemd): `MastraError` / `OBSERVABILITY_INVALID_INSTANCE_CONFIG`, `"Invalid configuration for observability instance 'default': At least one exporter or a bridge is required"`, thrown from `@mastra/observability/dist/index.js:9434` before the Slack connection is ever opened, so the unit exits immediately.
  - **Root cause.** `src/mastra/index.ts` built `exporters` from two spreads that could both be empty: `...(isProduction ? [] : [new MastraStorageExporter()])` dropped the DuckDB exporter under `NODE_ENV=production`, and `...(env.LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY && env.LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY ? [...] : [])` dropped Langfuse when the keys were unset. `env.ts` had both keys `.optional()`. Production plus no Langfuse keys therefore produced `exporters: []`, which Mastra rejects.
  - **Why it hit 100% of deploys.** `.env.example` never listed `LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY`, `LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY` or `LANGFUSE_BASE_URL`. It still advertised `MASTRA_PLATFORM_ACCESS_TOKEN` and `MASTRA_PROJECT_ID`, which `env.ts` stopped reading when `MastraPlatformExporter` was removed. Anyone filling in `.env` from the example got a config that could only crash in production, while `bun run dev` worked fine because the DuckDB exporter covered the empty branch.
  - **Fix.** `env.ts:30-31` `LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY` and `LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY` are now `z.string().min(1)`. The empty-exporter state is now unreachable rather than guarded, so the ternary in `index.ts` was deleted and the two Langfuse exporters are unconditional. `LANGFUSE_BASE_URL` stays defaulted to `https://cloud.langfuse.com`, so only self-hosters set it. `.env.example` swaps the dead Mastra Platform block for a Langfuse one; the README env table gains all three rows.
  - **Deliberately not done.** Did not make the config degrade to zero exporters in production. Tracing is the only production observability this project has, and a bot that boots with no traces fails silently later, which is worse than refusing to boot. Owner chose required over optional.
  - **Note for forks.** This makes a Langfuse account a hard dependency of running gorkie in production, not just a recommended one.

## 2026-09-14

- **[Models: why deepseek 400s on tool turns, and why renaming won't fix it]** Investigation only, no ladder change applied yet. Symptom in prod: `opencode-go (model: deepseek-flash)` returns `400 [invalid_request_error] The reasoning_content in the thinking mode must be passed back to the API` on every tool-calling turn, then the ladder falls to `glm-5.3-flash` which `MastraTimeoutError`s (`firstChunkMs`) on the huge-context turns, so a thread cycles the whole ladder. Single-shot deepseek turns succeed. Langfuse census of the last 194 generation spans: `google/gemini-3.5-flash-lite` 109 (the summarizer, not the reply model), `glm-5.3-flash` 43, `deepseek-flash` 42, `muse-spark-1.3-contributor` **0** (never reached; safe to remove, and it trains on data). All spans logged `DEFAULT`, none `ERROR`: Langfuse records the successful retry, not the failed attempt, so failure rate is not visible there, only which models produce output.
  - **Root cause (a provider-routing mismatch, verified in `node_modules/@mastra/core/dist`):** there are two OpenAI-compatible message converters in the bundle. The **generic** one (`dist-_EYayO5k.js:2632`, the path gorkie's calls take per the stack trace `OpenAICompatibleChatLanguageModel.doStream … dist-_EYayO5k.js`) attaches `...reasoning.length > 0 ? { reasoning_content: reasoning } : {}`, i.e. it sends `reasoning_content` only if a `reasoning` part is still on the assistant message. Across a tool round-trip that part is not retained, so the field is omitted and DeepSeek's thinking-mode gateway 400s. The **DeepSeek-aware** converter (inside `@ai-sdk/deepseek`, `models-dev-Bs2EOpUp.js:5558`) force-sends `reasoning_content: reasoning != null ? reasoning : isDeepSeekV4 ? "" : void 0`, guaranteeing the field. opencode's own client does the same round-trip in `packages/llm/src/protocols/openai-chat.ts` (`lowerAssistantMessage` replays reasoning captured this turn, or `message.native.openaiCompatible.reasoning_content`).
  - **Why gorkie misses the fix:** it needs BOTH (1) routing through the real `deepseek/` provider (the only path that loads `@ai-sdk/deepseek`), and (2) `modelId.includes("deepseek-v4")` (`models-dev-Bs2EOpUp.js:5483`). gorkie satisfies neither: it uses the `opencode-go/` gateway (→ generic converter always, regardless of model id) and the alias `deepseek-flash` (no "v4").
  - **Dead-end premise, do not re-propose:** switching the id to `opencode('deepseek-v4.1-flash')` does NOT fix it. The name would satisfy `.includes("deepseek-v4")`, but that check is never reached under `opencode-go/`: the provider, not the name, selects the converter. The code path that drops `reasoning_content` is byte-identical for any `opencode-go/deepseek-*` id, so the same 400 is expected (the gateway's v4.1 upstream behaviour is unverified by live test, but gorkie's serialization is unchanged).
  - **The only real fixes:** (A) route through the paid real provider `deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash` (needs `DEEPSEEK_API_KEY` + direct billing, loses the free HackClub/opencode-go gateway, verify ≥1M context) → `isDeepSeekV4` true → fix applies; (B) keep the gateway but add a transport shim (custom `fetch` on a self-built opencode-go OpenAI-compatible provider that injects `reasoning_content: ""` on any assistant message carrying `tool_calls` that lacks it), medium effort, replicates opencode/@ai-sdk-deepseek, brittle to gateway changes; (C) disable thinking mode if the gateway honours it: cheapest but guts the only reason to want deepseek; (D) make `glm-5.3-flash` the default and sidestep entirely (recommended for the cutover). Reference clones used: `sst/opencode` and `itzmetanjim/coolton` (both fetched to the session scratchpad, not committed).

## 2026-09-13

- **[Slack search: standardized on one identity]** Collapsed the two search identities into one. Search was dual-path: a per-mentioner `action_token` (`searchedAs: 'requester'`, bot token) when Slack put one in the event, falling back to `SLACK_SEARCH_USER_TOKEN` (`searchedAs: 'workspace'`, the installer's user token). A 50-call channel-mention stress test in the dev workspace showed all 50 taking the fallback and failing `assertPublicOnlyFallback` because the configured token was over-scoped (`search:read.private/.mpim/.im`). That surfaced two things: (1) the over-scoped token needs reissuing as `search:read.public` only (owner action, plus the manifest now declares `oauth_config.scopes.user: ["search:read.public"]` so a reinstall can mint it), and (2) `action_token` only rides along on the assistant-surface event, not on a plain channel `@mention` (`handlers.ts` captured it from `message.raw.action_token`), so in ordinary channel use the "fallback" was always the real path. Decision (`search-slack.ts`): since we restrict to `channel_types: public_channel`, "search as the pinger" and "search as the installer" return essentially the same results (public channels are workspace-visible to everyone), so the requester apparatus bought almost no behavioral difference for a lot of machinery. Dropped: `action_token` capture (`captureSearchToken` + schema + 3 call sites in `handlers.ts`, and the now-unused `zod` import), `searchToken` on thread state (`chat/state.ts`, `types/thread.ts`), the `identitySchema` requester/workspace enum, the `searchedAs` output field, identity-prefixed cursors and the foreign-cursor rejection, and the requester→fallback token-expiry try/catch. Kept all four privacy gates unchanged: `channel_types: public_channel` (with the comma-string-vs-array footgun comment), `assertPublicOnly` scope check (renamed from `assertPublicOnlyFallback`; `verifiedToken` from `verifiedFallbackToken`), the `fetchMetadata` visibility post-filter batched 4-at-a-time, and the live-message guard (workspace identity never runs on scheduled/unattended turns). Net −150 lines in `search-slack.ts`. Tradeoff accepted: every search now runs under the installer's identity and rate budget (the 50-call test would hit one budget), fine because public-only makes results near-identical anyway. typecheck + biome clean. Comments trimmed to the three real whys + the biome-ignore. Compared against `itzmetanjim/coolton`, which uses classic `search.messages` with one broad `search:read` user token and no public-only filtering (its docstring admits it searches "channels, DMs, files"); did NOT adopt that, its cheapness is exactly the privacy hole our fan-out closes. Follow-ups in the same change: renamed the env var `SLACK_SEARCH_USER_TOKEN` -> `SLACK_USER_TOKEN` (a user token is not inherently search-specific and may do more later; also matches coolton), updated across `src/env.ts`, `search-slack.ts` (usage + the three refusal messages), `.env`, `.env.example`, `README.md`; the stale "after the bot's ephemeral search token expires" wording in README/.env.example was corrected since there is no longer a fallback path. Added `docs/slack-search.md` documenting the public-only design, setup, and the cumulative-grant gotcha (a token authorized with broader user scopes keeps them until revoked; removing them from the manifest does not shrink an issued token, so `auth.test` still reports the old grant and gorkie refuses it, fix is Revoke All OAuth Tokens then reinstall, which does not remove the bot from channels). Bumped local Node to 24.21.0 (`.nvmrc`/engines already pinned 24) so `check:spelling` runs.

- **[Split]** This session was divided into two stacked branches: `github-setup` (everything below except backgrounding and the trim-payloads span processor) and `background` (those two, stacked on `github-setup`, not merging yet). Entries below that describe backgrounding or `trim-payloads` reflect work now on the `background` branch.

- **[Correction: reverted my blobless clone back to shallow]** Earlier this session I switched `checkout` to `git clone --filter=blob:none` to fix shallow-push. That was wrong: the prior agent had already fixed push (unshallow-retry) and rejected blobless because a treeless clone lazy-fetches blobs on later git ops (`git diff <ref>`, `blame`, `show`), and on a private repo those fetches fall outside the credential window and fail. Reverted `checkout` to `--depth 50` and restored the push comment; the push `/shallow/` unshallow-retry is now live and correct again. checkout/push/TODO consistent.

- **[allowlist: one list, SDK-derived split]** Collapsed `READ_TOOLS` + `WRITE_TOOLS` into a single flat `ALLOWLIST`, removed the `misgrouped` reconciliation and its throw. `github/index.ts` already gated on `isWriteTool` (= `name in GITHUB_WRITE_TOOLS`, the SDK), so the hand-maintained read/write split was redundant. Now read/write is correct-by-construction from the SDK (a reclassified tool can never be mis-gated, so no throw is needed), a dropped/renamed tool still fails typecheck (literal not assignable to `GithubToolName`), and there is one curated list to maintain instead of two buckets plus a check. Kept the explicit list rather than an SDK preset on purpose: a preset could silently add tools to gorkie's approval surface on a bump (this supersedes the earlier throw/log-drop entries).

- **[Correction: fail-loud on SDK drift]** Reversed two earlier calls after realizing the type system already covers one of them.
  - **Missing/renamed SDK tool name:** reverted the runtime skip-guard in `github/index.ts`. `READ_TOOLS`/`WRITE_TOOLS` are typed `GithubToolName[]`, so a bump that drops or renames a tool makes the string literal an invalid `GithubToolName` and `bun run typecheck` fails in CI, louder and earlier than any runtime throw, and before it ships. The guard defended a case the compiler blocks.
  - **Reclassified tool (read<->write, name unchanged):** flipped `allowlist.ts` back from log+drop to **throw** at module load. This is NOT type-caught (the name stays valid, only its bucket changes), so a wrong approval-gate would silently ship. The throw only fires when `@github-tools/sdk` is bumped (dev-time); the pinned prod deploy already passed it. Removed the now-unused `logger` import and the ALLOWLIST filter.
  - **/no-comments follow-up trims:** `config.ts` backgroundTimeout and `db/index.ts` migrations comments trimmed to their non-obvious why (dropped the name-restatements Comment Sicko had left breathing).

- **[Review fixes, wave 2 + /no-comments]**
  - **Fork-push path mismatch (fixed).** `push.ts` derived its working dir from the push *target*, so the fork flow the tool prescribes (clone upstream, push to fork) looked in a dir that was never cloned and died with an opaque ENOENT. Added an optional `checkout` input (the repo you cloned, defaults to the target); path now comes from `checkout ?? repository` while the remote stays the target. The 403 message and tool description tell the model to set `checkout`. Also corrected the now-stale "clones shallow" comment (checkout is blobless since the `--filter=blob:none` change) and kept the unshallow retry as a fallback for pre-change persisted sandboxes.
  - **One missing SDK name no longer kills all github tools (fixed).** `github/index.ts` destructured `built[name]` unguarded; a dropped SDK name would throw and the outer catch would return zero github tools. Now iterates a `Partial<typeof built>` view and skips absent names.
  - **Spurious StaleFileError (fixed).** `filesystem.ts` `stat` fell back to `new Date()` for a missing mtime while `writeFile`'s expectedMtime check used `new Date(0)`, so every edit-after-read of a file the sandbox reported no mtime for threw StaleFileError. Aligned `stat` to `new Date(0)`.
  - **Recursive listing prefix (fixed).** `listFiles` returned bare basenames, so a recursive listing showed many indistinguishable `index.ts` rows. Recursive mode now returns the path relative to the listing root (`EntryInfo.path` exists); flat listings unchanged.
  - **Deterministic 400 retry (fixed).** `error-handling.ts` retried a 400 once (guaranteed second failure for a shape error); set `isBadRequestError` to `maxRetries: 0` so the run falls through to the next model in the ladder immediately.
  - **/no-comments (whole branch).** Comment Sicko swept ~54 comment sites across the `316b089...` branch diff. Accepted: deleted 1 pure what-comment (`tool-media.ts` drop-loop narration); trimmed 3 (`config.ts` maxReadBytes to the OOM why, `background-tasks.ts` ACTIVE to drop the bug war-story, `checkout.ts` execute to keep only the unique fail-safe/TOCTOU note). Rejected 1: the `push.ts` "clones shallow" liar was already corrected by the fork-push rewrite, and its unshallow branch is not dead (pre-blobless sandboxes), so kept. Everything else it hunted crawled away as legitimate non-obvious why on external/platform/security paths (its own clause-2). No reruns, no constraint comments.
  - **Still open (not this wave):** modal 3s-ack (connect/settings/mcp), `__unscoped__` shared sandbox, expired-token-shows-connected, `filesystem` maxDepth off-by-one; per-thread bg cap + footer per-turn query move with the backgrounding branch.

- **[Review fixes]** Batch from the review triage.
  - **token.ts transient-blip credential deletion (fixed).** The refresh catch deleted the stored credential on *any* throw, so a GitHub 5xx/DNS blip during the 5-min refresh margin logged the user out for good. Now it deletes only when GitHub returns `bad_refresh_token`; every other failure keeps the credential and returns the current token. Error code/status pulled via a Zod schema (no casts).
  - **GitHub client secret in logs (fixed).** That same `logger.warn` logged the whole octokit error, which carries `client_secret`/`refresh_token` in a top-level `request` the SDK's own redaction misses. Two layers: token.ts now logs only `{userId,status,code}`, and `logger/index.ts` redact list gained `request`, `client_secret`, `refresh_token`, `clientSecret`, `refreshToken` (app-wide defense).
  - **read_file OOM guard (fixed).** `filesystem.ts` readFile already stats the file; now it refuses over `config.file.maxReadBytes` (10MB) before `Buffer.from`. Stops `fallocate -l 8G x && read_file x` (reachable from injected repo content) killing the host.
  - **Shallow-clone-then-push (fixed).** `checkout.ts` clone switched from `--depth 50` to `--filter=blob:none`: full history so a later push is not rejected `shallow update not allowed`, still skips most of the download.
  - **get_slack_file retry on a locked stream (fixed).** The streaming write was wrapped in `retryOnDead`; a retry re-pipes an already-consumed ReadableStream (TypeError) and double-counts bytes. Dropped the wrapper; a dead sandbox mid-download errors and the next call resumes from the `.part` file.
  - **Re-pinned `@mastra/core` to exact `1.66.0`** (was `^1.66.0`) so a bump can't silently orphan the load-bearing approver-identity patch. Needs a `bun install` to sync the lockfile.

- **[GitHub]** `github_checkout` now only requires approval for **private** repos; public repos clone with no approval and no credential window.
  - **Why.** The reason checkout was gated was never "it's a repo", it's that `withCredential` opens ambient github.com auth on the whole sandbox (the real attack surface). A public clone is anonymous and needs no credential, so there is nothing to gate. Approval and the credential window now move together: public -> plain `git clone`, no `withCredential`, no approval; private -> `withCredential` + approval, as before.
  - **How.** `requireApproval` is now an async function of the input (`(input) => approval && (await repoAccess).private`), which Mastra supports (`boolean | ((input, ctx) => boolean | Promise<boolean>)`, verified in `tools/tool.d.ts`/`types.d.ts:551`). Visibility comes from a host-side authenticated `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}` (user's token, stays on host, 5000/hr, returns `private`). Broadened `repoPushAccess` -> `repoAccess` in `lib/github/api.ts` to return `{ private, push }` from the one response, so `execute` gets visibility (for the clone decision) and push access (for the note) in a single call. Fail-safe: no token / API error / absent `private` all treat the repo as private (gate + credential).
  - **Cost.** One extra `/repos` call per checkout (the `requireApproval` probe); `execute`'s probe replaces the old `repoPushAccess` call, so execute is net-zero.
  - **Known micro-window (accepted).** A repo flipped public->private in the seconds between the approval check and `execute` would be cloned anonymously and simply fail (never opening an ungated credential window); the reverse flip is harmless. Documented in the tool.
  - `bun run typecheck` + ultracite clean. `requireApproval` param type-inferred from the input schema, no annotation needed.

- **[Audit / review]** Branch review of `github-setup` vs `origin/main`, then fixes. Every claim was checked against `node_modules` or git history rather than the diff, which killed two candidate findings before they were written up: `isWriteTool = name in GITHUB_WRITE_TOOLS` is safe (the SDK exports an object literal, `index-BNeMjmgp.d.mts:296`, not an array), and the deferred background executor **does** receive the dispatching turn's `requestContext`, because `agent-CEHR0Wd8.js:26936` spreads the live `toolOptions` into the executor closure. That second one closes the largest open unknown in the backgrounding notes below: `requireSandbox` resolves normally inside a background task, and the `reached` WeakSet entry is shared with the turn that dispatched it.
  - **Missing columns on any database built from `main` (fixed, `drizzle/0002_add_missing_columns.sql`).** `0000_baseline.sql` is `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` on the stated grounds that the tables already exist everywhere. True of the tables, false of three columns: `main`'s `createTables()` creates `mcp_servers` without `permission` and `user_settings` without `github_permission`/`github_threads`. Those were added by this branch's own `add column if not exists` block (`5df3a12`, still readable at `aefc63a^`), which never shipped, so on production the baseline is a no-op and the columns stay missing. There was no later migration adding them, verified by grepping `drizzle/*.sql`. The symptom would have been near-invisible rather than loud, because almost every read is caught: `githubAccess` catches into `{state:'unreadable'}` so **GitHub silently loads zero tools**, `userMCPTools` catches so **every user's MCP tools silently vanish**, `orchestrator.ts:79` catches, and `app-home/view.ts` `settled()` catches. Fix is a hand-written `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` migration, the same escape hatch `0001` already uses for the drop; `drizzle-kit check` passes with the carried-forward snapshot.
  - **`github_list_pull_request_files` threw in shared threads (fixed, `tools/github/index.ts`).** The old build spread the SDK's `toModelOutput` in and *then* overrode `execute` with `handoff`, so the handoff's `{message}` was fed to a formatter expecting a GitHub API result.
