Four of the five formatters survive by accident (`getFileContent` guards on `'content' in result`, the other three on `result.files ? …`), but `listPullRequestFilesToModelOutput` calls `truncatePatchFields(output)` unguarded (`write-tools-Dk32V9Hl.mjs:481-492`) and throws `files.map is not a function`. Now the non-direct branch replaces the tool outright, destructuring `toModelOutput` off rather than layering. Also dropped `needsApproval` to `false` there, since nothing on that path reaches GitHub.
  - **Background-task completion wake had no channel context (fixed, `index.ts` + `lib/background-tasks.ts`).** `notifyBackgroundTask` sent `ifIdle: {behavior:'wake'}` with no `streamOptions`, while `scheduled-tasks/create.ts` and `wait.ts` both pass `requestContext: { channel }`, and omitting exactly that is recorded below as the root cause of scheduled tasks failing. It could not be fixed in place: `BackgroundTask.threadId` is the **memory** thread, not the Slack `slack:C…:ts` that `channelContext()` holds, which is why `create.ts` passes both separately. Fix is a capped LRU in `lib/background-tasks.ts` keyed on the memory thread id (the one id both sides see), written by `run_background` from `context.agent?.threadId` and read back in `notifyBackgroundTask` from `task.threadId`. Note `sendSignal`'s `streamOptions.requestContext` wants a real `RequestContext` instance, unlike `schedules.create`, which takes a plain object.
  - **`pauseSandbox` froze the box under a pending task (fixed).** `turn-footer.ts` treated `pending|running|suspended` as still-working while `workspace/index.ts` guarded on `running` alone, so a task that had not started yet got its sandbox paused, and `run_background` only calls `ensureRunning` once at the top before holding a 30-minute RPC. The two branches also disagreed about `clearSandboxCache`. `threadHasBackgroundTask` now owns the status set and takes a bare `threadId`, so they cannot drift again.
  - **`git()` ran on E2B's 60s default (fixed).** No `timeoutMs` was passed, so a cold clone or large push died at 60s *inside* `withCredential`, where `retryOnDead` re-ran the whole clone to die again. New `sandbox.gitTimeout` (5 min) in `config.ts`.
  - **Overlapping GitHub git ops corrupted each other's credential window (fixed, `tools/github/git.ts`).** The first operation's `finally` resets `baseRules()` while the second is mid-push. `TODO.md` claims `requireApproval` forces sequential execution, but at `level: 'never'` in a DM `asksBefore({kind:'write', level:'never'})` is false, so `pushTool` gets `approval: false` and concurrency returns. Added a promise-chain mutex keyed on `sandbox.e2b.sandboxId` that deletes its own tail entry, so the Map cannot grow.
  - **Shallow-clone push (mitigated, not proven).** `checkout` clones `--depth 50` and its own `note` promises `github_push_branch`; git refuses a push whose history bottoms out at the shallow boundary. Deliberately did **not** switch to `--filter=blob:none`, which looked like the obvious fix and is wrong here: a treeless clone fetches blobs lazily, and every later `git diff`/`git checkout` would need github.com auth *outside* the credential window, breaking private repos. Instead `push.ts` catches `/shallow/i` and retries once after `git fetch --unshallow`. Still needs a live private-repo run.
  - **Media payloads back in traces (fixed, `observability/trim-payloads.ts`).** `view_image` puts the full base64 in `outputSchema.data`; `toModelOutput` only changes what the *model* sees, so ~13MB of base64 per call still lands in message history and on the `tool_call` span, which is **not** in `excludeSpanTypes`, reintroducing the bloat class `ISSUES.md` §B measured. Fixed generically rather than per tool, because Mastra's own `read_file` returns media the same way: a span output processor truncates any string over 20,000 chars down to a 2,000-char head plus a marker. Head length is far under the limit on purpose so re-running is a no-op (`processSpan` can run per exporter), and unchanged subtrees are returned by reference so nothing is deep-copied for the common case. Verified: idempotent, reference-stable, and safe at 40 levels of nesting.
  - **`!stop` double-posted (fixed).** `stopThread` posted `_Stopped._` and then the agent's `onAbort` posted `that turn stopped before I finished`, which also contradicts a deliberate stop. `stopThread` now returns `'aborted' | 'cancelled' | 'idle'` and the command stays silent on `'aborted'`, leaving that message to `onAbort`; a run that only cancelled background tasks still reports itself.
  - **`allowlist.ts` took the bot down at import (fixed).** A `@github-tools/sdk` reclassification threw at module load, killing the whole Slack bot rather than GitHub. Now it logs and filters the misgrouped names out of `ALLOWLIST`, which still fails closed (the tool disappears rather than running at the wrong approval level).
  - **`wait` swept schedules across every thread (fixed).** `schedules.list({agentId})` is unpaginated and returned other threads' rows to delete. `ListSchedulesFilter` has `threadId`, so it is scoped now.
  - **Image-omission notice displaced the user's request (fixed, `processors/tool-media.ts`).** The `N older tool images omitted` notice was appended at the very end of the prompt, making a synthetic user message the most recent thing said. It now rides on the tool result the image was stripped from, which also covers the case the trailing notice missed (a result with other content alongside a dropped image) and removes the `kept.length === 0` special case entirely. Verified against a 12-image prompt: exactly 8 kept, newest-first, four per-result notes, no empty tool results, and `THE REAL REQUEST` still last.
  - **Deliberately not changed.** The per-turn `threadHasBackgroundTask` query in `turn-footer` stays: gating it on an in-memory flag would go stale across a restart exactly when `BackgroundTaskManager.restart` re-registers executors, and pausing a sandbox under a restarted task is worse than one indexed query per turn. `check:spelling` still cannot run locally (Node 22.17.0 against cspell's `>=22.18.0`), unchanged and already logged.


- **[Models]** Promoted `opencode-go/deepseek-flash` to the top of the shared orchestrator/research/explore fallback ladder in `providers.ts`, ahead of both GLM rungs (replacing the short-lived `deepseek-v4-flash-vision-exp` promotion from earlier the same day). GLM (`opencode-go/glm-5.3-flash` then `hackclub z-ai/glm-5.3-flash`) stays as fallback; `muse-spark-1.3-contributor` stays last. Branch base is commit `316b089e2c31a2244bd308f41c19b3075c252e61`, not `main`.
  - **Why.** The whole 2026-09-13 live session was GLM eating itself: `[too_many_images]` (max 8 inline images / 64 MiB, GLM hard cap) returns a **non-retryable 400**, so it burned *both* GLM rungs (1 and 2 were both GLM) before ever reaching deepseek at rung 3, and image-heavy threads (captcha solving, screenshots) got blank/looping responses. Also frequent `firstChunkMs` timeouts. A vision-first model at the top sidesteps the 8-image cap entirely. Complementary to the already-landed `moveToolImages` context-image cap (which was not deployed during those logs).
  - **Naming caveat (unresolved on models.dev).** The user is firm the id is `deepseek-flash`. models.dev's snapshot does NOT list `deepseek-flash` under `opencode-go` (only `deepseek-v4-flash`, `deepseek-v4-flash-vision-exp`, `deepseek-v4.1-flash`); a bare `deepseek-flash` appears only under the native `deepseek` provider at 1M/384k. Went with the user's id since they know what their opencode-go endpoint actually serves and the catalog can lag; could not independently confirm `opencode-go/deepseek-flash`'s context window or whether it is vision-capable. If it is NOT vision-capable, image-heavy threads lose the vision path that motivated moving off GLM, revisit if captcha/screenshot threads regress.
  - **R3/R4 partial.** Verified on models.dev that the rest of the ladder + summarizer hold >=1M input and output >=65,536: `opencode-go/glm-5.3-flash` 1M/131k, `zai/glm-5.3-flash` (hackclub proxy) 1M/131k, `opencode-go/muse-spark-1.3-contributor` 1.05M/131k, `google/gemini-3.5-flash-lite` (hackclub) 1.05M/65,536 (the output floor CLAUDE.md cites), `opencode-go/mimo-v2.5` 1M/128k. The new rung-1 `opencode-go/deepseek-flash` is UNVERIFIED on the catalog (see naming caveat); native `deepseek/deepseek-flash` is 1M/384k which would clear the bar if it is the same model.
  - **Interaction to remember.** `preferLastWorking()` still floats the last-good model back to rung 1, so this default order governs fresh threads and post-failure fallover, not a thread where GLM last succeeded. `bun run typecheck` + ultracite clean.

- **[Security]** Two confirmed findings fixed (a third, shared-thread credential steering, left open in TODO as a design decision).
  - **Fail-open approval guard (fixed).** The approver-identity patch `patches/@mastra+core@1.66.0.patch` (both the ESM and CJS hunks) gated with `if (approvalRequesterId && actingUserId && approvalRequesterId !== actingUserId)`, which lets an approval through when `actingUserId` is unreadable (fail-open, another user could answer a credentialed GitHub approval). Changed to `approvalRequesterId && (!actingUserId || approvalRequesterId !== actingUserId)` (fail-closed: an unknown actor is ignored). Re-applied via `bun install`; verified both `agent-*.js` and `agent-*.cjs` bundles carry the fix and the old guard is gone. The bug had survived the 1.62.0-alpha.9 -> 1.66.0 patch regeneration.
  - **User MCP tools could shadow built-in github tools (fixed).** `orchestrator.ts` merged tools as `{ ...base, ...github, ...userTools }`, so a user MCP server whose name concatenates into a built-in id (MCP namespaces `<server>_<tool>`; the name regex allows underscores, so `github_create` + tool `issue` = `github_create_issue`) would override the built-in and bypass its approval policy + shared-thread handoff. The exact-`'github'` name block did not cover such prefixes. Flipped the merge to `{ ...userTools, ...github, ...base }` so built-ins always win over user tools, order-based, not a name blocklist to keep chasing.

- **[Critical / Product]** Cap images sent to the model + surface model errors in Langfuse, and correct the backgrounding plan.
  - **Image cap (fixes the live `too_many_images` 400).** `processors/tool-media.ts` `moveToolImages` now, across the whole prompt, keeps only the most-recent images within `config.image.maxContextImages` (8) and `maxContextBytes` (60 MiB), relocating the kept ones into user messages (as before) and dropping older ones with a single "N older tool images omitted" note (and a per-result "image omitted" placeholder). Root cause was that it relocated EVERY tool image into the request with no cap, blowing GLM's 8-image/64-MiB non-retryable limit on image-heavy threads (the ~50-frame OCR thread). There is no Mastra built-in image cap (`maxImagesPerCall` is model metadata only). New tool `view_image` made this easy to hit.
  - **Model errors now reach Langfuse.** Removed `SpanType.MODEL_GENERATION` from `observability.excludeSpanTypes` in `index.ts` so model-call errors (e.g. `too_many_images`, firstChunk timeouts) appear in traces instead of only raw logs (the trace `ff8dd4c7...` had SPAN/TOOL/AGENT only because model spans were excluded). Kept `MODEL_STEP` excluded to bound volume. Trade-off: image-heavy threads get larger traces again; the image cap above bounds the worst of it.
  - **"Working in the background" turn indicator** (twa request): `turn-footer.ts` posts "_working in the background, I'll message you here when it's done…_" (no rating) instead of "_done in X_" when the thread still has a pending/running/suspended background task; the completion wake's own turn then posts the real "_done in X_". Shared `lib/background-tasks.ts` `threadHasBackgroundTask({ threadId, statuses })` helper used by the footer (pending/running/suspended) and `pauseSandbox` (running).
  - **Backgrounding BUILT (owner approved schema + config; typecheck + biome green; live-untested).** Native Mastra background tasks + a signal-wake bridge, per the corrected plan: `Mastra({ backgroundTasks: { enabled, onTaskComplete, onTaskFailed } })` (creates `mastra_background_tasks`); new `run_background(command, reason)` `createTool` (`e2b.commands.run` with a 10-min `e2b.setTimeout` keepalive, 30-min cap, output tailed to 10k, registered in `orchestratorTools` + `statuses.ts`); opted in agent-level (`orchestrator.backgroundTasks.tools.run_background`, deferred) so `execute_command` stays synchronous; `notifyBackgroundTask` bridge fires `orchestrator.sendSignal(notification, ifIdle:wake)` on complete/fail; `pauseSandbox` skips pausing when the thread has a running task. Four things need live verification on the running bot (documented in TODO): deferred-executor `requestContext` availability, idle-wake actually posting to Slack, keepalive holding a >16-min job, and model prompt guidance (deliberately not added until the wake is confirmed). This supersedes the "blocker found" note below.
  - **Backgrounding plan corrected (BLOCKER found, informed the build above).** Verified in source that `execute_command` CANNOT be flipped to background-eligible: agent-level `AgentBackgroundToolConfig` is only `{ enabled, timeoutMs }` (no `defaultDisposition`), eligible tools default to `deferred`, and channels never passes `untilIdle`, so a deferred `execute_command` would dispatch async and never return its result in-turn, breaking every command. `defaultDisposition: 'foreground'` exists only at the tool level (`createTool`). So backgrounding needs a NEW gorkie `run_background` `createTool`, plus the `onTaskComplete -> sendSignal(ifIdle wake)` bridge, a `pauseSandbox` guard on live tasks, and periodic `e2b.setTimeout` refresh, and it needs live iteration (untestable offline). Did NOT ship half-machinery blind. Full corrected step list in `TODO.md` under "Backgrounding". Owner approved the schema + config change; the feature itself is the next chunk.

- **[Audit / review]** Deep multi-subagent review of `github-setup` vs `main` (42 commits, 244 files), six reviewers by area, every Required/Critical claim re-verified against source. No Critical defects survived. Fixes applied this pass, owner-directed subset:
  - **App Home corrupt-credential UX (real logic bug).** `view.ts` wired `unreadable` to the settings read, so a stored GitHub token that no longer decrypts (`getGitHubCredential` -> unguarded `decryptSecret`, `db/queries/github.ts:34`) collapsed to `credential === undefined` and rendered a benign "Not connected" instead of the "Unavailable, reconnect" recovery branch; a transient settings hiccup meanwhile mislabelled a healthy connection. Fix: pull the credential read out of `settled` into its own `.then(ok/err)` so a decrypt/DB throw sets `unreadable: true` while a genuinely absent row stays `undefined`. Reads stay parallel in the same `Promise.all`.
  - **Stale `call_slack_api` references** after the tool was deleted: dropped the phrase from `prompts/features/code-mode.ts:7` and removed the dead status handler in `chat/status/statuses.ts`. Left the `grep` status handler: `grep` is still a live workspace tool (`workspace/tool-names.ts:5` `GREP = 'grep'`), the reviewer's "dead grep handler" call was wrong.
  - **slack-identity comment corrected.** The old comment claimed "SensitiveDataFilter never touches requestContext", which is false. Rewrote the rationale around what is actually true and verifiable: `RequestContext.serializeForSpan` passes plain objects through by reference so the render context reaches exporters, hence drop the whole entry. Kept the `'__mastra_chat_channel_render'` literal: `CHAT_CHANNEL_RENDER_CONTEXT_KEY` is declared only in `dist/channels/output-processor.d.ts` and is NOT re-exported from `@mastra/core` or `@mastra/core/channels`, so there is no clean public import; reaching into dist internals would be more fragile than a documented literal.
  - **Pause sandbox on abort and throw.** The `sandbox` output processor's `processOutputResult` pause never runs on an aborted or thrown turn, so a sandbox stayed live until its own 16 min timeout after every stopped turn. Extracted `pauseSandbox(requestContext)` in `workspace/index.ts` (three call sites: the output processor, `onAbort`, and a new `onError` in the orchestrator's `defaultOptions`).
  - **`list_channels` private filter removed (owner decision).** The branch had added a filter hiding private channels other than the current one; owner wants private channels the bot is a member of to be listable, and no private filter on `list_canvases` either. Removed the filter and the now-dead `currentChannelId`/`channelContext` import. This is a deliberate loosening: a user in one channel can now see the names of private channels the bot is in but they are not.
  - **`langfuse-feedback` fetch** wrapped in try/catch so a network failure to Langfuse warns instead of rejecting the exporter promise.
  - **`MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES` -> `config.ts`** as `upload.maxBytes` (standards: config values live in `config.ts`). **`addServer` return type** made explicit (`Promise<{ action: 'errors'; errors } | undefined>`).
  - **`fitHome` simplified.** Replaced the round-robin dealer (outer do-while + `Map<HomeSection, number>` + `paged.includes`) with a greedy in-order fill that keeps the reserved overflow slots and the "+N more" notices. Earlier sections win the remaining budget, which is fine at App Home's handful of rows; owner found the fairness machinery too complex for its scale.
  - Verified clean and left as-is: the `usedSandbox`/`__unscoped__` split (exec-only turns still pause; `requireSandbox` hard-throws without a threadId); per-user GitHub OAuth injected at the E2B proxy (token never enters the sandbox); timeout/`defaultOptions`/`onAbort` shapes match Mastra; OM output cap 32,768.
  - Deliberately NOT done (owner calls, recorded so they are not re-raised): canvas edit stays gated at read scope not manage scope; no IP pinning for MCP SSRF (Cloudflare and similar rotate IPs, so pinning the resolved IP would break legitimate servers, leaving the residual check-then-connect DNS TOCTOU accepted); `scheduledTasks.minInterval` 60s-off-production floor is intended; `.catch('write')` fail-open on `githubPermissionSchema` left as-is (explained, owner did not ask to change); `orchestrator` `maxMessages` 0 is intended because thread continuity comes through the custom `withHistory` (`chat/history.ts`), not Mastra's built-in backfill; `search_slack` shared-thread token race left for the planned search refactor.
  - **Finding 1 fixed.** `scheduled-tasks/create.ts` and `wait.ts` now persist `{ channel: channelContext(context.requestContext) }` into the schedule's `ifIdle.streamOptions.requestContext` instead of `context.requestContext?.toJSON()`. `toJSON()` (unlike `serializeForSpan`) does not redact `mastra__authToken` and keeps any probe-serializable registry value, so it was writing the whole registry into a durable Postgres row. Verified `'channel'` is the ONLY key anything in `src/mastra` ever reads out of requestContext (one `grep` hit, in `channelContext`), so `{ channel }` is fully sufficient to resume a woken turn; Mastra rebuilds its own render/adapter context on fire and gorkie never uses `mastra__authToken`.

- **[Simplification]** Second review-driven pass, owner-directed:
  - **Removed `mcpServerNames`** (`mcp/user-servers/client.ts`), the module-level `Map<threadId, Set<serverName>>` the reviewer flagged as unbounded. It existed only to let the synchronous typing-status renderer (`chat/status/index.ts`) turn an MCP tool call `<server>_<tool>` into "is using fathom: search"; the names were stashed at async tool-load time because the sync renderer only has `threadId`. Dropped the Map, its write in `tools.ts` (and the now-unused `threadId` param of `userMCPTools`, updated the orchestrator call site), the read loop in `status/index.ts`, and the re-export. MCP tool calls now fall through to the generic `is using <tool>` status. Acceptable since almost nobody has MCPs configured.
  - **Langfuse feedback via SDK.** Replaced the hand-rolled `fetch` to `/api/public/scores` (Basic auth + manual JSON) in `observability/langfuse-feedback.ts` with `@langfuse/client`'s `LangfuseClient.score.create` + `score.flush`. Promoted `@langfuse/client` (already transitive via `@mastra/langfuse`) to a direct dependency (`^5.11.1`, lockfile updated via `bun add`) so it does not vanish on a `@mastra/langfuse` bump. The client is constructed per-exporter (in a field, so it only builds when the exporter is instantiated, which index.ts guards on the keys being present). ScoreBody's `value`/`dataType` are independent (not a discriminated union), so the numeric/categorical branching stays a plain object.
  - **`/no-comments` pass** (Comment Sicko over the full branch diff). Killed 6 what-comments/narration on our own code (`config.ts` x2, `orchestrator.ts` reasoningPrompt, `view.ts` credentialResult, `canvas/sections.ts`, `scheduled-tasks/create.ts`). Kept every external-gotcha comment (Slack/Mastra/GitHub/E2B/undici/DNS platform constraints, the slack-identity and pauseSandbox rationales). Rejected its "MUST KILL reshape" rename flags: they are refactors beyond comment removal, and one (`config.ts` extract `productionMinInterval`/`devMinInterval`) directly conflicts with the repo's "avoid one-use constants" rule.
  - Guidelines sweep of the whole diff: no em dashes, no `as` casts (only `as const`), no `process.env` outside `src/env.ts` (only `drizzle.config.ts`, the standalone migration config, which is conventional). typecheck + biome green.
  - **Finding 5 fixed (fail closed).** `types/github.ts` `githubPermissionSchema` now `.catch('all')` instead of `.catch('write')`. These are approval levels, not access tiers (`all` = ask before everything, `write` = ask before writes/deletes, `never` = never ask); there is no `read`. So the fail-closed default on a corrupted/absent value is `all` (maximum approval friction), never a value that would let writes through unasked.
  - **Finding 9 fixed.** MCP App Home buttons dropped the bot-wide catch-all `onAction`. Configure/Remove now use static `action_id`s (`ids.configure`/`ids.remove`) with the server name in the button `value`, and two precise `bot.onAction(id, ...)` handlers read `event.value`, matching the scheduled-tasks pattern. Deleted `openServerAction` and the `${id} ${name}` action-id encoding. A catch-all runs for every action across the whole bot; this removes that.
  - **Working-model cache collapsed to one shared key** (`lib/working-model.ts` `CACHE_KEY = 'working-model'`), dropping the per-agent `agentKey` from `recallModel`/`rememberModel`/`preferLastWorking` and their call sites. The orchestrator, research and explore share one ladder, so "which model is answering" is one global fact; `preferLastWorking` ignores a slug absent from a given agent's own list, so a shared value can never mis-order an agent that lacks that model. `agentKey` still feeds `opencode`'s `x-opencode-session` fallback and the per-agent processor id, so it stays there. Global scope kept (not per-thread: per-thread would re-probe a known-down provider on every new thread); 30 min TTL kept.
  - Fixed two `as {…}` casts in `processors/working-model.ts` `providerOf` (missed by the earlier sweep whose regex only caught `as <Capital>`), replaced with a Zod `safeParse`.
  - **`<mcp_status>` renamed to `<mcps>`** in the orchestrator's failed-server system message, matching the earlier `<github_status>` to `<github>` rename.
  - Second `/no-comments` pass over the updated diff: killed 2 more what-comments (the `working-model` shared-key rationale and the `fitHome` narration, both added earlier this session). Applied its reshape flags instead of restoring the prose: `CACHE_KEY` -> `SHARED_WORKING_MODEL_KEY` (name now says the key is shared across agents) and `MAX_HOME_BLOCKS` -> `SLACK_APP_HOME_BLOCK_LIMIT` (name now carries the platform origin). All other comments kept as external gotchas.

- **[Audit / review]** Opus max-effort deep dive of the sandbox-lifecycle refactor + a second whole-branch pass, then owner-directed fixes.
  - Verdict on the sandbox refactor: **safe, and the abort/error pause genuinely fires (not a no-op)**. Verified against Mastra source that the `requestContext` closed over by `onAbort`/`onError` is the same object instance tools tag in the `reached` WeakSet (one RequestContext per message, mutated in place, never cloned, flowing unchanged through `defaultOptions`). Semantic equivalence preserved, double-pause harmless (2nd call hits a cleared cache -> `SandboxNotReadyError`, swallowed, VM stays paused), no import cycle.
  - **R1 fixed: wrong-repo path collision.** `checkout.ts` and `push.ts` derived the sandbox path from the repo basename (`repository.split('/')[1]`), so `facebook/react` and `myfork/react` collided and `test -d .git || git clone` skipped the second clone, operating on the first repo while reporting the second. Now `${workdir}/${repository.replace('/', '__')}` (owner+repo), identical in both files.
  - **R2 fixed: `get_slack_file` broke for DM'd files.** The readable check used only `channels`/`groups`; a file DM'd to the bot has empty channels/groups but populated `ims`, so it threw "not associated with a channel" (regression vs main). Added `...(fileInfo?.ims ?? [])`. Safe because an im is never workspace-visible, so `assertReadableChannel` admits it only when it equals the current thread.
  - **#5 fixed: GitHub credential window scoping.** The E2B egress proxy attaches the user's token to all github.com traffic for the duration of `withCredential`'s operation (a confused-deputy window; token never enters the VM, so it is host-side only). (1) `checkout.ts` now wraps only the git egress in `withCredential` and runs the host-side `repoPushAccess`/`githubAccess` checks outside, shrinking the window. (2) `git.ts` drop now retries `updateNetwork(baseRules())` up to 3 times before giving up (owner rejected trashing the sandbox on a failed drop: it can hold important work; retries chosen over recycle, residual risk accepted). Per-request scoping not done: E2B transforms are host-keyed, not request-keyed.
  - **Rejected as false positive:** the reviewer's langfuse "unhandled rejection" finding: `score.create()` is synchronous `void` (enqueue), not a promise, so nothing to reject; only `flush()` is async and is already awaited + caught.
  - **Still open, owner action:** R3/R4 need a models.dev check (the four orchestrator ladder models plus `mimo-v2.5` must each hold >=1M input, and every orchestrator model's output cap must be >= the 65,536 requested, ideally strictly above per AGENTS.md; cannot verify offline). Open hypothesis not yet investigated: a concurrent-turn race (a `wait`/scheduled wake overlapping a live turn on the same thread running concurrent `resolveSandbox`/`pause`/`clearSandboxCache`) as a possible cause of the observed background-job recycle/wipe, though the `too_many_images` turn errors are the more likely confound in that thread.

- **[Known issues]** Close the canvas tool scope hole. `read_canvas`, `lookup_canvas_sections`, `edit_canvas` and `list_canvases` take a bare canvas id and act on it with the bot token with no visibility check at all. Verified 2026-09-12: `assertCanManageChannel` (`tools/canvas/utils.ts:13`) exists in that directory but is wired only into `create.ts:46,69`, and a grep for the guards across `tools/` returns nothing for `read.ts`, `edit.ts`, `list.ts` or `sections.ts`. Every other read tool in the repo does call `assertReadableChannel`, which refuses anything whose `channelVisibility !== 'workspace'`, so this is an inconsistency rather than a deliberate policy. `list_canvases` with `scope: 'workspace'` is the enumeration half: `list.ts:75-78` sets `id` to `undefined` for that scope and then omits `channel` from the `files.list` call, so it returns every canvas the bot token can see, ids included. The manifest grants `canvases:read`, `canvases:write`, `groups:read` and `channels:join`, and the bot accumulates public channel membership automatically, so the reachable version is a workspace member DMing the bot, listing workspace canvases, and reading or silently rewriting one that lives in a private channel they are not in. `read_canvas`, `list_canvases` and `lookup_canvas_sections` are always loaded through `codeTools()` behind the always-present `slack` code-mode tool (`tools/code-mode/slack.ts:27-29`), and `edit_canvas` is `autoLoad: true`. Fix is to resolve the canvas's channel from `files.info` and run it through `assertReadableChannel`, or `assertCanManageChannel` for the edit, and to drop or gate `scope: 'workspace'`.

- **[Known issues]** Bind `get_slack_file` to a conversation the asker can see. `tools/slack/get-slack-file.ts:62` calls `files.info({ file: fileId })` on the bot token and downloads `url_private_download` into the per-thread sandbox, where `read_file` hands the contents back to the model and so to the user. Nothing checks that the file belongs to the current thread or to a channel the requesting user can read, and the tool is in `orchestratorTools()` so it is always available. Same confused-deputy shape as the canvas hole: a file id lifted from a permalink or surfaced by injected content pulls a private-channel file into a DM. Fix is to resolve `fileInfo.channels`/`groups`/`ims` and pass it through `assertReadableChannel`, or to restrict it to files attached to the current thread.

- **[Known issues]** Make `assertCanPostTo` enforce the rule its callers' descriptions already claim. `tools/slack/utils.ts:31-46` throws only on the `target.type === 'user'` branch; channel and thread targets fall straight through unchecked. Both `post_message` (`:56`) and `upload_file` (`:32`) tell the model that "channel and thread targets must be in the channel this conversation is already in", and nothing enforces it, while `joinChannel` runs first and the bot holds `channels:join`, so the bot joins the target public channel on demand. Neither tool requires approval. The reason this is worse than a stray post: `post-message.ts:94-102` sets `username` to `${requester} [${bot}]` and `icon_url` to the requester's own avatar, so an injected instruction reaching the agent through a fetched page, a GitHub issue body, an MCP result or another user's message in a followed thread posts into an arbitrary channel wearing that person's name and face. Chained with the `get_slack_file` item above it becomes private-channel file out, public channel in. Fix is to compare `rawId` of channel and thread targets against `ctx.channelId`, the way `assertCanManageChannel` already does.

- **[Known issues]** Re-validate MCP URLs on every connect, not just on add. `mcp/security.ts:14-42` resolves the hostname once and rejects private and reserved addresses, but the real connection is a second resolution and every later reconnect through `resolveClient` to `buildClient` (`mcp/user-servers/client.ts:49-78`) does a bare `new URL(server.url)` with no revalidation. An allowed user can add a host with a short TTL record that answers public during validation and `169.254.169.254` afterwards, and the bot host connects there every turn. Redirect-based SSRF is already blocked by `allowedHosts: [url.host]` (`client.ts:18`) through `@mastra/mcp`'s url-policy, and the IP-literal, localhost, hex and decimal forms are all correctly rejected, so same-host rebinding is the only live path. Either pin the validated IP and carry the hostname in SNI and Host, or re-run `findMCPUrlError` inside `buildClient`. Done 2026-09-13: `mcp/user-servers/client.ts` `buildClient` now runs `findMCPUrlError` on every server at connect (re-resolving the hostname), drops any that fail, and records the reason via `setMCPServerError` so App Home shows it. Closes the DNS-rebinding SSRF window where a host public at add time is later re-pointed internal.

- **[Known issues]** Filter `list_channels` by visibility. `tools/slack/list-channels.ts:40-61` passes `types: 'public_channel,private_channel'` with no filter, so it returns name, topic, purpose and member count for every private channel the bot sits in, to any user.

- **[Known issues]** Fix `scripts/traces.ts`, four bugs found by capturing the TUI through a pty rather than by reading the code. Traces with no `channel_externalThreadId` are processor spans and background runs, and each was listing as its own `?` row with no channel, user or title; excluded now unless `--orphans` is passed, which took the list from 4339 rows of noise to 687 real threads. A paste arrives as one `data` chunk and the handler only accepted `key.length === 1`, so a pasted Slack link, the lookup this tool exists for, was silently dropped whole; it now takes every printable character in the chunk. The row budget was `rows - 4` against three chrome lines plus a trailing newline, which came to exactly the terminal height and scrolled every frame by one, leaving stale rows that looked like a failed clear; it is `rows - 5` now with the header truncated so it cannot wrap. The earlier guess that `\u001b[2J` was not reaching the terminal was wrong, the pty capture shows it emitted correctly both times. The header counted the 500-row cap as the total, so it reads `N of M` now. `q` quits only on an empty filter, since it is also a letter people type. Separately `load()` is incremental: a full index of 4339 traces takes 81s and was re-running on every invocation while the download kept adding files, so now only unseen files are parsed and a warm run is 0.09s.

- **[Known issues]** Add an `assertCanPostTo`-style destination restriction to `react.ts` (currently the only Slack write tool without one).

- **[Known issues]** Parallel `upload_file` calls can interleave file and comment ordering across concurrent uploads (the `chat` package uploads then posts text sequentially per call, with no cross-call ordering guarantee). **Stale, closed 2026-09-12 without a code change.** The premise is real but describes a path gorkie does not take. Confirmed in `@chat-adapter/slack/dist/index.js:3331-3332`: `postMessage` really does `uploadFiles(...)` and then a separate `chat.postMessage(...)`, so two concurrent calls through that API interleave as upload A, upload B, post A, post B. gorkie never reaches it. `upload_file` is the only uploader in `src/` (grep for `files.upload`, `getUploadURLExternal`, `completeUploadExternal` returns `tools/slack/upload-file.ts` alone) and nothing anywhere calls `thread.post({ files })`, which is what `extractFiles` keys on. Our path finishes with a single `completeUploadExternal({ files, initial_comment, thread_ts })`, so Slack publishes the file and its comment as one message and they cannot separate. What remains is a different thing and not this bug: the agent can call `post_message` and `upload_file` in the same parallel batch, and those are two independent messages whose order is genuinely undefined. That is inherent to parallel tool calls and the lever is prompt guidance or sequencing, not the upload path. Reopen only with a thread showing a comment attached to the wrong file.

- **[Known issues]** Split the output cap, which was one number serving two ladders with different floors. Measured against models.dev on 2026-09-12: the orchestrator ladder's lowest output cap is 131,072 (`glm-5.3-flash` on both routes, `muse-spark-1.3-contributor`), with `deepseek-v4-flash-vision-exp` at 384,000 and `mimo-v2.5` at 128,000. The 65,536 floor that forced 32,768 belongs to `google/gemini-3.5-flash-lite`, which is in the summarizer ladder and not in the orchestrator's at all, so the orchestrator had been capped by a constraint from a model it never runs. Now `maxTokens.output` is 65,536 for the orchestrator and `maxTokens.summarizer` is 32,768 for `agents/summarizer.ts` and the OM observer and reflector, which all resolve `summarizerModel`. The orchestrator number is still below its own 131,072 floor on purpose: a Slack reply is short, and the headroom exists for reasoning tokens, which count against the same budget under `reasoning: 'medium'`. Raising it to 131,072 is a one-token change if reasoning turns out to be getting truncated.

- **[Known issues]** A zip uploaded by gorkie came back corrupt: "Unable to expand 9709-P1-questions-by-topic (1).zip. (Error 94 - Bad message.)" from macOS Archive Utility, which is what a truncated archive looks like. Strong hypothesis, not yet reproduced, and it points straight at the streaming upload rewrite in `tools/slack/upload-file.ts`. Ruled out first: E2B's `files.read(path, { format: 'stream' })` is typed `Promise<ReadableStream<Uint8Array>>` (`e2b/dist/index.d.ts:5301-5303`), so it is raw bytes and not a text-decoded stream, which would have been the obvious way to mangle a binary. The mechanism that does fit is the stream idle timeout. `FilesystemReadOpts.streamIdleTimeoutMs` "defaults to the request timeout (60s)" and the same doc comment adds that "a consumer that holds the stream but stops reading is reclaimed server-side" (`:5179-5185`, applied at `dist/index.js:1626`). We hand that stream straight to `fetch`, so E2B is drained at exactly the rate Slack accepts bytes; a large file over a slow link stalls past the window and E2B ends the body early. Nothing downstream notices: `sent.ok` only checks the HTTP status, Slack was told `length: stat.size` at `getUploadURLExternal` but does not hard-fail a short body, and `completeUploadExternal` then publishes the truncated file. That also explains why images were fine and a big zip was not, and why the one live test that was run (34.8MB, `fileId: F0C1J2W72J0`) looked like a pass: it only proved the upload completed, never that the bytes matched. Cheap confirmation before fixing anything: `files.info` on the Slack file id returns the stored size, so compare it against the original in the sandbox, or just compare the downloaded zip's size against what was uploaded. Three candidate fixes, and the first is worth doing whichever cause it turns out to be. One, verify after upload by comparing Slack's stored size against `stat.size` and throwing on a mismatch, which converts silent corruption into a visible error for every cause rather than this one. Two, pass `streamIdleTimeoutMs: 0` to disable the abort, noting the doc implies the server-side reclaim may be separate from the client-side timer. Three, stream only above some size and send a buffer with a real `Content-Length` below it, since a stream body forces chunked encoding and gives Slack no length to check against. Related: the existing open item to verify streaming upload against real Slack is now upgraded from untested to probably broken. **Fixed 2026-09-12**, detection first and cause second, because the detection is correct whatever the cause turns out to be. The stream is now piped through a counting `TransformStream` and the byte total is compared against `stat.size` *before* `completeUploadExternal` runs, so a short transfer throws and nothing is posted: an unfinished Slack upload id expires by itself, a published corrupt file does not. `streamIdleTimeoutMs: 0` was also passed to the E2B read, since `createIdleAbort` treats 0 as disabled (`e2b/dist/index.js:952`, `if (!idleTimeoutMs) return`). Worth recording that the client-side idle timer was probably never the whole story: `wrapStreamWithConnectionCleanup` (`:1005-1019`) arms the timer inside `pull` and clears it when the chunk lands, so it measures server slowness rather than consumer slowness, and `fetch` simply does not pull while Slack is busy. The mechanism that actually bites is the server-side reclaim the E2B docs mention, which surfaces as `done: true` on the next read, a clean end of stream with no error, which is exactly why this was silent. Verified the guard in isolation against a stream that closes cleanly partway: 5,000,000 of 5,000,000 publishes, 3,100,000 of 5,000,000 throws, 0 of 5,000,000 throws. Also checked the API the owner remembered: the chat SDK does expose `thread.post({ files: [...] })`, but `FileUpload.data` is typed `Buffer | Blob | ArrayBuffer` (`chat/dist/types-BtpYwkzQ.d.ts:1848-1855`), so it buffers the whole file, which is the thing the 1 GB cap required moving away from. Not usable here without giving up streaming. Still unproven against real Slack: whether a large upload now succeeds or merely fails loudly. If it fails loudly and often, the next lever is sending a real `Content-Length` below some size threshold and streaming only above it.

- **[Known issues]** The E2B sandbox has 1 GB of RAM, which is almost certainly why it "runs out of memory easily" and a strong candidate for the issue 35 report that gorkie's sandbox freezes compiling apps. `src/mastra/workspace/build-template.ts:76-77` builds `gorkie-workspace` with `cpuCount: 2, memoryMB: 1024`, and `memoryMB` is a template-build option (`BasicBuildOptions`, `e2b/dist/index.d.ts:10098`) rather than something `Sandbox.create` can raise per run, so changing it means rebuilding the template and bumping `config.template` past `gorkie-workspace:2.0`. 1 GB has to hold cloakbrowser, a bun or npm install, and a build at once, and an OOM there surfaces as `Killed` or exit 137 rather than a compile error, which is exactly the shape of a "frozen" sandbox. Prompt guidance added to `prompts/features/sandbox.ts` in the meantime: name the budget, say what an OOM looks like so the agent stops reporting the tool as broken, reduce parallelism before looking for more memory (`-j1`, `--max-old-space-size`, close `agent-browser` first), and only then try a swapfile, with an explicit instruction not to retry `swapon` if the VM refuses it. Checked the comparison the owner asked for and the answer is a flat no: neither project adds swap and neither sets memory. `coolton/build_sandbox_template.py:149` calls `Template.build(template, TEMPLATE_NAME, skip_cache=True, on_build_logs=_log)` with no resource options, and kyto's `Sandbox.create(config.template, { timeoutMs, ... })` (`packages/sandbox/src/lazy-sandbox.ts:156`) passes none either. So if their sandboxes really do compile apps that ours cannot, memory is not the difference and the parity item needs a different explanation. Still open and deliberately not done here: actually rebuilding the template at a higher `memoryMB`, which costs money and needs the owner to run it.

- **[Known issues]** Scrap the App Home build header and the git commit helper. Done 2026-09-12 at the owner's call on the grounds that shelling out to git from the running bot is not worth the footer it produces. `src/mastra/lib/version.ts` deleted, its `commit` import and the `Running on <sha> · <subject>` context block removed from `chat/app-home/view.ts`. Worth recording why the file kept causing trouble on the way out: its `catch {}` was the one place ultracite's `noEmptyBlockStatements` could only be satisfied by a comment, since it also rejects `return undefined` (`noUselessUndefined`) and bare `return` (`noUselessReturn`), so every no-comments sweep broke `bun run check` until the file was deleted. If a build identifier is wanted again, read it from an env var stamped at build time rather than from git at runtime.

- **[Known issues]** Code mode's call budget covers three tools out of twelve, and the gap is one line each. `prompts/features/code-mode.ts` tells the model "a turn that runs past its call budget is cut off mid-program, losing every page it had read but not yet written down", and that sentence is true, contrary to what this entry said when first written. `lib/slack-budget.ts` holds a 200-call-per-turn cap in a `WeakMap` keyed on the `RequestContext`, and it reaches code mode intact: `createCodeModeTool`'s `dispatch` forwards `requestContext: ctx?.requestContext` unchanged to each wrapped tool (`@mastra/core/dist/tools-mf1brz6C.js:498`), so the same object is the same WeakMap key and a script's `external_*` calls spend the same budget the agent's direct calls do. The real gap is coverage. Only `search_slack`, `read_conversation_history` and `list_threads` call `spendSlackCall`. The other nine tools in `codeTools()`, `get_user`, `get_channel_info`, `list_channels`, `get_permalink`, `get_slack_file`, `summarize_thread`, `list_canvases`, `read_canvas` and `lookup_canvas_sections`, spend nothing, so a loop over `external_get_user` is unbounded. `CodeModeConfig` itself carries only `tools`, `sandbox`, `timeout` and `id` (`tools/code-mode/types.d.ts:20-31`), so nothing upstream would ever cap this; the fix is ours and it is one `spendSlackCall(context?.requestContext)` per tool. **Done 2026-09-12**, all twelve now spend: the six Slack reads (`get_user`, `get_channel_info`, `list_channels`, `get_permalink`, `get_slack_file`, `summarize_thread`) and the three canvas reads (`list_canvases`, `read_canvas`, `lookup_canvas_sections`) joined the three that already did. Four of them (`get-user.ts`, `list-channels.ts`, `canvas/read.ts`, `canvas/sections.ts`) had no `context` parameter on `execute` at all and had to take one. `canvas/sections.ts` spends once at the top rather than in each branch, because exactly one `canvases.sections.lookup` runs either way. Checked the comparison again and neither project has anything like this: coolton's only rate limiter is a global 15 second interval guarding **Cerebras model calls** (`agent/agent.py:94`, called once at `:2832`), not Slack, and kyto has no Slack call budget at all, its budgets are for model tokens and for a shared daily quota on escalating to an expensive model (`tools/upgrade-model.ts`). So our 200-per-turn cap is the most developed of the three and the remaining question is only whether 200 is the right number against a Tier 3 limit of roughly 50 a minute shared workspace-wide. The only real bound is `sandboxConfig.timeout`, 8 minutes, so a generated script can make as many `external_*` round trips as fit in that window. At Slack's Tier 3 of roughly 50 requests a minute that is several hundred calls against a workspace limit shared with every other Slack user, which is the owner's actual concern with code mode, security having turned out to be the lesser one. Worth considering alongside it: 200 calls in one turn is still a lot against a Tier 3 limit of roughly 50 a minute shared with every other Slack user, so a minimum interval between calls would stop a tight loop bursting even while under budget. Also note `call_slack_api`, deleted in `316b089`, was the most careful consumer of this budget and is worth re-reading before rebuilding anything similar: it allowlisted read methods by final segment via regex, Zod-parsed the response, decoded `slack:C123:456.789` ids into channel and ts params, and capped the model-facing preview at 16 KB while writing the full body to the sandbox.

- **[Known issues]** MCP add showed a generic failure but stored the server anyway; fixed 2026-09-13. Cause: `addServer` in `app-home/mcp/actions.ts` ran the connection probe (`findMCPConnectionError`, up to a 2s connect plus MCPClient overhead) synchronously before `upsertMCPServer`, which can exceed Slack's modal-submit ack window, so Slack rendered a generic "we had some trouble connecting" while the handler kept running and completed the add. Now the SSRF URL check stays synchronous, the server is upserted immediately (fast ack), and the connection probe runs in the background, writing its result as the row's `lastError` and re-publishing Home, matching how reconnect status already works. A failed server therefore ADDS and shows its real reason on the row (e.g. Fathom's "No authorization provided") instead of a false failure. Note this does not fix Fathom's actual auth, that is the separate known issue about the token not being accepted / OAuth.

- **[Known issues]** The MCP remove confirm dialog showed literal `*fathom*`. Slack confirmation dialogs do not reliably render mrkdwn bold, so it printed the asterisks. Changed to `plain_text` without markup: "This removes <name> and its stored token." (`app-home/mcp/blocks.ts`).

- **[Architecture]** Decide whether gorkie needs both Observational Memory and working memory. It does not, and working memory has been removed. Three findings, in order of weight. First, compaction: Mastra ships none ("Mastra doesn't provide compaction out of the box", `docs-guides-context-engineering.md:264`) and OM is the replacement, not a partial one. The worry that OM only buffers is wrong: async buffering is a latency optimisation, and `reference-memory-observational-memory.md:94` states "A synchronous (blocking) observation runs when the messageTokens threshold is reached and buffered activation did not happen", with reflection falling back to a blocking run above its own `blockAfter` (~48k). So history cannot grow unbounded while the buffer lags; worst case a turn pays for a synchronous observation. Second, `resourceId` in a channels-backed thread is frozen to whoever created the thread, which kills resource-scoped working memory for shared Slack threads: `agent-CEHR0Wd8.js:21195` computes `defaultResourceId` as `${platform}:${message.author.userId}`, but `resolveChannelResourceId` is documented to run "only when a new thread is actually created, never when reusing an existing one (which keeps its stored owner)", and line 21215 then reads `mastraThread.resourceId` back off the stored thread and line 21342 passes it as `memory.resource` for every later turn. A resource-scoped profile would therefore file Bob's facts under Alice's id for the entire life of a thread Alice opened. Correct only in DMs. Third, the thread-scoped participants roster that was briefly in place was redundant: channels already prefixes every history line with author plus mention (`chat/history.ts`) and OM writes speaker-attributed observations natively. With `manageWorkingMemory: true` the roster also cost a `WorkingMemoryExtractor` pass on every observation cycle to maintain a blob nothing read.

- **[Architecture]** Installed the `typescript-best-practices` skill from `cursor/plugins` (2026-09-13), asked for alongside the existing `unslop` skill from the same pstack. `npx skills add cursor/plugins -s typescript-best-practices -a claude-code -y`. Two things to know for the next install: the agent id is `claude-code`, not `claude` (the CLI rejects `claude` outright and prints the valid list), and skills@1.5.26 copies the skill into `.claude/skills/` rather than symlinking, which does not match how every other skill here is laid out. Moved it to `.agents/skills/typescript-best-practices` and symlinked it back into `.claude/skills/`, matching the rest. The skill declares `paths: ["**/*.ts", "**/*.tsx"]` with `disable-model-invocation: true`, so it loads on TS file access rather than by name, and it carries a `references/patterns.md`. `skills-lock.json` records it; the lockfile does not record copy versus symlink, so `experimental_install` would restore it as a copy again.

- **[Ideas]** `taskContext` DB waste removed 2026-09-13. It ran a Postgres `listThreads` query on every scheduled-task and wait call to derive a thread id that channels already binds onto the tool context. The helper was two field reads once the query went, so it was deleted outright and inlined at all three call sites (`scheduled-tasks/create.ts`, `list.ts`, `wait.ts`): `context.agent?.threadId` and `context.agent?.resourceId`, throwing the same missing-context error if absent. `memoryThread()` stays for `chat/commands/stop.ts`, which only has an external thread id. Also dropped the dead `resolvedAgent`/`ctx` return fields and their `getAgentById`/`channelContext` work.

- **[Ideas]** `stepMs` step timeout added 2026-09-13. `config.agent.stepTimeoutMs = 2 * 60 * 1000`, wired as `modelSettings.timeout: { stepMs }` on orchestrator, research and explore. A stepMs timeout is not retried against the same model, it advances to the next fallback, so a provider that opens a stream then stalls no longer hangs the turn. `firstChunkMs` is the tighter variant if the 2min cap proves too loose for a wedged-before-first-token provider.

- **[Ideas]** Added `!help` and `!mcps` thread commands 2026-09-13 (`chat/commands/help.ts`, `mcps.ts`, registered in `commands/index.ts`). `!help` lists the commands ephemerally; `!mcps` lists the caller's MCP servers with connected/failed status and points to App Home. Same in-thread `!command` + ephemeral pattern as `!stop`. Referenced `techwithanirudh/gork-slack`'s help command for the shape (it uses Bolt slash-commands, so structure only, not code).

- **[Ideas]** Stop hand-maintaining the 33-name `EXPOSED` allowlist in `tools/github/index.ts` and derive it from the SDK. `@github-tools/sdk` exports the values needed: `GITHUB_TOOL_NAMES` (all ~79 names), `ALL_GITHUB_TOOL_NAMES`, `PRESET_TOOLS` (curated groups: `code-review`, `issue-triage`, and more) with `resolvePresetTools()`, and `GITHUB_WRITE_TOOLS` (already imported here for read/write approval classification). Three options, in ascending effort/behavior-change: (a) cheapest, keep the list but assert it against `ALL_GITHUB_TOOL_NAMES` at module load so a renamed or removed SDK tool fails fast rather than silently disappearing (today it is typed `GithubToolName[]`, which catches typos at compile time but not runtime removals); (b) compose `EXPOSED` from `resolvePresetTools(['code-review','issue-triage', ...])` so the curation comes from the SDK, noting the presets do not match our current 33 exactly (they add createLabel/deleteLabel/reactions we omit), so this changes the exposed surface and needs a review pass; (c) all of `GITHUB_TOOL_NAMES` minus an exclusion list, rejected because our omissions (gists, releases, workflow triggers, notifications, reactions, createRepository, createOrUpdateFile which is an API file write we forbid) are the whole point. There is no pure import because `EXPOSED` encodes policy, not just names; the win is expressing it as presets-minus-exceptions and getting compile+runtime validation for free. Do (a) now-ish regardless of the search-gating decision. **Done 2026-09-13, and the investigation resolved what can and cannot be derived.** Proven: no SDK preset or rule reproduces the exact 33 (best two-preset union misses `createPullRequest`/`forkRepository` and adds 15 extras; `maintainer` is all 79). So membership stays a curated allowlist, on purpose, because an allowlist fails safe (a new SDK tool, including a destructive one, is not surfaced until opted in) whereas a denylist fails open and would also re-inflate the 8,665-token prompt. What IS now derived from the SDK: names are `GithubToolName` (a rename breaks `typecheck`), and the read/write split is split into `READ_TOOLS` (20) and `WRITE_TOOLS` (13) with a load-time assertion that each agrees with the SDK's own `GITHUB_WRITE_TOOLS` (`isWriteTool = name in GITHUB_WRITE_TOOLS`), so if the SDK ever reclassifies a tool the build throws at startup rather than silently mis-gating its approval. The per-tool `kind` now reuses `isWriteTool`. Net: the security-relevant classification is SDK-checked and legible, the surface is unchanged, and the one thing that genuinely cannot be auto-derived, the policy of which tools to expose, is documented as such.

- **[Ideas]** Make `github_checkout` return the write path so tool choice after a clone is deterministic, not prompt-dependent, and make it reflect actual write access to the specific repo. Done 2026-09-13 (extended, first version wrongly assumed push always worked). The result now carries a `writing` field naming the exact next tools (`github_push_branch` then `github_create_pull_request`) and stating that nothing writes through the API. `github_checkout` is only ever registered in the `direct` branch alongside `github_push_branch` (`tools/github/index.ts:109-118`), so its presence already proves the credential can push, which is why the affordance is unconditional and needs no capability check. This also de-risks moving GitHub tools behind tool search: once checkout names the next tools by id, the model knows exactly what to `search_tools`/`load_tool` for, rather than relying on `prompts/tools.ts:26` still being in a compressed context. Kept `prompts/tools.ts:26` as the belt-and-braces statement of the same flow. Corrected same day after the owner pointed out the affordance must reflect write access to that repo, not just that a credential exists: `github_checkout` being in the `direct` branch proves the credential is usable, not that it can push to the *specific* repo. Checkout now reads `permissions.push` via a new `repoPushAccess` in `lib/github/api.ts` (`GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}`) and branches the `writing` field three ways: push access -> push a branch to origin then PR; no push but PAT -> fork with `github_fork_repository`, push to the fork, cross-repo PR; no push and app credential -> cannot fork (an app only pushes where installed), so read/issues only, say so rather than attempting a doomed push. `canFork` (`credential.kind === 'pat'`) is inferred inside the tool via the request-memoized `githubAccess` (a cache hit, and only called in the no-push branch), not passed in, so the tool resolves its own capability and the registration site stays at two params. The guidance is inlined, no one-use helper. This is the fork-vs-push decision made at checkout time instead of the model discovering it via a failed push. Cost: one extra repo API call per checkout.

- **[Product]** Issue 35, the "this thread stopped midway" comment is diagnosed. It was not the model and not the provider: `gorkie-monitor.service` restarted the bot mid-turn and the app, having no SIGTERM handler, was SIGKILLed 90 seconds later. A second instance is the 9709 study-PDF thread of 2026-09-07, killed 5 minutes in at `09:29:33Z` and silent for 70 minutes until someone poked it. Neither turn left a trace, because the platform only lists traces whose root span completed. Full write-up in `ISSUES.md` sections E and G, fixes applied in `IMPLEMENTED.md` 2026-09-12.

- **[Product]** Point PR 13 (Izie's Slack search, `IzieStratt:main` into `techwithanirudh:main`) at `github-setup` instead of `main`, without closing it. Done 2026-09-13 with `gh pr edit 13 --base github-setup`, a GitHub-side base change only: PR still open, 7 commits and 10 changed files unchanged, nothing pushed, the fork untouched. No rebase was needed because `origin/main` (7fcc03a) is an ancestor of `origin/github-setup` and the PR head (0ce9445) already merged current main in, so the merge base against `github-setup` is still 7fcc03a and the diff is exactly the PR's own work. Rejected: rebasing the 6 commits onto `github-setup`, which would have meant force-pushing `IzieStratt:main`, the contributor's fork default branch, rewriting their history and marking their review comments outdated, all to produce a diff that was already clean.

- **[Product]** Add back the join-thread tool. Done 2026-09-12: `tools/slack/join-thread.ts`, registered in `tools/slack/index.ts` and `toolsets.ts`, the mirror of `leave_thread`. It `subscribe()`s and sets `respondOnThreadMessages: true`, undoing exactly what leave did, `unsubscribe()` and `respondOnThreadMessages: false`. This is the gap coolton has a store for: once the bot leaves a thread it only wakes on a direct @mention of the thread's first message, so without a rejoin "come back" has no effect. Not deferred, it lives in `orchestratorTools()` so it is always resolvable.

## 2026-09-12

- **[Known issues]** Diagnosed why turns die silently, and fixed the cause in production. The reported symptom was a Slack thread that "stopped midway" with no reply for seven hours. It was not a hang and not a model problem. `gorkie-monitor.service` restarted the bot mid-turn, and because the app has no SIGTERM handler systemd SIGKILLed it 90 seconds later, destroying the in-flight turn and the buffered spans with it.

  Timeline, read off the production journal: `23:57:49` turn started; `00:02:01` `socket-mode:SlackWebSocket: A pong wasn't received from the server before the timeout of 5000ms!`, which is an independent subsystem reporting the event loop stalled past five seconds; `00:03:07` `health check failed (2/2)` and `systemctl restart`; ninety seconds of the app logging nothing at all; `00:04:37` `State 'stop-sigterm' timed out. Killing.`; `00:04:38` restart, online at `00:04:45`; zero turns between `00:05` and `00:30`.

  Why the loop stalled: the unit had `MemoryHigh=1G` and `MemoryCurrent` sat at `1073340416`, exactly on that line. `MemoryHigh` is a soft cap that makes the kernel continuously reclaim the cgroup's pages into swap, so the process was being swapped out by configuration, then blocking on major page faults to get its own memory back. The counter read 9,062,676 major faults and `/proc/pressure/io` showed `full avg10=42.81`, meaning everything on the box was fully stalled on disk 43% of the time.

  Three things turned out to be false and are worth recording. **Swap was never the constraint**: 5886 MB total with 4499 MB free, so adding swap would only have widened the target for eviction and made the thrashing worse. **The host never rebooted** (`uptime` 9 days) and the corpus-wide 134 minute quiet around the incident is ordinary, the corpus holds ten overnight gaps of 357 to 689 minutes. **The 406 watchdog restarts since 08-13 are not a steady rate**: 342 of them fall on 09-08 and 09-09, a crash loop firing every two minutes, which is a different failure from the healthy-but-stalled case. Steady state is one to four a day.

  Also corrected: adding `timeout` to the agents, which an earlier pass had ranked as the top fix for this, would not have prevented any of it. It guards a stalled provider, not SIGKILL from a watchdog. Still worth doing for the hang class it does cover, and it remains open in `TODO.md`.

- **[Known issues]** **This first attempt was wrong and caused an outage.** Raising `MemoryHigh` to `1400M` set it *equal to* `MemoryMax`, which deleted the reclaim buffer. The old config had `MemoryHigh=1G` against `MemoryMax=1400M`, so the kernel began reclaiming 400 MB before the hard wall: thrashy and slow, but survivable. With both at `1400M` the process grew freely to the hard limit where the only outcome is death, and at `15:42:13` the kernel OOM-killed it: `oom_memcg=/system.slice/gorkie.service`, `Killed process 509298 (bun)`, `anon-rss:1356348kB`, `Failed with result 'oom-kill'`. `Restart=always` brought it back and the in-flight turn was lost, the same user-visible symptom the whole exercise was meant to remove. Traded thrashing for OOM kills.

  Corrected to `MemoryHigh=1250M` with `MemoryMax=1550M`, restoring a 300 MB reclaim zone while still sitting well above the old 1 GB line. The lesson worth keeping: `MemoryHigh` and `MemoryMax` must never be equal. `High` is where the kernel starts working to keep you under; `Max` is where it gives up and kills. With no gap there is no "working to keep you under" phase at all.

  This also proved the footprint reduction is not optional. The process reached `memory.current` 1.47 GB and `memory.events` recorded `high 267382, max 408` before the kill, so it genuinely wants more than the box can give. Raising limits only moves the wall; `excludeSpanTypes` is what lowers the demand.

- **[Known issues]** Raised `MemoryHigh` from `1G` to `1400M` via `systemctl set-property gorkie.service MemoryHigh=1400M`, which applies to the live cgroup with no restart, so no turn was killed applying it. `/proc/pressure/io` went from `full avg10=42.81` to `full avg10=0.38` immediately, with `avg60=17.63` and `avg300=37.08` showing the decay from the old state. Resident swap stays where it was because pages already evicted are only faulted back when touched; what stopped is new eviction. Previous config backed up at `/root/memory.conf.bak.20260912`.

- **[Known issues]** Replaced the liveness monitor so it distinguishes busy from wedged. **Old implementation** (`/root/gorkie-monitor.sh.bak.20260912`): one `curl --max-time 8` against `127.0.0.1:4111` every 60 seconds, restart after `THRESHOLD=2` consecutive failures. That is a sampling test, not a duration test. With the process fully stalled 43% of the time, any single probe was close to a coin flip, and two unlucky samples a minute apart were enough to kill a turn that was working normally. The evidence it was too twitchy: 487 stalls recovered on their own against 406 that escalated.

  **New implementation**: five probes spread across roughly 24 seconds needing only two successes, cross-checked against `usage_usec` in `/sys/fs/cgroup/system.slice/gorkie.service/cpu.stat`. A wedged process burns no CPU; a thrashing but working one does. HTTP failing with CPU advancing means busy, so leave it alone; HTTP failing with CPU flat means wedged, so restart. `THRESHOLD=5` rounds, and `BACKSTOP=15` still catches a spin loop, which would keep CPU high while answering nothing.

  Chose the cgroup counter over the heartbeat file that was proposed first, and the reasoning matters: a heartbeat needs an app change and a deploy, it asks the process to self-report, and writing it requires the very event loop that is stalled. The cgroup counter is maintained by the kernel, needs no code change, and cannot be blocked by the thing it is measuring.

  Verified on the live box in both directions before installing. Healthy service: exit 0 in 24 seconds, service untouched. Six consecutive rounds of total HTTP failure with CPU advancing: zero restarts, logging "busy, not wedged: cpu advanced, leaving it alone". Five rounds with a faked frozen cgroup: restart fired correctly at the threshold.

- **[Architecture]** Set `excludeSpanTypes: [SpanType.PROCESSOR_RUN]` in `src/mastra/index.ts`. Committed to the repo only, not yet deployed. `deepClean` runs in the span **constructor** (`node_modules/@mastra/observability/dist/index.js:2897-2900`), deep-copying `input` and holding it on the span for the lifetime of the trace. `excludeSpanTypes` is checked in that same constructor and returns before any of it (`:2894-2896`), which Mastra's own comment on `isExcluded` describes as avoiding "both the deepClean cost and holding references to large payloads for the lifetime of the span".

  This reversed an earlier recommendation in `ISSUES.md` that preferred `customSpanFormatter` on the grounds that it keeps the spans and drops only the payload. That reasoning holds for storage and bandwidth and is wrong for memory: the formatter runs in `BaseExporter.exportTracingEvent` (`:4488-4526`) at export time, long after the constructor already spent the allocation. Memory is what was killing the bot, so the formatter was the wrong lever. Measured against 4337 traces, `processor_run` is 66.6% of span count, so this cuts memory, event-loop time, and billed events (about 126k a month becomes about 42k) together. The cost is losing per-processor latency data and the `stream-error-retry-processor` spans.

## 2026-09-11

- **[Known issues]** Hide GitHub tools that cannot work for the current credential. The `EXPOSED` loop skips `forkRepository` unless the credential is a classic token (`tools/github/index.ts:83-87`), with a why-comment, and the prompt's app-credential branch says forking is impossible rather than advertising it (`prompts/github.ts:29-31`). Fork is the only token-only tool in the surface today.
- **[Known issues]** `isSandboxDeadError` missing E2B's real not-found spelling, resolved upstream. `@mastra/e2b` 0.11.0 now carries the regex itself (`node_modules/@mastra/e2b/dist/index.js:1512`), so our patch is gone: `patches/` holds only the core patch and `patchedDependencies` lists only core. Note the narrower gap survives, `retryOnDead` still cannot recover a *paused* sandbox, which is why `requireSandbox` resumes instead.
- **[Rewrite parity]** Custom instructions are back. Full loop: the App Home tab under `chat/app-home/instructions/`, storage in `user_settings.instructions`, and injection as a `<user_instructions>` system message at `agents/orchestrator.ts:56-77`.
- **[Rewrite parity]** Image generation is back. `generate_image` plus reference-image editing (`tools/generate-image/`), registered in `tools/toolsets.ts`. Still on HCAI, which is why the "image generation off HCAI" item stays open.
- **[Rewrite parity]** Canvas read and edit are back. `create_canvas`, `list_canvases`, `read_canvas`, `edit_canvas` and `lookup_canvas_sections`, with real Slack change operations, wired into both the model and code mode.
- **[Legacy backlog]** Sandbox retries are implemented, via `E2BSandbox.retryOnDead` at roughly 20 call sites across the filesystem, the git tools, the Slack file tools and the lifecycle processor. The paused-sandbox gap is tracked separately.

- **[Known issues]** Reintroduced DuckDB path bug, found by mining deleted `TODO.md` history and fixed. `d979755` ("bork-ify gorkie", 2026-08-16) re-baselined the repo against a template and reverted the 2026-07-05 fix for the `mastra build` disk-space blocker, restoring the bare relative `path: './observability.duckdb'`. Confirmed live: `MASTRA_PROJECT_ROOT` appeared nowhere in `src/`, and a **2.4 GB** `observability.duckdb` plus its WAL were sitting in `src/mastra/public/`, which `mastra build` copies as static assets. Re-anchored, this time through `env.MASTRA_PROJECT_ROOT` so it honours the "never read `process.env` outside `src/env.ts`" rule that the original fix broke. The same commit also renamed the media CompatRule and wiped the entire `Recently completed` section, which is why the record of the original fix was gone.
- **[Known issues]** Restored the safety constraint on `moveToolImages`. Confirmed the rule is one lineage, not two attempts: created 2026-07-04 as `relocateToolResultImages`, renamed 2026-08-16, deleted 2026-08-20, restored today byte-identically. The rename dropped a docblock recording why the rule is scoped to `image/*`: the OpenAI-compatible user-message converter throws `UnsupportedFunctionalityError` for other media types such as PDF, so widening `mediaTypes` would crash the turn rather than merely not working. Re-added as a why-comment rather than JSDoc. **Corrected 2026-09-12**: the restored docblock's PDF example is wrong and was carried forward from the original without being checked. Read against the installed converter, the user branch has an explicit case for each of `image/*`, `audio/*` with a recognised format, `application/pdf` and `text/*`, and throws only on what is left over, while the OpenRouter provider that `hackclub()` routes through is looser again and falls through to `{ type: 'file' }` without throwing at all. So PDF is precisely the media type that could be widened to safely. The `image/*` scope is still correct, because video and archives really would throw, and it was left unwidened since nobody has asked for tool-result PDFs. The comment in `processors/tool-media.ts` now says what the converter actually does, with the date it was verified.
- **[Simplification]** Rewrote `fitHome`. The old shape ran `allowances()` to build a positional array, then recovered each section's share with `withRows.indexOf(section)` inside a `flatMap`, which is quadratic and breaks silently if two sections are ever reference-equal. Now one function, allowances held in a `Map` keyed on the section itself. Verified equivalent against the old implementation across 4000 randomised layouts with zero mismatches, also asserting the output never exceeds 100 blocks.
- **[Simplification]** Move the data layer from Kysely to Drizzle, 2026-09-11. Done for the migrations, not for the line count: the layer went 411 to 324 lines, and most of that came from deleting the three hand-written table interfaces that duplicated the SQL, not from the queries, which are about the same length either way. What it actually buys is a real migration history. The old `createTables()` ran `createTable().ifNotExists()` plus nine `add column if not exists` statements at boot, an idiom that can only ever add: no renames, no drops, no type changes, no backfills. `tool_display` proved the cost, since removing it from the schema left the column stranded in every database that had run it, with no way to clean up. Now `drizzle-kit generate` diffs the schema and `migrate()` applies on boot. Three things worth knowing. The baseline migration is hand-edited to `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` because every deployed database already has these tables; it is the only migration that should ever be edited by hand. Drizzle shares `postgresStore.pool` rather than opening a second pool, so connection limits stay one budget. And `scripts/postbuild.ts` now copies `drizzle/` into `.mastra/output` alongside `patches/`, without which a built deploy boots and then fails on the first query. `kysely` is removed; `drizzle` is gitignored from Biome since its metadata is generated. Untested against a real database.
- **[Simplification]** Aggressive cleanup of the GitHub surface, 2026-09-11. Three parallel reviews plus fixes. The whole surface went from 1806 to 1480 lines against `main`, 22 files to 19. Four real bugs fixed, each verified in the source rather than taken from the review. One, `git-remote.ts` Zod-parsed `{ result: ... }`, which is `CommandExitError`'s *private* field, working only because TS `private` is not runtime-private; replaced with `instanceof CommandExitError`, which also deleted the schema, the `Result` interface and `failure()`. Two, `checkout` ran the full `validateBranch`, so checking out `main` was refused with "Direct pushes to main are not allowed", contradicting its own description; the protected-branch rule now lives only on push's schema. Three, push's description advertised `github_create_or_update_file`, which is not in `EXPOSED` and does not exist at runtime. Four, App Home's disconnect confirm read "Gorkie forgets  and stops using GitHub." whenever the credential was unreadable, because two `.filter(Boolean).join()` lists over a field that is `'app' | 'pat'` could only ever hold one item anyway. Structurally: `checkout.ts`, `push.ts` and `git-remote.ts` merged into `git.ts`; `parseRepository`, `repoDir`, `remoteUrl`, `validateBranch` and the `Repository` type are gone, inlined or folded into Zod schemas; `utils.ts` renamed `handoff.ts` with its two one-use helpers inlined; `EXPOSED` is typed `GithubToolName[]` so an SDK rename is now a compile error instead of a silently dropped tool, which retires the risk noted below; `BuiltTool`, `WRITES` and `modelOutput` deleted. Also added `requireSandbox` and collapsed seven copies of the same guard onto it. Deliberately not merged: `checkout` and `push` stay two tools, since they differ in description, approval kind, input schema and output shape, and a `mode:` discriminant would fuse two approval units into one. No new dependencies: no SDK preset matches the curated 33, `requireApproval` cannot express the `all` level, and a Block Kit builder would be a third vocabulary.
- **[Simplification]** Re-merged the two `PRESETS` tables, 2026-09-11, into `app-home/presets.ts`. This does not walk back the original split, because the thing that was wrong with the shared module was the `nothingDeletes` flag, which forked one key's copy by consumer. The merged table has one row per rung of `ApprovalLevel` and takes no parameter: each feature indexes the rungs it offers, GitHub through `levelsFor` (`all`, `write`, and `never` only in a DM), MCP through its own `['all', 'write', 'delete']`. Four keys, four honest labels, nothing to pass, so the flag cannot regrow. Worth recording why `never` and `delete` stay separate rather than collapsing into one rung, since they look redundant: they are behaviourally identical for GitHub, because `tools/github/index.ts:94` only ever emits `kind: 'write' | 'read'` and never `'delete'`, so both thresholds gate nothing. They are not identical for MCP, which does emit `'delete'` via `destructiveHint` or a `delete_`/`remove_` prefix. Merging the keys would therefore need one label to describe both a rung that asks and a rung that cannot, which is the fork again. 80 lines to 61, and `presetStatus` is gone from both sides in favour of a direct property read. To be precise, `github/presets.ts` is deleted but `mcp/presets.ts` remains: it still owns `decodePreset`, whose `"<server> <level>"` scope prefix is genuinely MCP-only, and `presetRadio`. Only the copy table moved.
- **[Customization]** Add the `no-comments` skill from `cursor/plugins`, 2026-09-11. Installed with `npx skills add cursor/plugins -s <name> -a '*'` one name at a time, since a comma-separated `-s` silently lists instead of installing. It brought `how`, `why` and `architect` with it, all four in `.agents/skills/` and symlinked into `.claude/skills`, all four in `skills-lock.json`. The `Comment Sicko` subagent it spawns is hand-written at `.claude/agents/comment-sicko.md`, because the CLI installs skills and not agents, so that file is outside the lock and `skills update` will not refresh it. Two named skills are deliberately absent, `principle-fix-root-causes` and `principle-redesign-from-first-principles`: the skill says they "guide intent only", so they are cited rather than invoked, and `architect` cites four more the same way. Add them only if the principle set is wanted for its own sake.
- **[Product]** File the approver-identity gap upstream. Filed 2026-09-11 as mastra-ai/mastra#23687, "[BUG] Channels: any user can answer another user's tool approval card". Note for future `gh` use here: the ambient `GH_TOKEN` is a GitHub App user token restricted to installed repos and cannot open issues on other people's repositories. Run `env -u GH_TOKEN gh ...` to fall back to the stored OAuth token, which can.
- **[Product]** Bump Mastra and rebuild the patch. Done 2026-09-11. All eleven `@mastra/*` packages moved to latest (`core` 1.62.0-alpha.9 to 1.66.0, `deployer` and `server` 1.60.0 to 1.66.0, `e2b` 0.10.0-alpha.3 to 0.11.0, plus memory, pg, duckdb, loggers, mcp, observability). The silent-detach this file warned about happened exactly as described: the bump left `patchedDependencies` keyed on the old version and every one of the seven fixes vanished with no error. Rebuilt against the new content-hashed bundles (`agent-CEHR0Wd8.js`, `agent-BMi0F5ou.cjs`) as `patches/@mastra+core@1.66.0.patch`, 14 hunks. None of the seven had landed upstream, verified against a pristine 1.66.0 tarball. Two needed hand-placing rather than a straight port: the approver-identity block sits one nesting level deeper now, and `_buildRenderContext(chatThread, platform)` has three call sites in 1.66.0, so the requester hunk is anchored on `actor: message.author` to avoid hitting the approval path or the standalone helper. Verified by deleting `node_modules/@mastra/core` and reinstalling: the patch reapplies, both bundles parse under `node --check`, typecheck and lint are clean. Note `bun patch --commit` cannot be used in this environment, it dies on a broken `~/.gitattributes` symlink; the patch was generated with `diff -u` against the pristine tarball instead.

## 2026-08-27

- **[Known issues]** Tell the model not to pass `encoding` when reading an image. Mastra's `read_file` gates media on `if (!encoding && shouldReturnAsMedia(stat.mimeType))` (`workspace-Cd8_MLda.js:8960`), so any encoding value, `utf8` included, drops the file to text and the model gets unreadable bytes. Observed live 2026-08-27: gorkie read a PNG contact sheet with `encoding: utf8`, got bytes, and posted that it could not honestly claim to have read it. Our `E2BFilesystem` is not implicated, `stat()` fills `mimeType` from `lookup()` and the no-encoding path returns a `Buffer`.
