Mastra's own schema already says "Usually omit this" and the model ignored it, so `prompts/tools.ts` now carries a `<media>` block saying it plainly, plus a line telling it not to describe an image a call never returned. Prompt-only, so it reduces the odds rather than making it impossible; the structural fix would be wrapping `read_file` to drop `encoding` when the mime type is media. Not live-tested.
- **[Architecture]** **Channel context.** Done 2026-08-27, then reverted on request apart from the parsing. `lib/context.ts` parses the request context with Zod instead of reading it through an unparsed generic type parameter, and that is all it does now. Both shared requires are gone: `requireChannel({channelId, requestContext})` and `requireThread(requestContext)`. So `get-channel-info`, `list-threads`, `canvas/list`, `canvas/create`, `leave-thread` and the `workspace` sandbox factory are each back to their own presence check and their own refusal wording, which is the specificity the shared version traded away. Three further sites never used either helper because they are not plain requires: `upload-file` (falls back to an explicit target), `react` (channel comes out of a parsed Slack reference), and `summarize-thread` (supplied thread wins over the current one). Watch out when reverting `workspace/index.ts` wholesale: it also carries the `usedSandbox` WeakSet the lifecycle processor imports, and taking that with it breaks the typecheck.
- **[Architecture]** **App Home block limiter.** Done 2026-08-27. `chat/app-home/limit.ts` owns the 100-block cap. Sections now declare `{ fixed, rows, overflow, trailing }` and `fitHome` places rows round-robin across the variable ones, so ten MCP servers no longer squeeze scheduled tasks out. Both variable sections get their own overflow note; previously only scheduled tasks did, and it absorbed every other section's growth. `scheduledTasksBlocks` lost its `maxBlocks` parameter. Checked against 0, 2, 10, 50 servers and up to 200 tasks: never exceeds 100, and neither section starves. Also fixed the missing `hash: view.hash` on all three `views.update` calls (CODING_STANDARDS.md:207), via a new `ViewTarget` from `viewOf`.
- **[Architecture]** **GitHub access.** Done 2026-08-27, jointly with another session. It collapsed the "when does it stop to ask" half into `asksBefore({kind, level})` in `types/approval.ts`. This session did the "where may it run" half: `lib/github/access.ts` returns a `GitHubAccess` union (`unreadable` | `disconnected` | `connected` with `credential`, `direct`, `level`), and `tools/github/index.ts`, `prompts/github.ts` and `app-home/github/presets.ts` are now callers. `levelsFor(outsideDM)` is the single statement of "`never` only applies in a DM", which the UI option list and the runtime coercion had each spelled out separately. A `WeakMap` keyed on `RequestContext` means the tool factory and the status prompt share one read per turn instead of two.
- **[Architecture]** **E2B error translation.** Done 2026-08-27. `attempt({absent, inputPath, run})` in `workspace/filesystem.ts` is now the single place E2B's three spellings of "not found" become a typed error; typed errors raised inside `run` pass through untouched. `infoOrAbsent` covers the two callers that treat a missing path as an answer rather than a failure (`mkdir`, `writeFile` mtime). Nine try/catch blocks became four: `attempt`, `infoOrAbsent`, and the `force` paths in `deleteFile` and `rmdir`. Interface unchanged, `cause()` deleted.
- **[Architecture]** **Import-time construction.** Done 2026-08-27, partially. No top-level `await` remains anywhere in `src/mastra`: `mcpTools()` and `workspaceCodeMode()`/`slackCodeMode()` are memoized lazy factories, `orchestratorTools()` resolves, and `agents/research.ts` takes `instructions` and `tools` as functions the way the orchestrator already did. Verified by importing `mcp/index.ts` with `fetch` instrumented: no network, 213ms.

## 2026-08-26

- **[Architecture]** **Slack references.** Done 2026-08-26. `lib/ids.ts` now owns the Chat SDK `slack:<channel>:<ts>` wire format, Slack permalinks, and bare timestamps behind `parseSlackId`/`threadIdOf`/`chatChannelId`/`rawId`. `lib/slack-message.ts` is absorbed into it and 24 importers are repointed. The hand-rolled parsers are gone: `get-permalink.ts` no longer splits on `:` (it returned garbage where the adapter's `decodeThreadId` throws), `call-api.ts` lost its duplicate `encodedId` regex, `react.ts` and `post-message.ts` parse once, and `slackThreadId` collapsed to a single call. Named `ids` rather than `slack-ref` at the request of the other session's user, who found `-ref` less clear; the same argument was applied to the exports.

## 2026-08-24

- **[Simplification]** Hide `##` comments from the history prefill, and give `read_conversation_history` an `includeComments` opt-in. This reverses the decision recorded two items up, where comments were deliberately injected as context. The reversal is deliberate: a comment is a side remark nobody addressed to gorkie, and injecting it silently let other people's asides steer a turn. The risk is real though, since the 2026-08-24 live test built a correct handoff DM entirely out of `##` comments, and that exact case now depends on the mitigation rather than on the prefill. So the prefill still reports how many it dropped and names the tool and flag that read them back, and `read_conversation_history` returns a `note` sentence rather than dropping messages silently. That field is prose rather than a count on purpose: it is read by the model directly and by generated code in code mode, and a field named for a count that holds a string makes `result.omittedComments > 0` silently false. Not live-tested.
- **[Known issues]** Live-test the every-ping history injection. Confirmed 2026-08-24: the handoff DM named the task ("pin the footer on larger screens, remove the 'Meme GIF maker' text") built entirely from `##` comments that never triggered a turn, so unseen thread messages do reach the model.
- **[Known issues]** `grep` capped line count but not line length, so one match inside a minified file returned a 555KB tool result (~125k tokens; longest single lines 169,575 and 137,757 chars). That single message exceeded `TokenLimiterProcessor`'s remaining budget, and `trimMode: 'contiguous'` breaks at the first message that does not fit while walking newest-first, so it kept nothing and threw `TripWire(retry: false)`, killing the explore run mid-turn (2026-08-24 16:10, trace `881c8b06`). Explore's context was only 25k tokens at the previous step, so this was never accumulation. Fixed with `MAX_LINE_CHARS = 400` in `tools/grep.ts`; replayed against the captured payload, 502KB becomes 15KB.

## 2026-08-23

- **[Known issues]** Thread history is a steering channel. Closed by removing GitHub from threads entirely: every `github_` tool now refuses in a shared thread and hands back a DM to send, so no thread turn ever holds a credential. Focus mode, which only blocked concurrent messages and left prior history untouched, was deleted along with it. Focus starts on the first `github_*` call, so everything said before it, by anyone, is already in memory (`lastMessages: 20`) and is read by the model while it acts with one person's credential. Identity itself holds: `message.author.userId` is platform-verified per message and gorkie refused every impersonation attempt in the 2026-08-23 thread, including a pasted `U07BBQS0Z5J twa (Anirudh Sriram):` prefix. What is open is influence rather than authorship, a third party planting "the fix is X" or "the maintainer approved pushing to main" before the credentialed turn begins. Options: filter non-owner messages out of context for turns holding a credential (strongest, costs real collaboration), wrap every turn in Mastra's `<turn author_id=...>` shape and instruct the model to treat other authors as data (cheap, weaker), or scope GitHub to the thread starter as Devarsh suggested (blunt). Needs a decision before this is trusted in shared channels.

## 2026-07-07

- **[Recently completed]** Subagent stability and delegate long-run failure state, confirmed fixed (2026-07-07). both former P0s (subagents broken in Slack/interruption behavior, and long helper-agent runs showing a generic parent error) are resolved per direct confirmation; no longer tracked as open.
- **[Recently completed]** `post_message`'s DM anchor now mints from its own first post instead of guessing (2026-07-07). refined the third DM-anchor fix attempt (see the "DM tool display" item above). `postMessage`'s override in `chat/adapter.ts` no longer falls back to a `conversations.history` guess when there's no anchor yet, since `postMessage` can post unanchored (a fresh top-level message becomes its own thread root, same as a channel's first message), it just does that, persists the resulting message as the anchor, and corrects the returned `threadId` to reflect it immediately (the base adapter otherwise echoes the caller's original, unanchored threadId back unchanged). `stream()` still needs the `conversations.history` fallback since Slack's streaming API requires a `thread_ts` up front and can't post unanchored the way a plain message can.
- **[Recently completed]** Mid-thread re-mentions were skipping the thread-history backfill, root-caused and fixed (2026-07-07). live-reported bug, pinging gorkie a second time in the middle of a thread (not the thread root) replies but doesn't re-fetch the last 10 messages, even though gorkie never saw what happened between the two mentions. Root cause, traced into `@mastra/core`: `processChatMessage` (`chunk-WNAR4CR3.js:12180`) only backfills thread history when `!chatThread.isSubscribed()` (`:12196-12198`), but calls `chatThread.subscribe()` unconditionally on *every* processed message (`:12316`) regardless of which handler ran. Gorkie's own `respondOnThreadMessages` flag (`chat/handlers.ts`) is a completely separate, gorkie-owned concept that only gets set for root mentions, so a one-off mid-thread mention leaves Mastra's *own* subscription flag `true` from that first exchange onward, silently skipping backfill on every mention after the first, even when gorkie isn't actually following the thread in between. Fixed in `chat/handlers.ts`'s `onSubscribedMessage`: when responding to a mention we're not actively following (`respondOnThreadMessages` not set), call the (public) `thread.unsubscribe()` right before handing off to `defaultHandler`, forcing Mastra to see an unsubscribed thread and re-fetch history for that turn; Mastra re-subscribes on its own immediately after. `onMention` doesn't need the same fix, per `chat`'s own docs it's only ever called for mentions in threads Mastra already considers unsubscribed, so backfill already fires correctly there.
- **[Recently completed]** Migrated `@mastra/core` off the alpha channel to stable `1.50.0` (2026-07-07). was pinned to `1.50.0-alpha.2` solely to get ahead of unreleased fixes our token-estimation patch needed. Checked the actual diff between the newest alpha (`1.50.1-alpha.0`) and stable `1.50.0` first, it's one unrelated commit (provider/model registry data refresh), so there was no reason to stay on the alpha treadmill that had already broken our patch three times. Also bumped `@mastra/observability` (`1.16.0-alpha.0` → `1.16.0` stable), `@mastra/pg` (`1.15.0` → `1.15.1`), `@mastra/e2b` (`0.5.0` → `0.5.1`), and the `mastra` CLI devDependency (`1.18.0` → `1.18.1`). Regenerated the token-estimation patch against the new chunk files (`chunk-JGDMZZAO.js`/`chunk-EVJSSG7F.cjs`, replacing `chunk-WNAR4CR3.js`/`chunk-EMMWR65D.cjs`) using the same manual tarball-diff process as before (`bun patch`'s own diffing is broken in this sandbox, `.gitattributes` symlink resolution error), updated `scripts/verify-patches.ts` to check the new filenames, and added the new chunk hash substrings to `.cspell.jsonc`. **Found and migrated a real breaking change along the way**: `mastra.heartbeats` (the whole `Heartbeat`/`Heartbeats` API from `@mastra/core/agent`) was renamed to `mastra.schedules` (`AgentSchedule`/`Schedules` from the new `@mastra/core/schedules` subpath) somewhere between our old alpha and this stable release, same field shapes (`agentId`, `cron`, `prompt`, `threadId`, `resourceId`, `tagName`, `ifActive`, `ifIdle`, `metadata`, etc., confirmed via the new `.d.ts`), but `list()`/`pause()`/`resume()` now return the wider `AnySchedule` union (agent or workflow schedules) instead of a narrow agent-only type, so `tools/scheduled-tasks/{queries,list}.ts` needed type-guard narrowing (`item.agentId !== undefined`) to filter to agent schedules before treating them as `AgentSchedule`. All six `scheduled-tasks/*.ts` files updated (`create`, `list`, `pause`, `resume`, `delete`, `queries`, `utils`); no backward-compat shim exists for `mastra.heartbeats` (fully removed). Verified with a clean `bun install` (patch reapplies from scratch) plus `typecheck`/`check`/`check:spelling`, all pass.
- **[Recently completed]** Migrated observability from Langfuse to Mastra Platform (2026-07-07). swapped `LangfuseExporter` for `MastraPlatformExporter` in `src/mastra/index.ts`, replaced `LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY`/`LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY`/`LANGFUSE_BASEURL` with `MASTRA_PLATFORM_ACCESS_TOKEN`/`MASTRA_PROJECT_ID` in `env.ts`/`.env.example`, removed the now-unused `@mastra/langfuse` dependency, and updated `README.md`/`DESIGN.md`/`AGENTS.md` accordingly. The local `MastraStorageExporter` → DuckDB path is unrelated and was deliberately kept (see the disk-space item below), it's not a Langfuse-vs-Platform choice, it's local in-process query access (the cost/tok-s completion footer, ad-hoc SQL debugging) vs. a hosted dashboard, and both exporters run side by side.
- **[Priority queue]** `observability.duckdb` disk-space risk, resolved by dropping local observability storage entirely. Host disk was at 94% usage with the DuckDB file alone at ~12 GB and no retention: every trace and span write accumulated forever, and `@mastra/duckdb` does not implement the storage-retention/`prune()` feature that `@mastra/core@1.50.0` added (only Postgres, libSQL and MongoDB adapters do). Migrating to `PostgresStoreVNext` was considered and rejected: `MastraPlatformExporter` already ships every trace to Mastra Platform, so local storage only served the completion footer's cost line and ad-hoc SQL debugging. Final shape: a single plain `PostgresStore` for all storage, no domain split, no `MastraCompositeStore`, and `Observability` configured with `MastraPlatformExporter` alone. `@mastra/duckdb` dependency removed. Accepted tradeoff: the completion footer lost its `$0.0023 USD` line, which came from a local `getMetricAggregate` query. Token counts and tok/s are unaffected, they come from `usage`. The 12 GB file was left on disk deliberately, orphaned, pending a manual look.
- **[Priority queue]** `post_message` channel restriction, fixed with a same-channel-only policy. Live-observed that gorkie could relay a message into a restricted announcement-only channel where members can read but not post. Root cause: `assertCanPostTo` only checked membership, not posting rights, and Slack's real "who can post" API (`admin.conversations.getConversationPrefs`) is Enterprise-Grid-only and needs a user token gorkie does not have. Chose the most restrictive option: `post_message` and `upload_file` can only target the channel the conversation is already in. The membership check and its `conversations.members` call are gone, so `assertCanPostTo` is no longer async. Not live-tested.
- **[Priority queue]** DM-relay approval gap, fixed with a self-only policy. The read side was already blocked, everything routes through `assertReadableChannel`, and `search_slack` is scoped to the requester's own action token. The write side had none: `assertCanPostTo` returned early for any user target, so DMing a third party on the requester's behalf had zero checks. A user target must now be the requesting user themselves. Not live-tested.
- **[Features]** Allowlist and onboarding ported from the reference bot. New optional `OPT_IN_CHANNEL` env var, a no-op when unset. `lib/allowed-users.ts` caches through `chat().getState()` and stays live via a second `onMemberJoinedChannel` listener, which coexists with the existing greeting handler because the handler list is an array. `chat/onboarding.ts` offers an ephemeral opt-in card and grants access on accept. Wired into `onMention`/`onDirectMessage`, with `onSubscribedMessage` blocking silently rather than re-offering. Not live-tested.

## 2026-07-05

- **[Recently completed]** Fixed a real full-process crash from a dropped Slack Socket Mode connection (2026-07-05). live-observed in production logs, a WebSocket pong timeout ("A pong wasn't received from the server before the timeout of 5000ms!") dropped the connection, and the next queued event's `ack()` call then threw `Failed to send a WebSocket message as the client is not ready` from inside `@chat-adapter/slack`'s `startSocketMode()` event handler (`node_modules/@chat-adapter/slack/dist/index.js`, the `this.socketClient.on('slack_event', async (...) => {...})` callback), which has no try/catch around it. Since the callback is `async` and nothing awaits/handles its rejection, this became an unhandled promise rejection, fatal by default in Node, killing the entire bot process over one dropped socket message, not just that one event. Added a process-level safety net in `src/mastra/index.ts` (`process.on('unhandledRejection', ...)` / `process.on('uncaughtException', ...)`, logging instead of crashing) as a broad fix that also covers any other unhandled rejection in the dependency graph, not just this specific one. A more surgical fix (patching `@chat-adapter/slack` itself to wrap the handler in try/catch, same pattern as the existing `@mastra/core` patches) is still open if this class of error turns out to be frequent enough to warrant it.
- **[Recently completed]** Found and fixed a second, compounding observability bug. `src/mastra/index.ts`'s DuckDB path was a bare relative string (`'./observability.duckdb'`). Confirmed directly in `node_modules/mastra/dist/index.js` that `mastra dev`'s actual server process runs with `cwd` set to its own dev public dir, and `mastra start` spawns `node index.mjs` with `cwd: .mastra/output`, neither is the repo root, so the same relative path silently resolved to different files per mode, and it happened to land inside `src/mastra/public/` (a directory `mastra build` treats as static assets to copy, which is also the root cause of the previously-tracked 9.1 GB build disk-space blocker, now also fixed as a side effect). Fixed by anchoring to `process.env.MASTRA_PROJECT_ROOT`, which both CLI paths already inject as the true repo root for exactly this class of problem. Moved the existing 12 GB file (same filesystem, instant `mv`) to the new stable path so history isn't orphaned. Confirmed via direct SQL that a specific historical "frozen" thread has zero rows in this DB, its session was writing to a different, since-regenerated `.mastra/`-internal file that no longer exists on disk; that specific historical trace is unrecoverable, but the underlying bug (and the reason it was unrecoverable) is now fixed going forward.
- **[Recently completed]** Added an `AGENTS.md` rule: never start, restart, or kill `mastra dev`/`mastra start` on your own initiative, this is a live Slack bot the user runs themselves, and two instances racing for the same Slack Socket Mode connection causes real, confusing failures (confirmed one such live overlap this session, unrelated to the above).
- **[Recently completed]** Batch of fixes (2026-07-04, later): restored `scripts/verify-mastra-patch.ts` and the `postinstall` hook in `package.json` (both had gone missing, a real regression, since this is the safety net that catches `@mastra/core` silently installing unpatched; confirmed working again via a clean `bun install`). Fixed the completion-footer bug where `skip` suppressed the footer even if the model had already written a real reply earlier in the same turn (`turns.ts` now gates on `result.text.trim().length > 0`, the actual accumulated reply text, instead of "was `skip` called anywhere this turn"). Elevated repository transfer, collaborator changes, secret changes, and user-data deletion from "confirm first" to an absolute refusal tier in `guardrailsPrompt`, no phrasing or claimed authority unlocks them, including claiming to be gorkie's own owners; genuine owners are told to do these directly rather than through gorkie. Added a repo-ownership/no-LICENSE-file rule to the `gh-cli` skill for repos gorkie creates (owned by gorkie, @twa, @Devarsh). Added emoji download to `get_file` (shortcode like `:partyparrot:` → resolved via `emoji.list`, following alias chains; needs the `emoji:read` scope added to the app manifest and a reinstall to actually work, not currently granted). Reverted `create_scheduled_task`'s min-interval check from `croner` back to Mastra's own `validateCron`/`computeNextFireAt` (the dependency had crept back in from an interrupted parallel session) and removed `croner` from `package.json` again. Restarted the live server on `mastra dev` after finding it had drifted onto a stale prebuilt `.mastra/output` bundle from before all of today's changes.
- **[Recently completed]** Updated the file/image token-count patch (2026-07-05) from a flat guess (765 tokens for any image, 258 for any other file, regardless of actual size) to a byte-size-scaled estimate: reads `part.url`'s base64 payload length (real field per the AI SDK's `FileUIPart` type, confirmed via `node_modules/ai/dist/index.d.ts`, not guessed), converts to decoded byte size, divides by 750, clamps to `[200, 4000]`, and only falls back to the old flat constants when no `url` data is present at all. Not billing-accurate (every real provider tokenizes images differently, OpenAI tile-based, Anthropic `⌈w/28⌉×⌈h/28⌉`, both verified live against current docs, and this has no access to real pixel dimensions without decoding), but proportional rather than flat, so a tiny icon no longer costs the same budget as a 20MB photo. Verified both patched dist chunks still parse (`esbuild.transformSync`) and the estimate scales/clamps sensibly against synthetic base64 payloads of varying size. Candidate for the upstream Mastra PR discussed below (byte-size default, with an escape hatch for callers who want to supply their own per-part estimator).
- **[Recently completed]** Pushed current git changes (2026-07-05). committed and pushed the current working-tree changes to `origin/main`.
- **[Recently completed]** Killed running bot processes (2026-07-05). stopped the active `bun run dev`, `mastra dev`, and `.mastra/output/index.mjs` processes for this checkout.
- **[Recently completed]** Cleaned up tool display code (2026-07-05). split `tool-display` into `index.ts`, `agents.ts`, and `format.ts`, keeping the main dispatch small and moving delegate display plus formatting into focused modules.
- **[Recently completed]** Fixed missing tool input rendering (2026-07-05). tool display now falls back to Mastra's `argsSummary` when structured args are absent or empty, so inputs still render for those tool events.
- **[Recently completed]** Fixed skip-only footer bug (2026-07-05). `skip` no longer counts as a visible tool call for the completion footer, so a turn that only calls `skip` stays silent.
- **[Recently completed]** `chat` package version drift between top-level and `@mastra/core`'s bundled copy, fixed via `overrides` (2026-07-05). `@mastra/core` depends on `chat@^4.29.0` as a regular (non-peer) dependency, while gorkie's own `package.json` pins `chat@^4.32.0` at the top level. Since it's not a peer dependency, bun correctly resolved two separate copies, but they'd diverged enough (a new private field, a new `setMentionFlags` method) that TypeScript treated gorkie's own `Thread`/`Message` types as incompatible with what `@mastra/core`'s `ChannelHandler` expects, breaking `bun run typecheck`. Fixed by adding `"overrides": { "chat": "^4.32.0" }` to `package.json`, forcing a single deduped `chat` copy across the whole tree (confirmed: `node_modules/@mastra/core/node_modules/chat` no longer exists after reinstall). This is also suspected to be why DM markdown rendering, previously broken, now works correctly after this fix, not confirmed root-caused, just observed immediately after. Worth re-checking after any future `@mastra/core` bump whether its own `chat` dependency range has moved to `^4.32.0` or later, at which point this override can likely be dropped.
- **[Recently completed]** Fixed concurrent delegate tool display (2026-07-05). delegated child tool-call IDs now carry their parent delegate `toolCallId`, so inner tool updates target the correct parent card even when two same-type agents run concurrently.
- **[Research]** Mastra build disk-space blocker, fixed as a side effect. Root cause was `src/mastra/index.ts` passing DuckDB a bare relative `./observability.duckdb`. `mastra dev` runs with cwd set to its own dev public dir and `mastra start` spawns `node index.mjs` with cwd `.mastra/output`, so the same relative path resolved to two different files depending on mode, and one landed inside `src/mastra/public/`, which `mastra build` copies as static assets. Fixed by anchoring to `process.env.MASTRA_PROJECT_ROOT`, which both CLI paths inject for exactly this class of problem.

## 2026-07-04

- **[Recently completed]** Added a dedicated `guardrailsPrompt` system section (2026-07-04) covering destructive repository and production actions, harmful outbound messages, risky installs and tunnels like sshx or Termux-style remote shell workflows, and mandatory visible progress updates for sandbox, GitHub, browser, and deployment work. The `agent-browser` skill now also requires screenshots, narration, and confirmation before risky browser actions.
- **[Recently completed]** Reversed the same-day subagent removal: restored `agents/research.ts`, `agents/explore.ts`, `processors/delegates.ts`, the `agents: { research, explore }` block on `gorkieAgent`, `delegates` in `outputProcessors`, the `agent-*` display-name regex in `chat/tool-display.ts`, and the `agent-research`/`agent-explore` tools-prompt section, all restored verbatim from the last commit. Both helper agents now also get `fetch_url` in their `activeTools` (added alongside `search_web`), so research doesn't need the full workspace just to read a link, and explore doesn't need a redundant fetch path duplicated outside its read-only scope. The `Delegate long-run failure state` and `Subagent stability` P0s are back too, since the underlying bugs were never fixed, only sidestepped by removing the feature.
- **[Recently completed]** Deduplicated the Exa client into `lib/exa.ts` (was instantiated separately in `search-web.ts` and `fetch-url.ts`); both tools now import the shared `exa` singleton.
- **[Recently completed]** Reverted the sandbox-proxy experiment entirely (2026-07-04, same day it was verified working): removed `SANDBOX_PROXY_URL` from `env.ts` and `.env`, reverted `config.sandbox.template` to `gorkie-workspace:1.2`, and restored `build-template.ts` to a single plain build (no `proxiedTemplate`/`waitForPort` code, no SOCKS relay binary). The already-built `gorkie-workspace-proxied:1.2` E2B template alias is now unreferenced by any code; delete it from the E2B dashboard if desired. Existing paused sandboxes created while the proxied template was default are untouched (real per-thread state) and will keep working on that image until naturally replaced.
- **[Recently completed]** Fixed the completion footer posting to fake Studio thread ids and throwing `ValidationError`: `processOutputResult` (`processors/turns.ts`) now checks `channelContext().platform === 'slack'` (populated for real Slack turns via Mastra's own `buildEventContext`, matches our Studio preset's `platform: 'studio'`) before attempting to post, so non-Slack contexts skip it entirely instead of erroring.
- **[Recently completed]** Confirmed live in Slack: the `read_file` tool-result image relocation fix works end to end, the model correctly describes actual image content (not a filename-based guess) via `read_file` on both the `opencode-go` and `openrouter` routes. The corresponding E2E testing item is closed.
- **[Recently completed]** Batch of UX/safety fixes: `post_message` catches Slack `channel_not_found`/`not_in_channel` and returns actionable "Gorkie must be invited (`/invite @gorkie`)" errors (private channels surface as `channel_not_found` to non-members; un-joined public channels give `not_in_channel` unless the bot has `chat:write.public`); the tools prompt got a strong "consider delegating BEFORE multi-step research/exploration" section (gorkie kept forgetting helper agents exist) plus channel-membership and min-interval notes; the agent-browser skill now tells the agent to treat the user as a collaborator (short step narration, screenshots of key steps via `upload_file`, view its own website screenshots with `read_file` before declaring success, prefer session recordings for multi-step flows); `generate_image` filenames now include a per-call batch suffix so repeat calls stop overwriting earlier images; AGENTS.md documents the DuckDB observability database as the debugging ground truth.
- **[Recently completed]** Closed the `post_message`/`upload_file` write-side access gap (flagged live by a beta tester: "can a bad actor do this?"): both tools previously only required *Gorkie* to be a member of the target channel, not the requesting user, so anyone who could DM/mention Gorkie could have it relay a message into any channel Gorkie belongs to. Fixed via `assertCanPostTo` (`tools/slack/utils.ts`): for any target other than the current thread, checks `conversations.members` for the target channel and refuses if the requesting user isn't in it. Deliberately does not cover `target.type === 'user'` (DMing a third party on the requester's behalf) since "channel membership" doesn't apply to a 1:1 DM, that's a related but distinct risk still needing its own design, see the private-read-approvals item above.
- **[Recently completed]** Enforced the scheduled-task minimum interval in code, not just the prompt: `create_scheduled_task` now uses `croner` to sample the next 5 fire times and rejects if any gap is under 30 minutes (`scheduledTasks.minInterval` in `config.ts`). Sampling multiple fires (not just the first gap) matters for irregular crons like `0,5 * * * *`, where checking only the next occurrence could see a long gap while a short one lurks later in the cycle, making the same cron string pass or fail depending on the clock.
- **[Recently completed]** Simplified `scripts/postbuild.ts` to run `bun install` itself (via `Bun.spawnSync`) instead of shelling out with `cd .mastra/output && bun install`, so the `postbuild` package.json script is just `bun run scripts/postbuild.ts`.
- **[Recently completed]** Confirmed `scripts/verify-mastra-patch.ts` (the `postinstall` safety check) actually catches a real failure mode: `bun install` with a `patchedDependencies` key that doesn't match the installed `@mastra/core` version succeeds with zero warning and silently installs the *unpatched* package. Verified experimentally that the check fails loudly in that scenario and passes cleanly otherwise.
- **[Recently completed]** Verified via the running dev server's observability API (DuckDB is writer-locked while `mastra dev` runs, so queried `http://localhost:4111/api/observability/traces` instead): across the 9 most recent agent runs, max `inputTokens` was ~48k of the 200k budget with zero error spans, no token overload after re-enabling `read_file` media.
- **[Recently completed]** Implemented the `read_file` image fix: `relocate-tool-result-images` `CompatRule` in `processors/tool-media.ts` (relocates `image/*` `media` parts from tool results into a synthetic user message with a `file` part, opencode-inspired), registered via `ProviderHistoryCompat` on `gorkie` and `explore`, re-enabled `mediaTypes` on `read_file`, and updated the `read_file` prompt note (images visible, PDFs not). Statically verified for both the bundled v2 OpenRouter and vendored v2 OpenAI-compatible (opencode-go) converters; awaiting live Slack E2E.
- **[Recently completed]** Bumped `@mastra/core` to `1.50.0-alpha.1` (and `@mastra/observability` to `1.16.0-alpha.0` for its new `flush` interface requirement) and shrunk the patch to `patches/@mastra+core@1.50.0-alpha.1.patch` containing only the token-limiter fix. Upstream [PR#18396](https://github.com/mastra-ai/mastra/pull/18396) (merged 2026-06-25, in alpha.1) made the local `applyStoredModelOutputsPatch`/`normalizeModelOutputPatch` half redundant. Also corrected the loop-path finding: the outgoing prompt is built by `llmPrompt()` (which had the substitution since alpha.0), not `model()`; the remaining `read_file`-image blocker is the bundled spec-v2 OpenRouter model's `getToolResultContent` stringifying all non-text tool output, so the planned `ProviderHistoryCompat` relocation fix is still required. Updated `scripts/verify-mastra-patch.ts` to the new chunk names (`chunk-B3SHPOO6.js`/`chunk-P6SA5MPH.cjs`); `bun install` + postinstall verify + `bun run typecheck` all pass.
- **[Recently completed]** Fixed `bun run build` silently shipping an unpatched `@mastra/core`: `mastra build` generates its own `.mastra/output/package.json` and runs a fresh `bun install` inside `.mastra/output`, which ignored the root `patchedDependencies`/`patches/` entirely, so the deployed bundle had neither the token-limiter fix nor the `toModelOutput` multimodal fix even though local dev (using the root `node_modules`) had both. Added `scripts/postbuild.ts` plus a `postbuild` script (bun auto-runs it after `build`, same as npm) that copies `patches/` into `.mastra/output` and merges `patchedDependencies` into its generated `package.json`, then re-runs `bun install` there so the patch actually applies. Verified both fixes are present in `.mastra/output/node_modules/@mastra/core` after a full `bun run build`.
- **[Recently completed]** Added `generate_image`: generates AI images (`google/gemini-3.1-flash-image` via the Hack Club OpenRouter-compatible proxy) and always writes them into the sandbox. Delivery is left to `upload_file` (already has full thread/channel/user target support) rather than duplicating destination logic in `generate_image` itself. Model gateway config lives in `providers.ts` as `imageGenerator`, a single config (no cross-gateway fallback, deliberately kept simple; add fallback later if the single gateway proves unreliable).
- **[Recently completed]** Confirmed the "sent on behalf" attribution finding (above) applies equally to `upload_file`: it uses the identical `withAttribution(..., userId, isCurrentThread)` call with the same `channelContext().userId`.
- **[Recently completed]** Confirmed "sent on behalf" attribution (`withAttribution` in `chat/attribution.ts`) already credits whoever sent the *current* triggering message, not the thread starter: Mastra's own `channelContext().userId` is rebuilt per inbound message from `buildEventContext({ actor: message.author })` (see `chunk-6NSMFZFB.js`'s `processChatMessage`/`buildEventContext`), so it's already "who asked the agent to send this," not the resourceId/thread creator. No code change needed; flag a concrete repro if this is ever observed wrong in Slack.
- **[Recently completed]** Standardized Slack ids: removed `normalizeTarget()` (the flexible raw-or-prefixed input shim in `chat/target.ts`) since the `chat` package's own canonical id scheme is already `slack:C...`/`slack:C...:ts` everywhere (confirmed in `chat`'s source docs). `post_message`/`upload_file`'s `targetSchema`, and the `channelId` params on `get_channel_info`/`list_threads`/`read_conversation_history`, now explicitly require the standardized form in their descriptions, plus a shared note in the tools prompt telling the model to reuse ids exactly as seen in other tool outputs rather than reformatting them.
- **[Recently completed]** Fixed sandbox git identity: `build-template.ts`'s image-build `git config --global user.email` and `network.ts`'s `GIT_AUTHOR_EMAIL`/`GIT_COMMITTER_EMAIL` now use `gorkie@agentmail.to` (Gorkie's actual GitHub/AgentMail address) instead of the placeholder `gorkie-agent@users.noreply.github.com`.
- **[Recently completed]** Updated the `read_file` tool note (now that media works) and the `agent-browser` skill stub to say screenshots/images can be viewed with `read_file`, not just downloaded.
- **[Recently completed]** Re-enabled `read_file` sandbox media (removed `mediaTypes: false` in `workspace/index.ts`) after patching the root cause: Mastra's `createTool`/`toModelOutput` multimodal bug ([mastra-ai/mastra#18400](https://github.com/mastra-ai/mastra/issues/18400), fix in open PR [#18418](https://github.com/mastra-ai/mastra/pull/18418), not yet merged/released). Root cause: `MessageList.all.aiV5.model()`, the path the non-durable agent loop uses, never substituted a tool's stored `toModelOutput` (`providerMetadata.mastra.modelOutput`) into the tool-result part sent to the model, so `read_file`'s image/media output was replaced by the model with the raw tool return value (which just describes the file, not the actual image bytes). Ported the PR's `applyStoredModelOutputs` fix by hand into the installed dist chunks (`chunk-TQ2PVBZA.js` / `chunk-VUSAEB2W.cjs`) as `applyStoredModelOutputsPatch`, wired into both `model()` and `prompt()`, added to the same `patches/@mastra+core@1.50.0-alpha.0.patch`. Verified the transform logic in isolation with a simulated `read_file` image result. Should be dropped once the upstream PR ships in a release.
- **[Recently completed]** Bumped `@mastra/core` to `1.50.0-alpha.0` and patched (via `bun patch`, see `patches/@mastra+core@1.50.0-alpha.0.patch`) a real upstream bug in `TokenLimiterProcessor.countInputMessageTokens`: it counted image/file message parts by `JSON.stringify`-ing the entire part, including the base64 payload, as literal text tokens. A single image attachment could look like 100k+ tokens, so `trimMode: 'contiguous'` evicted the whole conversation (including the message with the image) to fit the budget, producing "No user or assistant messages in the request". The patch counts a small fixed token cost per file/image part instead of the base64 data. Image attachments are intentionally left in `message.attachments` (not stripped) so they still reach the model. Still open: models/routes with no vision endpoint (see P0 above).
- **[Recently completed]** Normalized raw Slack upload/post targets so `C...` channel ids become `slack:C...`, raw thread ids become `slack:C...:ts`, and user ids stay suitable for `openDM`.
- **[Recently completed]** Kept Slack read tools conservative after prompt-injection concerns: current conversation and public workspace channels only until private-read approvals are designed.
- **[Recently completed]** Added Slack timezone fields to `get_user` and updated scheduled-task prompting to infer the requester's IANA timezone from their profile for time-of-day schedules.
- **[Recently completed]** Confirmed the AgentMail skill already treats `gorkie@agentmail.to` as Gorkie's official/default inbox.
- **[Recently completed]** Configured sandbox Git commit identity: new E2B templates write `gorkie-agent` name/email to global Git config, sandbox env includes `GIT_AUTHOR_*` and `GIT_COMMITTER_*`, and the GitHub contribution skill repairs identity in existing sandboxes before committing.
- **[Recently completed]** **Root-caused and fixed the real multi-minute/indefinite tool-call hangs (2026-07-04)**, live-reproduced via direct SQL against the observability DuckDB against an in-progress run: Mastra's built-in `grep` workspace tool is a pure-JS recursive filesystem walk (one E2B `readdir`/`stat`/`readFile` network round-trip per file and directory, fully sequential, no concurrency, no timeout, no file-count cap on the walk itself, only the 1000-match *output* cap). On a large tree (e.g. a freshly `git clone`d Rails app), three parallel `grep` calls in one step measured live at 4m20s, 6+ minutes, and 6+ minutes (still open when checked). This is almost certainly also what explains the earlier "gorkie forgets a tool call for 30 minutes then resumes" reports. Fixed by disabling the built-in tool (`enabled: false` on `WORKSPACE_TOOLS.FILESYSTEM.GREP` in `workspace/index.ts`) and replacing it with `tools/grep.ts`, a custom tool with the same id/name and near-identical input schema that shells out to real `rg` inside the sandbox via `execute_command`'s underlying `sandbox.e2b.commands.run`, with a 30s internal timeout and an output-size cap. `ripgrep` was already installed in the sandbox template. Updated the tools prompt's `grep` note accordingly (no longer says "avoid it, use execute_command instead", it now *is* execute_command-backed).

## 2026-07-01

- **[Recently completed]** `get_file` rebuilt for large files: streamed downloads (no host buffering, streams into an E2B `.part` file), resumable via HTTP `Range` with a clean restart fallback, then numbered chunk files (`.part.00000`) for known-size Slack files. Validated on a 568,059,725-byte zip with forced aborts at 30s, 60s, 10s and 5s; `.part` advanced across resumes and `unzip -t` passed.
- **[Recently completed]** Lazy Slack attachment downloads. Attachments are no longer copied into the sandbox automatically; the model sees metadata and calls `get_file` only when it needs bytes.
- **[Recently completed]** Slack search compact output: 254,519 bytes to 18,859 on a synthetic 10-result search, a 93% reduction, preserving author, user, channel, timestamp, permalink, snippets and cursor. Added a `TokenLimiterProcessor` input guard with contiguous trimming.
- **[Recently completed]** Token limiter research. The processor runs per step, counts tool-result JSON, removes whole old messages, and can TripWire if nothing fits. It is a hard guard, not a semantic compressor. Observational memory recalls `lastMessages` before later per-step pruning and is separate from live prompt compression.
- **[Recently completed]** E2B lifecycle fix: use Mastra's `ensureRunning()` rather than direct `start()` for the filesystem, `get_file` and `upload_file`; removed the local `sandboxStarts` workaround after confirming upstream prefers lifecycle wrappers.

## 2026-06-28

- **[Recently completed]** The channels architecture flip-flop, recorded in order because it reads as nonsense otherwise. First, migrated off Mastra `AgentChannels` to own the Chat SDK directly, after hitting the ceiling: grouped output needed a `resolveToolDisplay` monkey-patch, there was no access to the Chat instance, and inline tool status was impossible. Then flipped back to `AgentChannels`, having found that handler overrides (`onMention`/`onSubscribedMessage`/`onDirectMessage` calling `defaultHandler`) let us own subscription policy while reusing all of `processChatMessage`'s stable internals: message build, multi-user attributes, history fetch, `sendMessage` and native steering. Deleted the owned pipeline and the `getBot`/`setBot` ref. Traded away reasoning-as-task and per-tool inline status. In between, the upstream channels source was copied into a review folder with commit provenance to produce a real size and diff artifact showing what owning it would cost.
- **[Recently completed]** Slack Postgres channel state via `@chat-adapter/state-pg`, so subscriptions and `thread.state` persist. Replaced a temporary root-message fallback for following top-level mentions after a restart.
- **[Recently completed]** Disabled the Slack adapter's incoming inline mention resolution, so `<@U...>` stays as raw ids in model-visible input; later moved into the custom adapter.
- **[Recently completed]** Provider and logging cleanup: inference provider fallback across all model chains, shared chains named `orchestrator`/`summarizer`/`memory` in `providers.ts`, redacted huge model request logs and provider response headers from failure output.
- **[Recently completed]** Sandbox config centralised into `src/mastra/config.ts`, processors renamed (`sandbox-lifecycle.ts` to `sandbox.ts`, `turn-log.ts` to `turns.ts`), logger moved to `lib/logger.ts`, sandbox path joining extracted to a helper.
- **[Recently completed]** `mermaid` tool renders via `mermaid.ink` (deflate plus pako URL, no new dependency) and uploads the PNG. Attachment downloads deduped so the same upload is not fetched twice. Skill tool cards hidden. `skip` made terminal. `LocalSkillSource` anchored on `process.cwd()` so skills load the same under `mastra dev` and `mastra start`. Attachments seeded at absolute paths so commands keep working after the model `cd`s away.

## 2026-06-27

- **[Recently completed]** Sandbox reset between tool calls, fixed. `sandboxCacheKey` returned null because `MASTRA_THREAD_ID_KEY` is not set at resolve time, so the workspace fell back to a per-RequestContext WeakMap and spun up a new E2B sandbox on every call, losing files and installs. Keyed on `channel.threadId` instead, which channels reliably sets: one persistent sandbox per thread.
- **[Recently completed]** Sandbox lifecycle for cost: bump the timeout only on steps that call a sandbox tool, and pause at turn end so idle compute is not billed. Research note: `timeout` is the sandbox max lifetime set at creation with `onTimeout: 'pause'`, not a per-command timeout and not refreshed on activity, so a command running past it risks the sandbox pausing underneath.
- **[Recently completed]** Tool batches shipped: core Slack read (`read_conversation_history`, `get_user`, `get_channel_info`, `list_threads`), files (`get_file`, `upload_file`), act (`post_message`, `schedule_reminder`, `leave_thread`), and `search_web` via Exa. `get_weather` removed.
- **[Prompt caching]** Kept the system-prompt prefix static so identity blocks are byte-identical across threads and cache globally: no timestamp and no speaker in the system prompt (both cache-killers, the speaker rides in the message body), and sandbox guidance moved to `E2BSandbox.instructions`.
- **[Open questions]** Decisions taken, two later reversed: sandbox `timeout` set to 90s (now 8 minutes), keep `MastraStorageExporter` (reversed 2026-07-07 when local observability was dropped), and `maxSteps` raised to 150 (now 1000).

## Undated

- **[Simplification]** Delete the `instructions.replaceAll('execute_typescript', 'slack')` in `prompts/features/code-mode.ts`. It is dead as of the 1.61.0 bump: Mastra used to build code-mode instructions from a static `USAGE_CONTRACT` with the tool name hardcoded, and now parameterizes it as `createUsageContract(config.id ?? 'execute_typescript')` (mastra-ai/mastra#21920). We already pass `id: 'slack'`, so the literal can no longer appear and the replace can never match.
- **[Simplification]** Replace the Slack read budget (`lib/slack-budget.ts`, added in c4ba59c) with a fix for the amplification it meters. `parseSlackMessage` awaits `lookupUser(event.user)` per message and a page is parsed under `Promise.all`, and `lookupUser` has no in-flight dedup, so its cache write lands only after every concurrent read has already missed: a 200-message page from 5 authors fires ~200 `users.info` calls, not 5. Override `lookupUser` in `SlackAgentAdapter` (it is `protected`, and we already override `resolveInlineMentions`) with a per-id in-flight promise map plus the negative caching 676e586 only applied to mention resolution. Then the budget can be raised a long way, and should degrade softly rather than throwing: in code mode a thrown tool error surfaces as an exception inside the model's own program and loses the whole turn's work, and `read_conversation_history` currently throws *after* the fetch, so the API cost is paid and the data discarded.
- **[Simplification]** Deslop the prose across the repo (unslop + writing guidelines): README, `docs/github-app.md`, `.env.example`, App Home copy (`chat/app-home/*`, `chat/content.ts`), and the `github` skill. Fix passive voice, bold-for-emphasis, three-dot ellipses, and copy that names buttons that do not exist.
- **[Simplification]** Clear the code smells the branch review raised: the duplicated `/user` fetch and login schema across `lib/github/api.ts` and `lib/github/token.ts`, the `network()` and `checkoutPolicy`/`pushPolicy` wrappers that only forwarded, the two parallel permission ladders and evaluators behind `GitHubPermission`/`ToolPermission`, and `repoDir`/`remoteUrl` re-splitting a raw `owner/repo` string. One `ApprovalLevel` ladder in `types/approval.ts` now backs both features, with each keeping the subset it offers.
- **[Simplification]** Make the `GITHUB_WRITE_TOOLS` claim below true rather than aspirational. `tools/github/approval.ts` hand-maintained a 33-entry map of `read`/`write` values that reproduced the SDK's own write list exactly (verified: 0 mismatches), so the classification was duplication while only the tool selection was load-bearing. The file is gone, its one consumer now holds an `EXPOSED` name list and derives each kind from `GITHUB_WRITE_TOOLS`. Note the residual risk: if the SDK ever drops a name from that list while keeping the tool, the kind silently downgrades to `read` and it stops asking. Adding is safe, removal is not.
- **[Simplification]** Stop `processors/sandbox.ts` guessing from a list of tool names. It hardcoded `slack`, `get_slack_file`, `upload_file`, `github_checkout` and `github_push_branch` while each tool declared the same id itself, which is how the 8 minute timeout bug below happened. An intermediate fix centralised the ids in `tools/tool-names.ts`; that is gone again, because Mastra has a better hook. `processToolResult` fires after a tool succeeds, so the processor now asks whether the sandbox was actually reached (a `WeakSet` on `RequestContext`, set in `getSandbox`) instead of predicting it from a name. Every custom sandbox tool and the filesystem resolver route through `getSandbox`, so only `execute_command`, `get_process_output` and `kill_process` still match by name, and those come from `workspaceToolNames`, which is derived from our own rename config rather than duplicated. The pause in `processOutputResult` is gated the same way, so a turn that never touched the sandbox no longer resolves one just to pause it. One caveat: `processToolResult` does not fire when a tool throws, so a failed tool no longer extends the timeout the way the pre-execution check did.
- **[Simplification]** Collapse the seven identical `getSandbox` plus `if (!sandbox) throw new Error('No sandbox available.')` guards into `requireSandbox`. Well past the inline-over-extract threshold at seven copies.
- **[Simplification]** Fold `ensureRunning()` into `requireSandbox`. Turn end pauses the sandbox but leaves it cached under `sandboxCacheKey`, so anything resolved on a later turn is paused, and `retryOnDead` cannot recover that: a paused sandbox throws `SandboxNotReadyError`, which matches no branch of `isSandboxDeadError`. Four call sites called it by hand, two got it through `withCredential`, and `upload-emoji.ts` reached for `retryOnDead` without it, which is the shape the open item below describes. One helper now covers all seven. **Correction, 2026-09-11:** this entry was wrong against the tree. `requireSandbox` never called `ensureRunning()` and seven call sites still did it by hand, so either the change was reverted or the entry was written for a version that never landed. Actually folded in on 2026-09-11: `workspace/index.ts` now resumes inside `requireSandbox`, and the five call sites that already went through it, plus `withCredential` and `upload-emoji`, dropped their manual calls. `filesystem.ts` keeps its own, since `init()` is the filesystem lifecycle rather than a tool resolving a sandbox.
- **[Simplification]** Unexport `githubApi`. It was only ever called by `githubUser` and `countInstallations` in its own file, but `lib/github/index.ts` re-exported it app-wide through `export *`.
- **[Simplification]** Make `isComment` look only at the opening line. It scanned every line, so a real request with a `##` aside on the end was swallowed whole and never triggered a turn. Moved from `chat/handlers.ts` to `chat/message.ts` next to `rawText` and `withoutLeadingMentions`, since the prefill and the history tool both need it now. Verified against 10 cases including `<@U123> ## x` (comment), a leading-blank-line comment, `# single hash` (not a comment), and `real request\n## aside` (not a comment).
- **[Customization]** Add focus mode: while a turn holds a per-user credential, other people's messages are refused rather than queued. Built as `lib/focus.ts` with a `config.focus.maxDuration` ceiling so a crashed turn cannot lock a thread, a gate in `runTurn` that posts an ephemeral and returns without calling `defaultHandler`, `chat/focus.ts` for the badge, and a `releaseFocus` output processor. No Mastra patch needed, since handlers own dispatch so a refused message never enters `pendingSignalsByThread`. **Withdrawn 2026-08-25** by `37b7098`, which removed focus mode along with GitHub-in-threads. Recorded because the reasoning survives nowhere else: queuing was the wrong call because leftover queued signals replay as a fresh run at completion under the previous run's `streamOptions`, so a stale steer would replay carrying the credentialed run's request context.
- **[Customization]** Show which commit gorkie is running on, as a footer at the bottom of App Home. Read once at startup from git, linked to the commit when `origin` is a GitHub remote, and dropped entirely when there is no checkout to read.
- **[Known issues]** Evict the `sandboxCacheKey` entry when the turn ends. `_sandboxKeyCache` is a plain `Map` that only `clearSandboxCache` and `destroy` empty, so every thread left a `Promise<E2BSandbox>` for the life of the process. `processOutputResult` now clears the thread's entry after pausing. It inherits the known hole: that hook does not run on a thrown or aborted turn, so those paths still leak an entry.
- **[Known issues]** `annotationCoverage` counted every listing, not every tool. `mcp/user-servers/tools.ts:29-37` does `counts.total += 1` per tool on every `userMCPTools` call and never resets, so the map is a running total across turns rather than the size of the current tool set. `app-home/mcp/views.ts:24` renders it straight to the person as "its N tools", so a five tool server reads as fifty after ten turns. It is also keyed by server name alone (`mcp/user-servers/approval.ts:4`), so two people who both add a server called `notion` share one entry. Recompute per listing and key it by user and server.
- **[Known issues]** `mcpServerNames` was a process-wide `Set` that is never cleared (`mcp/user-servers/client.ts:6`, filled at `tools.ts:17`, read at `chat/status/index.ts:56`). Every user's server names accumulate in it forever, so one person's server name can label another person's typing status, and it grows for the life of the process. It exists only to strip a `<server>_` prefix off a tool name; the caller already knows which servers belong to the current user.
- **[Known issues]** `probe.ts` and `client.ts` each built the same `MCPClient` server config by hand (url, `allowedHosts`, and the `requestInit` bearer header). Two copies of the shape that has to match, in the two places that talk to user servers.
- **[Known issues]** `resolveClient` keyed its client cache on `JSON.stringify(servers)` (`mcp/user-servers/client.ts:91`), which puts decrypted bearer tokens into a map key and makes the cache depend on property order. Hash the fields that matter instead.
- **[Known issues]** `SlackId` (`lib/ids.ts`), `Block` (`chat/app-home/limit.ts`), `GitHubAccess` (`lib/github/access.ts`) and `GitHubSettings` (`db/queries/settings.ts`) are exported but imported nowhere. Either move them to `types/` if they are shared, or drop the `export`.
- **[Known issues]** Force thread history on every ping, not just the first. Mastra's backfill runs only when `!chatThread.isSubscribed()` (`agent-DSxJoGjY.js:21230`), so anything said between two pings never reached the model: gorkie answered "what do you mean by 'it'?" to a request whose subject sat in the two `##` comments above it. `chat/history.ts` replaces it, walking `thread.messages` back to `lastSeenMessage` in thread state and injecting up to 10 unseen messages per turn, skipping DMs and gorkie's own posts. `threadContext.maxMessages` is now `0` so only one path injects, and the `thread.unsubscribe()` workaround in `onSubscribedMessage` is gone. `##` comments were originally included as context even though they never trigger a turn; see the item below that reversed this.
- **[Known issues]** Split `app-home/presets.ts` into `github/presets.ts` and `mcp/presets.ts`. The shared module had grown a `nothingDeletes` flag whose only job was forking the wording between the two consumers, so each feature now owns its own labels and its own `decodePreset` (GitHub returns a bare permission, MCP keeps the server-name scope prefix).
- **[Known issues]** `github_push_branch` asked for approval before validating the branch, so a push to main burned a click and only then refused. `validateBranch` now runs in the input schema, so the model gets a validation error with no card at all. That also stops the model spending two approvals in one turn on a single push, which is the shape that tripped the resume bug above.
- **[Known issues]** `sandboxTools` in `processors/sandbox.ts` did not list `github_checkout` or `github_push_branch`, so a turn that only touched GitHub never extended the 8 minute sandbox timeout the way every other tool turn does. Both added.
- **[Known issues]** Decide the commit identity for sandbox work. Commits are now authored by `gorkie-agent <gorkie@agentmail.to>`, gorkie's own AgentMail address, rather than the previous `slack-agent@users.noreply.github.com`, which matched no account anywhere. The push and the pull request stay attributed to the connected person. Consider a `Co-authored-by` trailer naming the requester so the split is visible in the commit itself.
- **[Known issues]** Decide how the device flow handles someone authorising without installing the app on any repository: App Home now calls `GET /user/installations` after signing in and shows an install link when it comes back empty. Confirmed against a real app that the two steps are genuinely separate, and that a person who only signs in can still search public repositories, so it looks connected while every write fails. The web flow would join the two steps but needs a publicly reachable callback, which would mean a second deployment holding `DATABASE_URL` and `CREDENTIALS_KEY`; rejected on that basis.
- **[Known issues]** Check for cross-account leakage on a mid-run message. Answered from source, no live test needed: `@mastra/core/dist/agent-controller-sWTmXOK3.js:3671` calls `sendSignal` without `streamOptions` when a run is already active, so the interjected message's `RequestContext` is dropped and the run keeps the waking message's `channel.userId`. Tools therefore always resolve the run owner, never the interjector, and resolving inside `execute` changes nothing. The approval card is the working identity gate: `requesterId` is stashed per `toolCallId` (`agent-DSxJoGjY.js:21410`) and a click from anyone else is refused (`:20848`). Any tool acting with a per-user credential in a shared thread must require approval unconditionally.
- **[Known issues]** Decide whether to move GitHub from a pasted PAT to the OAuth device flow. Settled: the device flow is the default, and since the swap to native tools its token really is installation-scoped, so it buys tighter scopes and not only UX. An optional classic `public_repo` token now sits alongside it for the one thing an app structurally cannot do, fork or open a pull request against a repository somebody else owns. Tokens carrying `repo` are refused.
- **[Known issues]** Add an approval preset per connection in App Home, one for GitHub and one for each MCP server: ask for everything, ask before writing or deleting (default), or ask only before deleting. Replaces the fixed `readOnlyHint` rule, which made creating a branch as interruptive as merging a pull request. Per server rather than per person because `requireToolApproval` is already per-server config, and a read-only docs server does not deserve the same caution as GitHub.
- **[Known issues]** Harden the PR #13 Slack search user-token fallback (public-channel pin, scope verification, live-message gate, identity-pinned cursors, `searchedAs` provenance). Pushed to PR #13 as `8cf2273`; still needs a live Slack run to confirm the scope check and the expiry-to-fallback path.
- **[Known issues]** Move off the `1.60.1-alpha.0` prerelease pin onto `1.61.0` stable (`1.60.1` never shipped). Patch redirected; all 10 hunks still needed, none fixed upstream.
- **[Ideas]** Replace the hosted GitHub MCP server with native tools from `@github-tools/sdk` and add brokered git push. Full plan in [docs/brokered-git.md](./docs/brokered-git.md). GitHub no longer goes through `MCPClient`: `tools/github/index.ts` builds a curated 33-tool surface out of the SDK's 79 (merge, repo admin, gists, releases, label and comment deletion, and CI mutation deliberately excluded, following eve's judgement), named `github_*` snake_case so the prompt and skill did not need renaming. Forking is the exception to the exclusions: `github_fork_repository` is exposed, but only for a classic token, since an app structurally cannot fork a repository it is not installed on. The token is a `() => Promise<string>` provider that the SDK invokes inside every `execute`, so it is resolved per call rather than frozen into a client at run assembly the way `mcp/user-servers.ts:103` still does for user servers. Approval is carried per tool via `needsApproval`, which Mastra reads off Vercel tools, and `GITHUB_WRITE_TOOLS` replaces the `readOnlyHint` sniffing, which also retires the `^(delete|remove)_` caveat below. `github_push_branch` brokers the credential at the sandbox firewall for one git command and requires approval unconditionally.
- **[Ideas]** Reconsider the `delete` preset for GitHub. It gated nothing at all, not the two tools the earlier note claimed, because every destructive tool was excluded from the surface. Renamed to "Never ask" for GitHub only; MCP servers keep "Ask only before deleting", where the `delete_`/`remove_` prefix check does gate something.
